**Corrections from implementing Task 2** (what the plan's code blocks got
wrong, and what the landed code does instead):

1. **`invalidInputProblems`, `missingDocument`, `pathProblems` and
   `asValidation` did not ship.** All four are unexported and no code in
   this task calls them, and `unused` is enabled in `.golangci.yml`, so
   `make check` refuses them. `asValidation`'s own doc comment explains
   why the plan did not notice: it says the helper is "used by this
   package's own tests", but this package's tests are `package
   markdown_test`, which cannot reach an unexported identifier at all.
   Whichever task first needs them adds them with its own caller — Task
   3 for `pathProblems` and `invalidInputProblems`, Task 3 or 6 for
   `missingDocument`. **Their definitions are in this task's own Step 4
   and Step 5 code blocks** — `invalidInputProblems`, `missingDocument`
   and `asValidation` in Step 4 ("Write the errors file"),
   `pathProblems` in Step 5 ("Write the path grammar"). Copy them from
   there rather than writing them again: the point of naming them here is
   that four helpers Tasks 3 to 6 all call must have one definition, and
   two implementers rewriting them from scratch is exactly the drift
   `errors.go`'s own package comment takes the metamodel dependency to
   avoid.
2. **The plan's `SplitContent` could not pass the plan's own
   `TestFrontmatterThatIsNotAMappingIsRefused`.** `yaml.Unmarshal` into
   a `map[string]any` refuses a sequence with "cannot unmarshal !!seq
   into map[string]interface {}", which the plan's single branch
   reports as "not valid YAML" — a message that does not contain "a set
   of key/value pairs", and that sends a caller hunting a syntax error
   that is not there. `parseFrontmatter` now decodes a second time into
   `any` **only on failure**, purely to tell "does not parse" from
   "parses into something that is not a mapping".
3. **The plan's argument for the `json.Marshal` failure branch names an
   unreachable cause, and the reachable one is the standing lesson.**
   The comment blamed "a non-string mapping key, most often": measured,
   `yaml.Unmarshal` folds `1: one` into the string key `"1"` when the
   target is `map[string]any`, so that branch is never taken for a
   scalar key — `TestANonStringScalarKeyIsFoldedToAStringRatherThanRefused`
   pins the folding. What does reach it is `.nan`, `.inf` and `-.inf`,
   which decode to float64 NaN and ±Inf and which `json.Marshal`
   refuses; `TestFrontmatterHoldingAValueJSONCannotCarryIsRefused`
   covers all three. The comment now says so.
4. **`splitFence` checked the "closes on the first line" case last, and
   with a bound that never applied to it.** The plan's order searched
   `"\n---\n"` first, which cannot match `"---\n---\n"` (there is no
   newline before the closing fence), so the first-line case only ever
   worked by falling through. Reordered, with a comment saying why.
5. **The plan's `TestAnOversizedFrontmatterBlockIsInvalidInputAtPathContent`
   passed for the wrong reason and was rewritten.** Its block was
   `strings.Repeat("k: v\n", …)`, and yaml refuses the duplicate key —
   so with the byte bound deleted the test still went green (verified:
   nine of ten mutations in Step 13 went red, this one did not). It now
   builds distinct keys and asserts the bound's own wording, and a
   companion test pins that a block just under the bound is accepted.
6. **The plan's `TestOnlyALineThatIsExactlyThreeHyphensClosesTheBlock`
   as first written could not pass either.** `"---\ntitle: X\n----\n---\nbody\n"`
   makes the block `"title: X\n----\n"`, which is not valid YAML. The
   landed test proves the same rule without a parse: with `"----"` as
   the only candidate there is no closing fence at all, so the whole
   input is body and the title falls back to the path.
7. **Bounds, stated against the database's index bound.** `MaxBodyBytes`
   (1 MiB = 1048576 **bytes**) is **looser than** `0007_documents.sql`'s
   `left(body_md, 131072)` (131072 **characters**) — eight times it for
   ASCII prose and about 2.7 times for CJK, since the two are not the
   same unit — so for a body over 131072 characters the
   database's `left()` is the binding constraint and the document is
   indexed by its head alone, silently — Task 1's correction 2 recorded
   it and this task's `MaxBodyBytes` comment repeats it rather than
   claiming a backstop. `MaxTitleLen` (200 runes) and `MaxSummaryLen`
   (500 runes) are far **below** `left(title, 131072)` and
   `left(summary, 131072)`, so those two are always indexed whole.
   `MaxPathLen` (200 bytes) relates to nothing in the index: the path is
   not part of the generated vector.
8. **The path is the one input here with no separate UTF-8 check, and
   that is now stated and pinned.** The ASCII-only segment pattern
   subsumes it — an invalid byte decodes as U+FFFD, which is outside the
   class — so `CheckPath` refuses `"lore/dusk\x80wood"` as a bad
   segment. Added as a fourteenth case of
   `TestEveryBadPathIsInvalidInputAtPathPath` and proved red by adding
   U+FFFD to the character class, because the rule everywhere else in
   this codebase is to check the encoding first and explicitly.
9. **`MissingError` and `ConflictError` are declared here and first used
   by Task 10, so `errors_test.go` reads both back now.** The sentinel
   each satisfies, `MissingError.Fields`, and `ConflictError.Details`
   with and without `Include` are asserted through the exported surface
   — the same surface `internal/web` will use — so neither is a
   write-only shape waiting nine review rounds to be noticed. Both
   mutations (wrong sentinel, `Details` ignoring `Include`) were proved
   red.
10. **Doc comments that would have overclaimed were cut back.** Every
    "internal/web publishes this" sentence in `errors.go` now says it is
    Task 10's work and names the test that pins what is true today, and
    `CheckPath`'s bound comment states what the bound is *not* related
    to rather than implying a backstop.

11. **`splitFence` resolved its closing fence by list order, and
    deleted body text for it.** The four line-ending candidates were
    tried in the order they were written and the first that matched
    *anywhere* won, rather than the one that occurred *earliest*. A CRLF
    frontmatter block above a body containing an LF `---` therefore
    matched the LF candidate at a further offset: the block ran past the
    real fence, `yaml.Unmarshal` kept only the first of the two YAML
    documents inside it, and the prose in between vanished.
    `"---\r\ntitle: X\r\n---\r\nintro\n---\ntrailing\n"` returned
    `body="trailing\n"` where `"intro\n---\ntrailing\n"` was written —
    contradicting `Content`'s own "byte for byte" promise with data loss
    attached, on the mixed line endings agent-assembled content produces
    routinely. Both fences now resolve by position: `closingFence` takes
    the minimum index across every spelling, `openingFence` takes the
    longest prefix, so neither depends on the order the spellings are
    listed in. **Note how it shipped: none of the four CRLF branches had
    a test, and statement coverage reported `splitFence` at 100% because
    they were iterations of a loop rather than statements.** A coverage
    number certified nothing here, and it is the second time in this
    sub-project that a number stood in for a case nobody had written.
12. **A closing fence at end of file was not treated as one.** Both
    candidates required a newline *after* `---`, so `"---\ntitle: X\n---"`
    was an unterminated fence: the YAML was stored as prose, the title
    fell back to the path and the summary became the literal `"---"`.
    Jekyll, Hugo and goldmark's own frontmatter extension all accept an
    EOF-terminated block, and a metadata-only document written without a
    trailing newline is an ordinary thing for an agent composing a JSON
    string. "An unterminated fence is body" is the right rule for a
    *missing* fence; a fence terminated by EOF is not missing.
    `closingFence` now considers the `"\n---"` suffix, and `splitFence`
    handles `"---\n---"` alongside the existing first-line case.
13. **A leading byte-order mark defeated frontmatter silently, and is now
    refused by name.** U+FEFF is category `Cf`, not `Cc`, so it passed
    the control-character scan; the opening `CutPrefix` then failed
    because the fence no longer sat at byte 0, and the whole document —
    mark included — became body, with the summary reading `U+FEFF` plus
    `---` and nothing said. Refused rather than stripped, for the reason
    `CheckText` gives about control characters: deleting part of what a
    caller wrote answers a question it did not ask, and here the deletion
    would be invisible in a value the caller reads back. Only a *leading*
    mark is refused — inside the body it is an ordinary zero-width
    character and prose is not ours to judge.
14. **The UTF-8-then-control scan is now shared, before Task 3 copied it
    a third time.** `SplitContent` reimplemented what
    `metamodel.textProblem` already did, with a different allowance and
    different wording, while `errors.go`'s package comment justifies the
    metamodel dependency precisely as avoiding "a second, drifting copy
    of each". `metamodel.CheckText(value, allowedControls)` is now the
    judgement — the ordering, the allowance as a parameter, and which
    rune at which byte, returned as a `TextFault` — and `textProblem` and
    `SplitContent` are both callers that keep their own error type, their
    own argument path and their own wording. **Task 3 bounds `kind` and
    `message` through `CheckText`, not through a third scan.** The
    allowance is what each caller states for itself: `""` for a
    single-line value, `"\n\t"` for the metamodel's free-form prose,
    `"\n\r\t"` for a markdown body.
15. **Four documented behaviours that no test held are now pinned**, each
    proved red by mutation: the `mapping == nil` guard (delete it and a
    blank frontmatter block marshals to JSON `null`, headed for a
    `NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'` column and breaking `Content`'s "never empty,
    never null" promise); the UTF-8-before-control **ordering**, pinned
    with an input carrying both problems, since neither existing test
    could tell the order; `stringValue`'s empty-after-trim rule; and the
    frontmatter boundary — a block of exactly `MaxFrontmatterBytes` is
    accepted, the way `MaxBodyBytes` already had its boundary test.
16. **The derivation was markdown-naive, and its output is rendered by
    ten later tasks.** A `# ` line inside a fenced code block became the
    title and the fence itself became the summary, so a document whose
    first example carried a sample heading was listed under a line out of
    someone else's snippet. `eachProseLine` now skips fenced code blocks
    (backticks or tildes, three or more, up to three spaces of
    indentation; an unclosed fence runs to end of document, CommonMark's
    own rule). `isATXHeading` replaces the `strings.HasPrefix(line, "#")`
    summary skip, which made `#1 rule of Duskwood` — not a heading, since
    a heading needs whitespace after its hashes — invisible. And a
    frontmatter `title` or `summary` holding a control character is now
    refused rather than stored: both are rendered as one line, the same
    reasoning `textProblem` gives for refusing a newline in a `name`,
    while the body below the fence keeps its line breaks.

    **Why a line scan and not goldmark's AST**, which was the open half
    of the finding: goldmark is a declared dependency and Task 11 parses
    every document with it for the reading view, so the fuller derivation
    is available — but this code runs on the **write** path for two short
    strings, and a full parse of a megabyte body would be paid on every
    write and every revert, where Task 11's parse is paid once on a read
    that is already parsing. Handing the derivation to a parser also
    changes far more than the two cases at hand: setext headings, lazy
    continuation lines, HTML blocks and link reference definitions would
    all start deciding titles, silently and differently from today, on
    documents already written. So the two concrete failures are fixed and
    the residue is recorded in `eachProseLine`'s doc comment: an indented
    four-space code block is not recognised, a fence's info string is not
    parsed, and a `#` heading inside a blockquote or list item still
    counts. If Task 11's parse ever moves to the write path, derive from
    its AST and delete the scan.
17. **Smaller things the same round found.** A Go type name reached a
    caller-facing message — an agent was told its frontmatter was "not a
    set of key/value pairs (`[]interface {}`)" — and it now says "a list"
    or "a single value". `TestMalformedFrontmatterIsTheCallersOwnProblem`
    asserted only that the message contained "frontmatter", a word both
    parse failures share, so it could not tell "not valid YAML" from "not
    a mapping" — the exact distinction correction 2 exists to preserve;
    a new test asserts each. `[]rune(content[i:])`, up to a mebibyte of
    allocation to read one rune, is gone with the scan it belonged to.
    `MaxFrontmatterBytes`'s comment now says what the bound is **not**:
    a billion-laughs expansion is refused by `gopkg.in/yaml.v3`'s own
    alias budget, in single-digit milliseconds, at every block size and
    every bomb shape (nested aliases, alias fan-out, merge-key chains) —
    the byte bound's real job is bounding parse work, and if the parser
    is ever swapped that defence leaves with it. `MaxBodyBytes`'s comment
    no longer promises a field-level refusal for every 1 MiB body: the
    body travels inside a JSON string and Go's encoder escapes `<`, `>`
    and `&` as six bytes each, so an angle-bracket-heavy body of about
    700 KiB exceeds `maxContentRequestBodyBytes` (4 MiB) and is refused
    by size before this bound is consulted. The hedge is in before Task 3
    lands the route.

Verification for this pass: `go test ./internal/markdown/ -v -count=1` —
45 tests, 0 skipped, `TEST_DATABASE_URL` exported and confirmed reaching
the process. Thirteen mutations proved red before the fixes were kept, in
both packages: the closing fence resolved by list order; the EOF fence
clauses removed; the BOM check removed; the `mapping == nil` guard
removed; `CheckText`'s two scans reordered; `stringValue`'s trim removed;
the frontmatter bound made exclusive; the code-fence skip disabled; the
ATX rule replaced by `HasPrefix("#")`; the frontmatter title/summary
check disabled; the CRLF opening spelling removed; `%T` restored in the
"not a mapping" message; and `textProblem`'s allowance hardcoded to `""`,
which reddens
`TestUpsertEntityRefusesUnprintableTextBeforeItReachesPostgres` and so
proves the metamodel still routes its own asymmetry through the shared
check. `make check` clean across the repository (`gofmt`, `go vet`,
`golangci-lint`, `sqlc diff`, `go test -race ./...`).

### Task 3: Write and read one document

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/db/queries/documents.sql`
- Create: `internal/markdown/service.go`
- Create: `internal/markdown/events.go`
- Create: `internal/markdown/documents.go`
- Create: `internal/markdown/documents_test.go`
- Create: `internal/markdown/testhelpers_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/db/dbq/*` (regenerated by sqlc, never hand-edited)

**Requirement, before any query in this task is written:** every
`:one`/`:many` statement in `documents.sql` takes the resolved project id
and filters on it, including the ones that address a row by its primary
key. A document id in a caller's hand is not authority to read it. The
file's header comment says which of those filters are load-bearing and
which are defence in depth, because a comment claiming isolation a
composite key is actually providing would be relied on as if it were the
mechanism.

**No query lands ahead of its caller.** This task writes exactly the six
statements it calls. Tasks 4, 5, 6, 8 and 9 add theirs.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the queries**

`internal/db/queries/documents.sql`:

```sql
-- Every statement in this file carries the resolved project id: the
-- reads and deletes as a filter, the writes as the column they set.
-- Isolation between games is enforced in SQL, not in Go, so a query
-- trusting a path or an id alone would hand a caller another game's
-- prose the moment one leaked.
--
-- Which filters are load-bearing, stated rather than implied:
--
--   * On documents, every one of them is. A path is caller-supplied and
--     names no parent, and a document id is a value a previous answer
--     handed back, so nothing but the project filter keeps either inside
--     one game.
--   * On document_versions and document_links, the project filter is
--     load-bearing *as well*, and this is the one place Maestro
--     deliberately does not follow the metamodel's reasoning. There, a
--     query reaching entities through an entity_type_id is covered by
--     the composite foreign key and the filter is defence in depth.
--     Here 0007_documents.sql gives both child tables the same composite
--     key, so the same argument would apply -- and the spec (§5) rejects
--     it for these two tables specifically: read_version and history are
--     the calls where "join to the parent, then check" is one forgotten
--     join away from serving another game's prose, and the invariant
--     must not depend on anyone remembering. So the version and link
--     queries filter on their own denormalised project_id and never join
--     documents to establish scope.
--
-- No write here sets updated_at. 0007_documents.sql puts a
-- set_updated_at trigger on documents, so the column has one mechanism
-- behind it rather than a trigger plus a clause every future query must
-- remember.

-- name: UpsertDocument :one
-- The compare-and-set, in one statement. Two writers cannot both read
-- current_version 3 and both succeed: the DO UPDATE is guarded by the
-- caller's expected version, so the loser matches no row and gets back
-- no row rather than an error, which Go turns into the typed conflict.
--
-- A creating caller passes expected_version 0, which no stored version
-- can equal -- current_version starts at 1 and only climbs -- so the
-- guard is a no-op on the insert path and a guaranteed mismatch when a
-- path the caller believed free turns out to be taken. That is the whole
-- mechanism behind `expected_version: 0` (spec §7), and it is why the
-- argument is required rather than inferred from absence.
--
-- The path column is deliberately not in the SET list: the first
-- spelling stored stands, so a re-seed under different casing addresses
-- the existing document rather than rewriting the handle other documents
-- and links refer to. That omission is also what lets Go refuse a
-- respelling *after* this statement runs -- the returned row still
-- carries the stored spelling -- which closes the creation race, where
-- there was nothing yet to lock.
--
-- created_by_* are not in the SET list either: the creator of a document
-- is a fact about its first version and does not change when someone
-- else edits it. updated_by_* do change, on every write.
--
-- deleted_at = NULL on the update arm is the resurrection rule (spec
-- §3): writing to a soft-deleted path brings the document back and
-- continues its version numbering, because the path is still taken and
-- the history is still there.
INSERT INTO documents (project_id, path, kind, title, summary, body_md, frontmatter,
                       current_version,
                       created_by_user_id, created_by_token_id,
                       updated_by_user_id, updated_by_token_id)
VALUES (sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid, sqlc.arg('path')::text, sqlc.arg('kind')::text,
        sqlc.arg('title')::text, sqlc.arg('summary')::text, sqlc.arg('body_md')::text,
        sqlc.arg('frontmatter')::jsonb, 1,
        sqlc.narg('actor_user_id')::uuid, sqlc.narg('actor_token_id')::uuid,
        sqlc.narg('actor_user_id')::uuid, sqlc.narg('actor_token_id')::uuid)
ON CONFLICT (project_id, lower(path)) DO UPDATE
SET kind                = excluded.kind,
    title               = excluded.title,
    summary             = excluded.summary,
    body_md             = excluded.body_md,
    frontmatter         = excluded.frontmatter,
    current_version     = documents.current_version + 1,
    deleted_at          = NULL,
    updated_by_user_id  = excluded.updated_by_user_id,
    updated_by_token_id = excluded.updated_by_token_id
WHERE documents.current_version = sqlc.arg('expected_version')::integer
RETURNING *;

-- name: GetDocumentByPath :one
-- The read path. A soft-deleted document is not found unless the caller
-- asked for it: deletion is soft so nothing is lost, not so that every
-- reader has to filter.
SELECT * FROM documents
WHERE project_id = sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid
  AND lower(path) = lower(sqlc.arg('path')::text)
  AND (sqlc.arg('include_deleted')::bool OR deleted_at IS NULL);

-- name: GetDocumentByPathForUpdate :one
-- FOR UPDATE, and deliberately with no deleted_at filter: a write to a
-- deleted path resurrects it, so the writer must see the row it is about
-- to bring back, and must hold it.
--
-- The lock does not buy the refusal -- the guarded DO UPDATE refuses on
-- its own. It buys *the number the caller is told to merge onto*: without
-- it the read runs against the transaction's snapshot, so a caller racing
-- an in-flight edit is told to merge onto a version that is already stale
-- by the time it retries, and retries into the same refusal forever.
-- TestTheReportedCurrentVersionIsTheOneTheWriteWouldHaveMet pins exactly
-- that.
SELECT * FROM documents
WHERE project_id = sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid
  AND lower(path) = lower(sqlc.arg('path')::text)
FOR UPDATE;

-- name: InsertDocumentVersion :one
-- The snapshot. Full bodies, never diffs (spec §3): a thousand versions
-- of a long script is a few megabytes, which is nothing beside a diff
-- chain that must be replayed to answer "show me version 12" and that
-- corrupts every later version if one link is wrong.
--
-- project_id is written from the caller's own resolved id, in the same
-- transaction as the document write, and 0007_documents.sql's composite
-- key is what keeps it honest: a version row whose project_id disagrees
-- with its document's cannot be inserted at all.
INSERT INTO document_versions (project_id, document_id, version, title, summary, body_md,
                               frontmatter, message, author_user_id, author_token_id)
VALUES (sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid, sqlc.arg('document_id')::uuid,
        sqlc.arg('version')::integer, sqlc.arg('title')::text, sqlc.arg('summary')::text,
        sqlc.arg('body_md')::text, sqlc.arg('frontmatter')::jsonb, sqlc.arg('message')::text,
        sqlc.narg('author_user_id')::uuid, sqlc.narg('author_token_id')::uuid)
RETURNING *;
```

```bash
make sqlc-check
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the test helpers**

`internal/markdown/testhelpers_test.go`:

```go
package markdown_test

import (
	"context"
	"testing"

	"github.com/google/uuid"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/markdown"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/metamodel"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/realtime"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/testutil"
)

// newService gives a test its own throwaway database, a markdown
// service and a metamodel service over the same pool. The hub is real,
// so the event tests have something to subscribe to; a test that does
// not care simply ignores it.
func newService(t *testing.T) (*markdown.Service, *metamodel.Service, *realtime.Hub, *pgxpool.Pool) {
	t.Helper()
	pool := testutil.NewPool(t)
	hub := realtime.NewHub()
	return markdown.New(pool, hub), metamodel.New(pool, hub), hub, pool
}

// newGame inserts a game with a plain INSERT through the pool the test
// already holds. There is deliberately no production "create a bare
// project for tests" API: it would ship in the binary and be callable
// over MCP.
func newGame(t *testing.T, pool *pgxpool.Pool, slug string) uuid.UUID {
	t.Helper()
	var id uuid.UUID
	if err := pool.QueryRow(context.Background(),
		`INSERT INTO projects (slug, name) VALUES ($1, $1) RETURNING id`, slug).Scan(&id); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("insert project %s: %v", slug, err)
	}
	return id
}

// requireFieldError asserts the exact path and the exact message of a
// refusal. Every negative test in this package goes through it: a test
// that asserts only err != nil is not a test of the thing it is named
// after, and six mutations once survived twenty-six such tests in the
// metamodel package.
func requireFieldError(t *testing.T, err error, wantPath, wantMessage string) {
	t.Helper()
	if err == nil {
		t.Fatalf("want a refusal at %q, got nil", wantPath)
	}
	var v *metamodel.ValidationError
	if !errorsAs(err, &v) {
		t.Fatalf("want a *metamodel.ValidationError, got %#v", err)
	}
	for _, f := range v.Fields {
		if f.Path == wantPath && stringsContains(f.Message, wantMessage) {
			return
		}
	}
	t.Fatalf("no problem at %q containing %q; got %v", wantPath, wantMessage, v.Fields)
}

func ptrInt32(v int32) *int32 { return &v }
```

Add `errorsAs` and `stringsContains` as one-line wrappers over
`errors.As` and `strings.Contains` at the bottom of the same file, with
the `errors` and `strings` imports — the same shape Task 1's schema test
uses, so a reader moving between them sees one idiom.

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the failing tests**

`internal/markdown/documents_test.go`:

```go
package markdown_test

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"strings"
	"sync"
	"testing"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/markdown"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/metamodel"
)

func TestWritingADocumentCreatesItAtVersionOneAndItReadsBackByte ForByte(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	content := "---\ntitle: Wanted: Hogger\nera: first\n---\n" +
		"# Ignored\n\nHOGGER: *snarls*\n\n  Indented line kept as written.\n"
	doc, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path:            "scripts/wanted-hogger",
		Content:         content,
		Kind:            "script",
		Message:         "first draft",
		ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Write: %v", err)
	}
	if doc.CurrentVersion != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("CurrentVersion = %d, want 1", doc.CurrentVersion)
	}

	// The read-back. This is the step the metamodel skipped for nine
	// review rounds, which is how an edge's field values shipped
	// write-only. Everything this write accepted must come back out.
	got, err := svc.Read(ctx, game, "scripts/wanted-hogger")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Read: %v", err)
	}
	wantBody := "# Ignored\n\nHOGGER: *snarls*\n\n  Indented line kept as written.\n"
	if got.BodyMd != wantBody {
		t.Fatalf("BodyMd = %q, want the body exactly as written (%q)", got.BodyMd, wantBody)
	}
	if got.Title != "Wanted: Hogger" {
		t.Fatalf("Title = %q, want the frontmatter title", got.Title)
	}
	if got.Kind != "script" {
		t.Fatalf("Kind = %q, want %q", got.Kind, "script")
	}
	var frontmatter map[string]any
	if err := json.Unmarshal(got.Frontmatter, &frontmatter); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("decode frontmatter: %v", err)
	}
	if frontmatter["era"] != "first" {
		t.Fatalf("frontmatter = %v, want it to echo era back untouched", frontmatter)
	}
}

func TestTheFirstWriteAlsoWritesVersionOneWithItsAuthorAndMessage(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "The world is called Azeroth.\n",
		Message: "the first note", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Write: %v", err)
	}

	var version int32
	var message, body string
	if err := pool.QueryRow(ctx,
		`SELECT v.version, v.message, v.body_md
		   FROM document_versions v JOIN documents d ON d.id = v.document_id
		  WHERE d.project_id = $1 AND d.path = 'bible'`, game).Scan(&version, &message, &body); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("read the version row: %v", err)
	}
	if version != 1 || message != "the first note" || body != "The world is called Azeroth.\n" {
		t.Fatalf("version row = (%d, %q, %q), want (1, %q, %q)",
			version, message, body, "the first note", "The world is called Azeroth.\n")
	}
}

func TestASecondWriteNeedsTheCurrentVersionAndAdvancesIt(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("first write: %v", err)
	}
	doc, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "two\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(1),
	})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("second write: %v", err)
	}
	if doc.CurrentVersion != 2 {
		t.Fatalf("CurrentVersion = %d, want 2", doc.CurrentVersion)
	}

	var count int
	if err := pool.QueryRow(ctx,
		`SELECT count(*) FROM document_versions v JOIN documents d ON d.id = v.document_id
		  WHERE d.project_id = $1 AND d.path = 'bible'`, game).Scan(&count); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("count versions: %v", err)
	}
	if count != 2 {
		t.Fatalf("%d version rows, want 2: every save is a snapshot", count)
	}
}

func TestAStaleVersionIsAConflictThatCarriesTheCurrentBody(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("first write: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "two\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(1),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("second write: %v", err)
	}

	_, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "three\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(1),
		IncludeCurrent: true,
	})
	if !errors.Is(err, markdown.ErrVersionConflict) {
		t.Fatalf("want version_conflict, got %v", err)
	}
	var conflict *markdown.ConflictError
	if !errors.As(err, &conflict) {
		t.Fatalf("want a *markdown.ConflictError, got %#v", err)
	}
	if conflict.Current != 2 {
		t.Fatalf("Current = %d, want 2", conflict.Current)
	}
	// The whole point of the shape: an agent merging prose needs the
	// text, and a second round trip for it costs a turn and a context
	// window.
	if conflict.BodyMD != "two\n" {
		t.Fatalf("BodyMD = %q, want the current body %q", conflict.BodyMD, "two\n")
	}
	if got := conflict.Details()["current_body"]; got != "two\n" {
		t.Fatalf("Details()[current_body] = %v, want the current body", got)
	}
}

func TestIncludeCurrentFalseWithholdsTheBodyAndKeepsTheVersion(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: strings.Repeat("a", 4096), ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("first write: %v", err)
	}
	_, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "small\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
		IncludeCurrent: false,
	})
	var conflict *markdown.ConflictError
	if !errors.As(err, &conflict) {
		t.Fatalf("want a *markdown.ConflictError, got %#v", err)
	}
	details := conflict.Details()
	if details["current_version"] != int32(1) {
		t.Fatalf("current_version = %v, want 1", details["current_version"])
	}
	if _, present := details["current_body"]; present {
		t.Fatal("include_current false must withhold the body")
	}
}

func TestAMissingExpectedVersionIsInvalidInputRatherThanAGuess(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	_, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{Path: "bible", Content: "one\n"})
	requireFieldError(t, err, "expected_version",
		"is required: pass 0 to create a document")
}

func TestExpectingAVersionOfADocumentThatDoesNotExistIsNotFound(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	_, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(3),
	})
	// not_found and not version_conflict: there is no version to merge
	// onto, so telling a caller to merge would send it round a loop that
	// cannot terminate.
	if !errors.Is(err, markdown.ErrNotFound) {
		t.Fatalf("want not_found, got %v", err)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(err.Error(), `"bible"`) {
		t.Fatalf("the message must name the path, got %q", err.Error())
	}
}

func TestARespelledPathIsRefusedNamingBothSpellings(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "lore/duskwood", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("first write: %v", err)
	}
	_, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "Lore/Duskwood", Content: "two\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(1),
	})
	requireFieldError(t, err, "path", `already exists here spelled "lore/duskwood"`)
}

func TestTwoConcurrentWritesLeaveOneWinnerAndNoGapInTheVersions(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("first write: %v", err)
	}

	var wg sync.WaitGroup
	results := make([]error, 2)
	for i := range results {
		wg.Add(1)
		go func(i int) {
			defer wg.Done()
			_, results[i] = svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
				Path: "bible", Content: "rewrite\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(1),
			})
		}(i)
	}
	wg.Wait()

	var won, conflicted int
	for _, err := range results {
		switch {
		case err == nil:
			won++
		case errors.Is(err, markdown.ErrVersionConflict):
			conflicted++
		default:
			t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
		}
	}
	if won != 1 || conflicted != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("got %d winners and %d conflicts, want exactly one of each", won, conflicted)
	}

	var current int32
	var versions int
	if err := pool.QueryRow(ctx,
		`SELECT d.current_version, (SELECT count(*) FROM document_versions v WHERE v.document_id = d.id)
		   FROM documents d WHERE d.project_id = $1 AND d.path = 'bible'`,
		game).Scan(&current, &versions); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("read the document: %v", err)
	}
	if current != 2 || versions != 2 {
		t.Fatalf("current_version = %d with %d version rows, want 2 and 2: "+
			"no gap and no duplicate in the sequence", current, versions)
	}
}

func TestReadingAnotherGamesDocumentIsNotFound(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	azeroth := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	outland := newGame(t, pool, "outland")

	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, azeroth, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "secret\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := svc.Read(ctx, outland, "bible"); !errors.Is(err, markdown.ErrNotFound) {
		t.Fatalf("a token for outland must not read azeroth's prose, got %v", err)
	}
}

func TestAWriteIsAnnouncedOnlyAfterItCommits(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, hub, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	sub := hub.Subscribe(game, "viewer", false)
	defer sub.Close()

	// A refused write announces nothing.
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(9),
	}); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("want a refusal")
	}
	select {
	case ev := <-sub.Events():
		t.Fatalf("a refused write announced %v", ev)
	default:
	}

	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}
	ev := <-sub.Events()
	if ev.Kind != "document.written" {
		t.Fatalf("Kind = %q, want %q", ev.Kind, "document.written")
	}
	payload, ok := ev.Payload.(markdown.DocumentEvent)
	if !ok {
		t.Fatalf("Payload = %#v, want a markdown.DocumentEvent", ev.Payload)
	}
	if payload.Path != "bible" || payload.Version != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("payload = %+v, want path bible at version 1", payload)
	}
}

func TestAKindAndAMessageAreBoundedAsTheCallersOwnArguments(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	_, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
		Kind: strings.Repeat("k", markdown.MaxKindLen+1),

	_, err = svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
		Message: strings.Repeat("m", markdown.MaxMessageLen+1),
	})
	requireFieldError(t, err, "message", "must be at most")

	_, err = svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
		Kind: "scr\npt",
	})
	requireFieldError(t, err, "kind", "control character")
}

func TestEveryProblemWithOneWriteIsReportedInOnePass(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	_, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "lore//duskwood", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
		Kind: strings.Repeat("k", markdown.MaxKindLen+1),
	})
	requireFieldError(t, err, "path", "has an empty segment")
	requireFieldError(t, err, "kind", "must be at most")

	var v *metamodel.ValidationError
	if !errors.As(err, &v) || len(v.Fields) != 2 {
		t.Fatalf("want both problems in one answer, got %#v", err)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the service scaffolding**

`internal/markdown/service.go`:

```go
package markdown

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"strings"

	"github.com/google/uuid"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgconn"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/db/dbq"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/metamodel"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/realtime"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/roles"
)

// Actor records who performed a write, for the audit columns. It is
// metamodel.Actor, not a second copy: the two domains record the same
// two kinds of actor into the same two shapes of column, and a token id
// belonging to another game is refused by the database in both
// (0007_documents.sql's composite keys), not here.
type Actor = metamodel.Actor

// Service is the markdown domain.
type Service struct {
	pool *pgxpool.Pool
	q    *dbq.Queries
	hub  *realtime.Hub
}

// New builds the service. The hub may be nil, in which case nothing is
// published.
func New(pool *pgxpool.Pool, hub *realtime.Hub) *Service {
	return &Service{pool: pool, q: dbq.New(pool), hub: hub}
}

// publish emits a change event, if a hub is attached.
//
// minRole and humanOnly are passed explicitly rather than inferred from
// kind, exactly as internal/metamodel's and internal/web's publish do,
// so each call site shows the gating it chose instead of inheriting one
// from a table three files away. The values are named constants declared
// beside the kind they belong to, in events.go, which is where the
// reasoning for each lives.
//
// **Every caller must call this after withTx has returned, never from
// inside fn.** An event published inside the transaction announces a
// change that may still roll back, and a subscriber that re-reads on
// hearing it would read the state before the change and cache it as the
// state after. TestAWriteIsAnnouncedOnlyAfterItCommits pins the refused
// case; Task 12 pins the rolled-back one.
func (s *Service) publish(projectID uuid.UUID, kind string, minRole roles.Role, humanOnly bool, payload any) {
	if s.hub == nil {
		return
	}
	s.hub.Publish(realtime.Event{
		ProjectID: projectID,
		Kind:      kind,
		MinRole:   string(minRole),
		HumanOnly: humanOnly,
		Payload:   payload,
	})
}

// withTx runs fn inside a transaction, rolling back unless it returns
// nil. Every mutation here needs one: a document row and the version row
// that records it are one change, and half of it landing would leave a
// document whose current_version names a snapshot that does not exist.
func (s *Service) withTx(ctx context.Context, fn func(*dbq.Queries) error) error {
	tx, err := s.pool.Begin(ctx)
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("begin: %w", err)
	}
	defer func() { _ = tx.Rollback(ctx) }()

	if err := fn(dbq.New(tx)); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	if err := tx.Commit(ctx); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("commit: %w", err)
	}
	return nil
}

// The bounds on the two short strings a caller may attach to a write.
//
// Exported for the rule the metamodel established for MaxSearchQuery: a
// bound a caller cannot read is a bound a caller trips over, and the
// tool descriptions are built with these values interpolated rather than
// typed out.
const (
	// MaxKindLen bounds `kind`, the free-text grouping label a project
	// puts on a document ("lore", "script", "pitch"). Maestro attaches
	// no meaning to it and ships no vocabulary — the same rule that
	// forbids a built-in Quest type forbids a built-in Lore kind.
	MaxKindLen = 64

	// MaxMessageLen bounds the "why this edit" line a version records.
	// It is a commit message, not a changelog entry: the change itself
	// is in the diff.
	MaxMessageLen = 500
)

// checkShortText is the one rule every single-line caller string in this
// package obeys, reported at the argument's own path.
//
// Newline and tab are refused here and allowed in a body, and the split
// is deliberate: a body is prose and its line breaks are the writing, a
// kind or a message is one line that will be rendered in a table cell
// and a listing. Refuse, never strip — deleting part of a caller's input
// answers a question it did not ask.
func checkShortText(path, value string, max int) []metamodel.FieldError {
	problem := func(message string) []metamodel.FieldError {
		return []metamodel.FieldError{{Path: path, Message: message}}
	}
	switch {
	case len(value) > max:
		return problem(fmt.Sprintf("must be at most %d bytes, and this one is %d", max, len(value)))
	case !utf8ValidString(value):
		return problem("is not valid UTF-8: a byte in it does not decode as any character, " +
			"and Postgres refuses that outright")
	}
	if i := strings.IndexFunc(value, isAnyControl); i >= 0 {
		return problem(fmt.Sprintf(
			"holds a control character (%U at byte %d): this is one line of text",
			[]rune(value[i:])[0], i))
	}
	return nil
}

// notFound maps pgx's no-rows sentinel onto a named miss, leaving every
// other error wrapped with what was being looked up.
func notFound(err error, missing error, doing string) error {
	if err == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) {
		return missing
	}
	return fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", doing, err)
}

// oversizeForIndex recognises a write refused because a value was too
// large for the generated search vector, and reports it as the caller's
// own input rather than as a server fault; every other error passes
// through unchanged.
//
// This path is reachable, not merely a backstop: left(title, 131072),
// left(summary, 131072) and left(body_md, 131072) in 0007_documents.sql
// bound the generated expression at 131072 characters, while
// MaxBodyBytes allows a body up to 1 MiB (1048576 bytes) -- eight times
// higher. A body between those two sizes writes successfully and is
// indexed by its first 131072 characters only; this handler exists for
// the rarer case, a single field (title, summary, or a body) so large
// to_tsvector itself refuses the row (SQLSTATE 54000), which is the
// shape metamodel.searchLimitExceeded takes for the same SQLSTATE, and
// it is here for the same reason: left unmapped, 54000 lands on the
// default arm as internal_error, which tells an agent to give up on a
// call it could have fixed by writing less. (Task 1's correction 1 and
// 2 record the measurement and the false claim this comment used to
// make about which of the two bounds actually binds.)
func oversizeForIndex(err error) error {
	var pgErr *pgconn.PgError
	if !errors.As(err, &pgErr) || pgErr.Code != "54000" {
		return err
	}
	return invalidInput("content", fmt.Sprintf(
		"is too large to index (%s): write less in one document", pgErr.Message))
}

// pathRespellingError is what a caller sees when its path matches an
// existing document's only case-insensitively.
//
// It is metamodel.keyRespellingError applied to a path, and the argument
// is the same one: silently updating the differently-spelled document
// would let a typo'd capital overwrite content, and letting the database
// raise it would surface as a version conflict — which says nothing
// about the actual problem — because the upsert carries an ON CONFLICT
// clause. Naming both spellings and both remedies is the whole answer,
// and a designer never has to know an index folds case.
func pathRespellingError(requested, stored string) error {
	return invalidInput("path", fmt.Sprintf(
		"%q already exists here spelled %q, and paths are matched without regard to case: "+
			"use %q to update it, or pick a path that differs by more than capitalisation",
		requested, stored, stored))
}
```

Add `utf8ValidString` and `isAnyControl` to the bottom of the file as
one-line wrappers over `utf8.ValidString` and `unicode.IsControl` with
their imports, so `frontmatter.go`'s `isForbiddenControl` and this
file's stricter rule sit under two obviously different names and cannot
be confused for one another.

- [ ] **Step 7: State this task's event gating**

`internal/markdown/events.go`:

```go
package markdown

import "github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/roles"

// Event kinds this package publishes, with the role and human-only
// gating each one carries.
//
// They live here, together, for the reason internal/metamodel/events.go
// and internal/web/publish.go both give: the gating of an event is a
// decision about who may learn a fact, and a decision made four call
// sites apart is a decision re-made four times.
//
// Two rules every payload here obeys. A payload carries the *identity*
// of what changed — a path, a version number, an id — and never a value
// a client could then treat as current: publication order is not commit
// order, so a payload holding a body could stably tell a client the
// wrong body with nothing to signal it, and prose is the largest payload
// in the system besides. And publication happens after the transaction
// commits, never inside it (Service.publish's own doc comment).
const (
	// eventDocumentWritten fires from Write once its transaction has
	// committed. Tasks 4, 5 and 6 add the other three kinds and state
	// their own gating here rather than copying this one.
	//
	// **MinRole is empty — every member of the game, viewer included.**
	// Reading documents is not role-gated anywhere: a viewer's browser
	// renders a quest's script, so gating the invalidation above viewer
	// would leave exactly the reader who cannot re-fetch on demand
	// looking at prose that silently drifts. The precedent is
	// internal/metamodel's entity events, which land on the same value
	// for the same reason, and internal/web's eventGameDeleted, which
	// argues the shape from the other side.
	//
	// **HumanOnly is false**, and it is the considered answer rather
	// than the default. internal/web's member, token and invite events
	// all set it true, because each mirrors a REST listing that
	// requireHumanCaller refuses a token caller outright — a token
	// subscriber learning about membership churn would be reading, over
	// the stream, a fact it could not have asked for over REST. A
	// document is the opposite case in every respect: Task 10 mounts
	// docs.read and docs.write on MCP *for agents specifically*, so an
	// agent that may read every document on demand loses nothing by
	// being told one changed. It is also the subscriber with the most to
	// lose from not being told — the spec's own definition of done is
	// two agents rewriting one script, and the one that is not told its
	// base moved learns about it as a version_conflict it cannot
	// explain.
	eventDocumentWritten = "document.written"
)

// documentEventMinRole and documentEventHumanOnly are the gating decided
// above, named so the call sites read as the decision rather than as two
// bare literals a later edit could drift apart. Tasks 4, 5 and 6 reuse
// these two constants — the four kinds are one decision about one kind
// of fact, unlike the metamodel's four pairs, which cover two genuinely
// different facts (a game's vocabulary and its content).
const (
	documentEventMinRole   = roles.Role("")
	documentEventHumanOnly = false
)

// DocumentEvent is the payload of every document event. It is a typed
// struct, never hand-built JSON with caller values interpolated into it,
// and it carries identity only.
//
// Path is the *stored* spelling, not the caller's: paths match without
// regard to case, so repeating the caller's spelling would name an
// identity no other reader sees.
type DocumentEvent struct {
	ID      uuid.UUID `json:"id"`
	Path    string    `json:"path"`
	Version int32     `json:"version"`
}
```

```go
package markdown

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"

	"github.com/google/uuid"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/db/dbq"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/metamodel"
)

// WriteInput is one document write.
//
// **ExpectedVersion is required and 0 spells a create** (spec §7). It is
// a pointer so that "absent" is distinguishable from "zero", and absent
// is invalid_input at its own path rather than a guess: the metamodel
// reads an absent version as "I am creating", and inheriting that here
// would throw away the whole value of 0 — a typo'd path would silently
// become a second document and the caller would be told it succeeded.
//
// IncludeCurrent asks for the current body back on a conflict. It
// defaults to false in Go, and the *tool* defaults it to true (Task 10):
// the default belongs on the wire, where an agent that does not know
// about the argument gets the useful behaviour, and the Go zero value
// stays honest.
//
// Task 6 adds a Links field to this struct. It is deliberately not here
// yet: a field no caller sets is a shape pre-committed sight unseen.
type WriteInput struct {
	Path            string
	Content         string
	Kind            string
	Message         string
	ExpectedVersion *int32
	IncludeCurrent  bool
	Actor           Actor
}

// Write creates or updates one document and records the snapshot.
//
// The whole compare-and-set happens in SQL: UpsertDocument's DO UPDATE
// is guarded by the caller's expected version, so two writers cannot
// both read version 1 and both succeed. The FOR UPDATE read before it
// does not buy that refusal; it buys the *number* the loser is told to
// merge onto, which is what makes "retry" advice that works.
func (s *Service) Write(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, in WriteInput) (dbq.Document, error) {
	// Every problem in one pass: a caller whose path and whose kind are
	// both wrong hears about both, rather than fixing one, calling again
	// and learning about the other.
	problems := pathProblems(in.Path)
	problems = append(problems, checkShortText("kind", in.Kind, MaxKindLen)...)
	problems = append(problems, checkShortText("message", in.Message, MaxMessageLen)...)
	if in.ExpectedVersion == nil {
		problems = append(problems, metamodel.FieldError{
			Path: "expected_version",
			Message: "is required: pass 0 to create a document, or the version you read " +
				"to update the one that is there",
		})
	}
	if len(problems) > 0 {
		return dbq.Document{}, invalidInputProblems(problems)
	}

	content, err := SplitContent(in.Path, in.Content)
	if err != nil {
		return dbq.Document{}, err
	}

	var written dbq.Document
	err = s.withTx(ctx, func(q *dbq.Queries) error {
		var err error
		written, err = s.writeWith(ctx, q, projectID, in, content)
		return err
	})
	if err != nil {
		return dbq.Document{}, err
	}
	s.publish(projectID, eventDocumentWritten, documentEventMinRole, documentEventHumanOnly,
		DocumentEvent{ID: written.ID, Path: written.Path, Version: written.CurrentVersion})
	return written, nil
}

// writeWith does the work against any queries handle, so Task 5's revert
// shares one implementation with this one. Every caller runs it inside a
// transaction, and none of them publishes from in here.
func (s *Service) writeWith(ctx context.Context, q *dbq.Queries, projectID uuid.UUID,
	in WriteInput, content Content,
) (dbq.Document, error) {
	expected := *in.ExpectedVersion

	// Read under the row lock, so the spelling and the version this
	// caller is told about are the ones its own write will meet. No
	// deleted_at filter: a write to a deleted path resurrects it.
	existing, err := q.GetDocumentByPathForUpdate(ctx, dbq.GetDocumentByPathForUpdateParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, Path: in.Path,
	})
	switch {
	case err == nil:
		// Spelling before version: a caller failing for both reasons
		// hears the one it can act on.
		if existing.Path != in.Path {
			return dbq.Document{}, pathRespellingError(in.Path, existing.Path)
		}
		if expected != existing.CurrentVersion {
			return dbq.Document{}, conflictOn(existing, in.IncludeCurrent)
		}
	case errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows):
		// Creation. A caller that expected a version of a document that
		// does not exist gets not_found and not version_conflict: there
		// is nothing to merge onto, so "merge and retry" would send it
		// round a loop that cannot terminate.
		if expected != 0 {
			return dbq.Document{}, missingDocument(in.Path)
		}
	default:
		return dbq.Document{}, fmt.Errorf("lock document: %w", err)
	}

	row, err := q.UpsertDocument(ctx, dbq.UpsertDocumentParams{
		ProjectID:       projectID,
		Path:            in.Path,
		Kind:            in.Kind,
		Title:           content.Title,
		Summary:         content.Summary,
		BodyMd:          content.Body,
		Frontmatter:     content.FrontmatterJSON,
		ExpectedVersion: expected,
		ActorUserID:     in.Actor.UserID,
		ActorTokenID:    in.Actor.TokenID,
	})
	if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) {
		// The guarded DO UPDATE matched nothing: between the locked read
		// above and this statement another writer created or advanced
		// the row. Only reachable on the creation path, where there was
		// nothing to lock.
		return dbq.Document{}, s.conflictAfterFailedUpsert(ctx, q, projectID, in)
	}
	if err != nil {
		if mapped := oversizeForIndex(err); errors.Is(mapped, ErrInvalidInput) {
			return dbq.Document{}, mapped
		}
		return dbq.Document{}, fmt.Errorf("upsert document: %w", err)
	}
	// The locked read cannot be the only place the spelling is checked:
	// on the creation path there is nothing to lock, so a writer racing
	// a creator passes both the read and the guard. path is not in the
	// SET list, so the row this statement touched still carries the
	// stored spelling, and comparing it here closes the race — the
	// caller's transaction rolls the write back.
	if row.Path != in.Path {
		return dbq.Document{}, pathRespellingError(in.Path, row.Path)
	}

	if _, err := q.InsertDocumentVersion(ctx, dbq.InsertDocumentVersionParams{
		ProjectID:     projectID,
		DocumentID:    row.ID,
		Version:       row.CurrentVersion,
		Title:         row.Title,
		Summary:       row.Summary,
		BodyMd:        row.BodyMd,
		Frontmatter:   row.Frontmatter,
		Message:       in.Message,
		AuthorUserID:  in.Actor.UserID,
		AuthorTokenID: in.Actor.TokenID,
	}); err != nil {
		return dbq.Document{}, fmt.Errorf("insert document version: %w", err)
	}
	return row, nil
}

// conflictAfterFailedUpsert re-reads a path whose guarded upsert matched
// no row and names what actually stands in the way — a respelling, or a
// version this caller was holding that has since moved.
func (s *Service) conflictAfterFailedUpsert(ctx context.Context, q *dbq.Queries,
	projectID uuid.UUID, in WriteInput,
) error {
	row, err := q.GetDocumentByPath(ctx, dbq.GetDocumentByPathParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, Path: in.Path, IncludeDeleted: true,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("re-read document after a failed upsert: %w", err)
	}
	if row.Path != in.Path {
		return pathRespellingError(in.Path, row.Path)
	}
	return conflictOn(row, in.IncludeCurrent)
}

// conflictOn builds the conflict a caller must merge onto, carrying the
// current document when the caller asked for it.
func conflictOn(row dbq.Document, include bool) error {
	return &ConflictError{
		Current:     row.CurrentVersion,
		Include:     include,
		Title:       row.Title,
		BodyMD:      row.BodyMd,
		Frontmatter: json.RawMessage(row.Frontmatter),
	}
}

// Read returns one document in full, by path.
//
// A soft-deleted document is not found. Deletion is soft so that nothing
// is lost and a mistaken removal is recoverable (spec §3), not so that
// every reader has to filter — a caller that wants the deleted row asks
// the listing for it (Task 8) or reads a version (Task 5).
func (s *Service) Read(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, path string) (dbq.Document, error) {
	if err := CheckPath(path); err != nil {
		return dbq.Document{}, err
	}
	row, err := s.q.GetDocumentByPath(ctx, dbq.GetDocumentByPathParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, Path: path, IncludeDeleted: false,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return dbq.Document{}, notFound(err, missingDocument(path), "read document")
	}
	return row, nil
}
```

---

**Corrections from implementing Task 3** (what the plan's code blocks
got wrong, and what the landed code does instead):

1. **The concurrency tests never raced, so the SQL compare-and-set was
   unpinned.** `TestTwoConcurrentWritesLeaveOneWinnerAndNoGapInTheVersions`
   as written starts two goroutines that each acquire a connection and
   open their own transaction before touching the row, and in practice
   one finishes before the other begins. The second writer's locked read
   then finds the committed row and `writeWith`'s own Go comparison
   refuses it, so the guarded `DO UPDATE` is never reached. Proved by
   mutation: widening the guard to `>=`, and deleting
   `WHERE documents.current_version = @expected_version` outright, both
   left the entire package green — at `-count=1` and at `-count=20`. The
   fix is a *staged* race,
   `TestTheGuardedUpsertIsWhatRefusesACreationThatRacedAnother`: another
   transaction inserts the path and is held open, the writer is started
   and waits on the unique index, the other transaction commits, and the
   guard is the only thing left that can refuse. Both mutations go red
   against it. The same staging gives
   `TestTheReportedCurrentVersionIsTheOneTheWriteWouldHaveMet` a
   deterministic form, and `waitForABlockedStatement` (polling
   `pg_stat_activity` for `wait_event_type = 'Lock'`) is what replaces a
   guessed sleep in both.

2. **`FOR UPDATE` buys neither the refusal nor the fresh version number,
   and the query comment claimed both.** Dropping `FOR UPDATE` from
   `GetDocumentByPathForUpdate` leaves the whole suite green at
   `-count=5`: without the lock the read sees a stale version, passes the
   Go check, the guarded upsert refuses, and `conflictAfterFailedUpsert`
   re-reads and arrives at the same fresh number by a longer route. So
   `TestTheReportedCurrentVersionIsTheOneTheWriteWouldHaveMet` pins the
   *guarantee* and not the clause, and it passes either way. The clause
   stays, because it turns a conflicting writer away before a body, a
   frontmatter blob and a version row are written and rolled back, and
   because the pre-write spelling check runs against a row no other
   transaction can move — but the comment now says that, and says
   plainly that no test here distinguishes its presence.

3. **The post-write `row.Path != in.Path` check in `writeWith` was
   unreachable, and the race the plan credited it with is closed
   somewhere else.** A row the upsert *updated* was matched by the guard,
   which only fires when `expected` equals the stored `current_version`;
   a racing creator's row stands at version 1 while a creating caller
   passes 0, so the losing creation always falls to the `ErrNoRows` arm.
   A row the upsert *inserted* carries the caller's own spelling by
   construction. Proved by mutation: deleting the check left the suite
   green at `-count=3`. What actually answers that race is
   `conflictAfterFailedUpsert`'s own re-read, which the plan mentions in
   passing and credits with nothing;
   `TestACreationLosingToADifferentlySpelledPathIsToldTheSpelling` stages
   it and goes red when that branch is removed. The unreachable check is
   deleted and `writeWith`'s comment records why, so the next reader does
   not restore it. The plan's own Step 10 table half-anticipated this —
   it predicted the *pre*-write check would be the redundant one — and
   had it exactly backwards: with the locked read in place the pre-write
   check is the one that fires, and removing either alone leaves
   `TestARespelledPathIsRefusedNamingBothSpellings` green while removing
   both reddens it.

4. **`TestAWriteIsAnnouncedOnlyAfterItCommits` cannot catch a publish
   moved inside the transaction.** Every failure `Write` can produce
   before its commit is a refusal, and a refusal never reaches a publish
   wherever the call sits. Proved: moving the publish to the last
   statement inside `withTx`'s callback, guarded on a nil error, left the
   suite green. `TestNoEventIsPublishedWhenTheWriteCannotCommit` closes
   it with `internal/metamodel`'s own technique — a
   `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` foreign key from `documents.id` to
   `projects.id`, installed in the test's own throwaway database, so
   every statement succeeds and only the `COMMIT` fails — and goes red
   against that mutation.

5. **The plan's first test fixture is not valid YAML.**
   `"---\ntitle: Wanted: Hogger\n..."` puts a colon-space inside a plain
   scalar, which `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` refuses, so the test failed on
   `SplitContent`'s "not valid YAML" rather than on anything it was
   about. The title is quoted in the landed test. (The refusal itself was
   correct, which is the only reason this was a two-minute finding rather
   than a wrong-reason pass.)

6. **`checkShortText` was a third copy of the UTF-8-then-control scan,
   which is what Task 2's correction 14 exists to prevent.** The plan's
   block hand-rolled `utf8.ValidString` plus `strings.IndexFunc` plus a
   `[]rune(value[i:])[0]` to recover the offending rune. It now calls
   `metamodel.CheckText(value, "")` — the allowance stated by the caller,
   the ordering and the `TextFault` shared — and keeps only its own
   wording and its own argument path. `utf8ValidString` and
   `isAnyControl`, which the plan asked for at the bottom of
   `service.go`, are therefore not written at all.

7. **The three helpers Task 2 deferred landed with this task as their
   first caller**, copied from Task 2's Step 4 and Step 5 blocks rather
   than rewritten: `invalidInputProblems` and `missingDocument` in
   `errors.go`, `pathProblems` in `path.go`. `asValidation` still has no
   caller — `pathProblems` is its only intended one and it reads two
   lines with `errors.As` inline — so it does not ship, and Task 6 should
   add it only if it has its own use.

8. **`oversizeForIndex`'s comment named a case it cannot see.** The
   plan's version said the handler exists for "a body between those two
   sizes", but `0007_documents.sql` bounds all three inputs with
   `left(…, 131072)`, so a large body is truncated for the index and
   written successfully, never refused. What reaches SQLSTATE 54000 is a
   row whose three *bounded* pieces together still exceed `to_tsvector`'s
   own limit. The comment says that, and says outright that **no test in
   this package reaches the handler**, with the argument for keeping it
   anyway (Task 4's resurrection and Task 6's revert write these columns
   from stored values no Go bound re-checks).

9. **Read-back, and the one value this task cannot read back.**
   `TestWritingADocumentCreatesItAtVersionOneAndItReadsBackByteForByte`
   was extended past the plan's four assertions to cover the derived
   summary, the stored path spelling and `current_version`;
   `TestTheActorOfAWriteReadsBackOnTheDocument` reads the four audit
   columns through `Read` and pins that `created_by_*` does not move on a
   second write; and every one of those was proved by a mutation that
   stops storing the value. **But a version's `message`, `author_*`,
   `title`, `summary`, `frontmatter` and `deleted` have no public read
   path at all until Task 6**, so
   `TestTheFirstWriteAlsoWritesVersionOneWithItsAuthorAndMessage` asserts
   them through SQL. That is the exact shape of the write-only defect
   this plan's header describes, held open deliberately by "no query
   lands ahead of its caller". **Task 6 must repeat those assertions
   through `History` and `ReadVersion`**, and until it does the values
   are pinned only against the service, not against the surface.

10. **Smaller things.** `realtime.Subscription` exposes a `C` field and
    the hub an `Unsubscribe` method; the plan's `sub.Events()` and
    `sub.Close()` do not exist. `api_tokens` has required a `token_hint`
    since `0003_api_token_hint.sql`, which the plan's test helper does not
    set. The plan's `errorsAs` / `stringsContains` wrappers are not
    written — the schema test it says to imitate has no such thing, and
    `requireFieldError` is their only would-be caller. And
    `TestAWriteIsAnnouncedOnlyAfterItCommits` blocked forever on `<-sub.C`
    under the `MinRole = editor` mutation, so it takes a 5-second timeout:
    a mutation that hangs a suite is worth no less than one that reddens
    it, but it should not need a `SIGKILL` to report.

**Corrections from Task 3's review** (a second pass, after `cbb21c7`
landed the task and `41618be` recorded corrections 1-10 above):

11. **BLOCKING — `kind` was silently erased by any edit that did not
    restate it.** `UpsertDocument`'s `SET` list carried
    `kind = excluded.kind` unconditionally, so a body-only edit with no
    `Kind` argument overwrote the stored label with the Go zero value.
    Proved live before the fix: create a document with `Kind: "lore"`,
    then a body-only edit passing no kind, and `Read` came back with an
    empty kind. This directly contradicted correction 10's own neighbour
    — `RevertInput`'s comment already said `kind` "is a property of the
    document and is not versioned, so a revert leaves it alone" — and
    `Write` was the one caller breaking that rule. **Fixed by making
    `WriteInput.Kind` a `*string`**, the same shape as `ExpectedVersion`:
    nil means "say nothing about kind" and any value, including `""`,
    means "set it to this." `UpsertDocument`'s `SET kind` is now
    `COALESCE(sqlc.narg('kind')::text, documents.kind)`, and its INSERT
    arm falls back to `''` rather than leaving a new document's kind
    NULL. `TestAnEditThatOmitsKindLeavesItUnchanged` pins both
    directions, and is proved by three separate mutations: reverting the
    `SET` clause to `excluded.kind` reddens the "preserved" assertion,
    and swapping the `COALESCE` arguments (`documents.kind` first)
    reddens the "cleared" assertion — both fail before their opposite
    number, showing the test does not pass by accident either way.
    Consequences chased: Task 5's `writeWith` call no longer needs (and
    no longer has) a `Kind: existing.Kind` workaround to keep its own
    "revert does not touch kind" promise — omitting the field now does
    that on its own — and Task 10's `DocsWriteInput.Kind` is corrected
    to `*string` for the same reason `ExpectedVersion` is one: a plain
    string with `omitempty` cannot tell "the caller said nothing" from
    `"kind":""`, and the tool description now says so.

12. **LOW — the `FOR UPDATE` read's project filter comment understated
    its own coverage.** It called the filter "unpinned until Task 4
    addresses a row by id," but it is pinned now: dropping it reddens
    `TestWritingAPathAnotherGameUsesCreatesASecondDocument` immediately
    — verified by actually dropping it (behind a no-op `OR TRUE` so the
    query's parameter list did not have to change), which turned that
    test's want-a-second-document assertion into a `version_conflict`
    from the first game's row instead. A comment that understates its
    own coverage is the inverse of this file's usual defect and just as
    misleading: it invites a future reader to move the filter believing
    nothing guards it. The comment now names the test.

13. **LOW — the pre-write Go version check and the `FOR UPDATE` clause
    are the same argument, applied to only one of them.** `writeWith`'s
    `if expected != existing.CurrentVersion` check is behaviourally
    redundant with `UpsertDocument`'s SQL guard — deleting it leaves the
    suite green at `-count=3`, verified directly, because the guarded
    upsert refuses on its own and `conflictAfterFailedUpsert`'s re-read
    reports the same version and body. That is exactly the property that
    got the *post*-write `row.Path != in.Path` check deleted as dead code
    in correction 3. The check stays — refusing before a body, a
    frontmatter blob and a version row are built, sent and rolled back is
    the same value `GetDocumentByPathForUpdate`'s own comment credits
    `FOR UPDATE` with — but until now that argument was written down for
    one of the two identical cases and not the other. The comment above
    the check now states it, so the file applies one standard rather
    than two.

14. **LOW — `FOR UPDATE`'s comment named two benefits and a reviewer
    found a third.** It now also says plainly that the lock serialises
    this write against a concurrent *delete* of the row: without it, a
    hard delete landing between the locked read and the upsert would let
    the upsert's INSERT arm fire on the freed path and silently
    re-create the document at version 1, rather than the writer being
    told the row is gone. Unreachable today — Task 3 has no delete, and
    Task 4's is soft — so the comment says that too, and flags a *hard*
    delete racing this read as something worth remembering rather than
    something either task needs to close now.

15. **Recorded for the tasks that follow, no code change here:**
    - **The staged-race technique** — polling `pg_stat_activity` for a
      blocked statement rather than pairing goroutines and hoping they
      overlap — is what correction 1 above already had to reach for, and
      Tasks 4-12 should reach for it first rather than rediscovering it:
      goroutine pairs measurably do not overlap in this suite.
    - **`document_versions.project_id` is written and asserted by
      nothing.** `InsertDocumentVersion` sets it from the caller's
      resolved id, and the composite foreign key from
      `0007_documents.sql` is what would refuse a mismatch, but no test
      in this package forces that refusal. Task 6's isolation tests
      (history, read_version) should cover it, the way
      `documents_schema_test.go` already covers the composite key
      itself.
    - **The `writeWith` comment explaining why there is no post-write
      path check (correction 3) must survive Task 4 and later edits to
      this function.** Its argument — the race is closed by
      `conflictAfterFailedUpsert`'s re-read, not by a check here — stops
      being true the moment someone restructures that re-read, and the
      comment is the only thing standing between a future editor and
      quietly restoring dead code. The same now applies to correction 13's
      comment on the pre-write version check: both explain why a
      seemingly redundant guard is kept, and both go stale the same way
      if the code they describe moves without the comment moving with it.

Verification for this second pass: `go test ./internal/markdown/ -v
-count=1` — 68 tests (`TestAnEditThatOmitsKindLeavesItUnchanged` added),
0 skipped, `TEST_DATABASE_URL` exported and confirmed reaching the
process (the baseline run was checked with `-v` before any mutation was
trusted to be "killed"). Five mutations run one at a time this pass, all
five red: `SET kind = excluded.kind` restored (kills the "preserved"
half), the `COALESCE` arguments swapped (kills the "cleared" half), the
`FOR UPDATE` read's project filter dropped behind a no-op `OR TRUE`
(kills `TestWritingAPathAnotherGameUsesCreatesASecondDocument`), and —
run to confirm what correction 13 states rather than to find a bug — the
pre-write version check deleted, confirmed green at `-count=3` as the
comment now says. `make check` clean across the repository (`gofmt`,
`go vet`, `golangci-lint`, `sqlc diff`, `go test -race ./...`).

### Task 4: Soft delete, the tombstone, and resurrection

**Files:**
- Modify: `internal/db/queries/documents.sql`
- Modify: `internal/markdown/events.go`
- Modify: `internal/markdown/documents.go`
- Create: `internal/markdown/delete_test.go`

**The decision this task makes, argued before its code.** A soft delete
**appends a version row** — a tombstone carrying the body exactly as it
stood, with `deleted` true — and **bumps `current_version`**. The
alternative, setting `deleted_at` and leaving the version line alone, is
simpler and wrong for two reasons:

1. **The invariant.** The spec's own definition of done (§9.3) is that
   `current_version` always equals the newest version row. A delete that
   moves one and not the other breaks it, and the invariant is not
   decoration — `current_version` is the concurrency token every writer
   compares against, and a token that can disagree with the history is a
   token nobody can reason about.
2. **The silent resurrection.** Without the bump, an agent holding
   version 3 from before the delete writes with `expected_version: 3`,
   the guard matches, and the document comes back with the deletion
   erased and nothing said. With the bump, that agent gets a
   `version_conflict` carrying version 4, re-reads, and discovers the
   document was deleted. The whole point of the token is to make a
   caller notice that the ground moved.

The cost is one duplicated body per deletion. Snapshots are full anyway
(spec §3), a deletion is rare next to an edit, and what it buys is that
`docs.history` shows *who deleted this and when* — which is the first
question anyone asks about a document that vanished.

- [ ] **Step 1: Add the query**

Append to `internal/db/queries/documents.sql`:

```sql
-- name: SoftDeleteDocument :one
-- Guarded by the caller's expected version, exactly as UpsertDocument
-- is, so a delete cannot race an edit: the loser matches no row and gets
-- back no row, which Go turns into the typed conflict.
--
-- deleted_at IS NULL is part of the guard, so deleting a document twice
-- is not_found on the second call rather than a second tombstone. That
-- is the honest answer: the document is already gone, and the caller has
-- nothing to do.
--
-- current_version advances here and the caller appends the matching
-- tombstone version in the same transaction. The two are one change and
-- withTx is what keeps them so.
UPDATE documents
SET deleted_at          = now(),
    current_version     = current_version + 1,
    updated_by_user_id  = sqlc.narg('actor_user_id')::uuid,
    updated_by_token_id = sqlc.narg('actor_token_id')::uuid
WHERE project_id = sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid
  AND lower(path) = lower(sqlc.arg('path')::text)
  AND deleted_at IS NULL
  AND current_version = sqlc.arg('expected_version')::integer
RETURNING *;
```

Run `make sqlc && make sqlc-check`. Expected: `sqlc diff` silent.

```go
func TestDeletingADocumentHidesItFromReadsAndKeepsItsHistory(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{

	}

	if _, err := svc.Read(ctx, game, "bible"); !errors.Is(err, markdown.ErrNotFound) {
		t.Fatalf("a deleted document must read as not_found, got %v", err)
	}

	var current int32
	if err := pool.QueryRow(ctx,
		`SELECT d.current_version, (SELECT count(*) FROM document_versions v WHERE v.document_id = d.id)
		   FROM documents d WHERE d.project_id = $1 AND d.path = 'bible'`,
		game).Scan(&current, &versions); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("read the document: %v", err)
	}
	if current != 2 || versions != 2 {
		t.Fatalf("current_version = %d with %d version rows, want 2 and 2: "+
			"a delete appends a tombstone and the two must never disagree", current, versions)
	}

	var deleted bool
	var body, message string
	if err := pool.QueryRow(ctx,
		`SELECT v.deleted, v.body_md, v.message FROM document_versions v

		  WHERE d.project_id = $1 AND d.path = 'bible' AND v.version = 2`,
		game).Scan(&deleted, &body, &message); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("read the tombstone: %v", err)
	}

		t.Fatalf("tombstone = (deleted %t, body %q, message %q), want (true, %q, %q)",
			deleted, body, message, "one\n", "cut for now")
	}
}

	defer sub.Close()

		Path: "bible", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(1),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Delete: %v", err)

		t.Fatalf("Kind = %q, want %q", ev.Kind, "document.deleted")
	}

	}
```
