- [ ] **Step 5: Write Delete**

Append to `internal/markdown/documents.go`:

```go
// DeleteInput is one soft deletion.
//
// Message is recorded on the tombstone version, so "why was this cut"
// is answerable from the history rather than from someone's memory.
type DeleteInput struct {
	Path            string
	Message         string
	ExpectedVersion *int32
	Actor           Actor
}

// Delete soft-deletes one document: the row keeps its path and its whole
// history, and a later write to the same path resurrects it.
//
// **It appends a tombstone version and advances current_version.** Task
// 4's own header argues why at length; in short, so that current_version
// never disagrees with the newest version row, and so that a caller
// holding a version from before the delete conflicts instead of
// resurrecting the document without noticing.
//
// Hard deletion is deliberately not offered. Losing a designer's writing
// to an agent's mistaken tool call is not a risk this product takes; an
// instance admin who genuinely must purge a row does it in the database.
func (s *Service) Delete(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, in DeleteInput) error {
	problems := pathProblems(in.Path)
	problems = append(problems, checkShortText("message", in.Message, MaxMessageLen)...)
	if in.ExpectedVersion == nil {
		problems = append(problems, metamodel.FieldError{
			Path:    "expected_version",
			Message: "is required: pass the version you read, so a delete cannot race an edit",
		})
	}
	if len(problems) > 0 {
		return invalidInputProblems(problems)
	}

	var removed dbq.Document
	err := s.withTx(ctx, func(q *dbq.Queries) error {
		row, err := q.SoftDeleteDocument(ctx, dbq.SoftDeleteDocumentParams{
			ProjectID:       projectID,
			Path:            in.Path,
			ExpectedVersion: *in.ExpectedVersion,
			ActorUserID:     in.Actor.UserID,
			ActorTokenID:    in.Actor.TokenID,
		})
		if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) {
			// Three things reach here and they need telling apart, which
			// is why this is a re-read and not a bare not_found: the
			// path names no document at all, it names a document already
			// deleted, or it names one whose version has moved. Only the
			// third is a conflict, and only the third has a recovery
			// that is not "stop".
			return s.deleteRefusal(ctx, q, projectID, in)
		}
		if err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("soft delete document: %w", err)
		}
		removed = row
		if _, err := q.InsertDocumentVersion(ctx, dbq.InsertDocumentVersionParams{
			ProjectID:     projectID,
			DocumentID:    row.ID,
			Version:       row.CurrentVersion,
			Title:         row.Title,
			Summary:       row.Summary,
			BodyMd:        row.BodyMd,
			Frontmatter:   row.Frontmatter,
			Message:       in.Message,
			Deleted:       true,
			AuthorUserID:  in.Actor.UserID,
			AuthorTokenID: in.Actor.TokenID,
		}); err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("insert tombstone version: %w", err)
		}
		return nil
	})
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	s.publish(projectID, eventDocumentDeleted, documentEventMinRole, documentEventHumanOnly,
		DocumentEvent{ID: removed.ID, Path: removed.Path, Version: removed.CurrentVersion})
	return nil
}

// deleteRefusal names what actually stood in the way of a delete that
// matched no row.
func (s *Service) deleteRefusal(ctx context.Context, q *dbq.Queries,
	projectID uuid.UUID, in DeleteInput,
) error {
	row, err := q.GetDocumentByPath(ctx, dbq.GetDocumentByPathParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, Path: in.Path, IncludeDeleted: true,
	})
	if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) {
		return missingDocument(in.Path)
	}
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("re-read document after a refused delete: %w", err)
	}
	if row.DeletedAt != nil {
		return &MissingError{
			Path: "path",
			Message: fmt.Sprintf("the document at %q was already deleted; "+
				"write to the path to bring it back", in.Path),
		}
	}
	// IncludeCurrent is deliberately false: a caller deleting a document
	// is not merging prose, and echoing a body it asked to remove would
	// be the largest payload in the system attached to the one call that
	// wanted none of it.
	return conflictOn(row, false)
}
```

`Revert` both call this statement and must pass `Deleted: false`
explicitly**; a compile error is what tells you if one was missed, which
is why the argument is required rather than defaulted.

---

**Corrections from implementing Task 4** (what the plan's code blocks got
wrong, and what the landed code does instead):

1. **`Delete` returns the tombstoned row, not just an error.** The
   plan's signature is `error`, and it leaves a caller with no way to
   learn the version the deletion landed on — which is the one number
   needed to bring the document back, and the one number `Read` cannot
   supply, because `Read` is precisely what stops answering. "Delete,
   then undo" would have been a caller inferring `current + 1` from
   whatever it happened to hold. It now returns `(dbq.Document, error)`
   like every other mutation in this package, and
   `TestDeletingADocumentHidesItFromReadsAndKeepsItsHistory` reads the
   version, the `deleted_at` and (in
   `TestTheActorOfADeletionIsRecorded`) the audit columns back off it.
   Every `svc.Delete(...)` call in the blocks of Tasks 6, 8, 9 and 10
   moves with this: they are written `if err := svc.Delete(...)` and
   must become `if _, err := svc.Delete(...)`.
2. **`documents.deleted_at` is a `pgtype.Timestamptz`, not a pointer.**
   Three of the plan's assertions are `doc.DeletedAt != nil`, which does
   not compile against the generated model. The test is
   `DeletedAt.Valid`. Same correction applies wherever a later task
   copies that line.
3. **BLOCKING for an agent, fixed here — a conflict on a deleted
   document sent the caller into a dead end.** The plan's
   `TestAStaleVersionCannotSilentlyResurrectADocument` asserts only that
   the stale writer gets `version_conflict` at version 2. It does, and
   the message it gets is `ConflictError`'s default: "merge onto it and
   write again." Following that instruction means re-reading the
   document, and re-reading a deleted document answers `not_found` —
   two refusals from one call with nothing connecting them, and no
   statement anywhere that the path is recoverable. `ConflictError`
   gains a `Deleted bool`, set by `conflictOn` off the row it already
   read (so no call site can forget it), which changes `Error()` to
   "this document was deleted at version N; write to the path with that
   `expected_version` to bring it back" and adds `"deleted": true` to
   `Details()` — present only when true, so an ordinary conflict is
   unchanged on the wire. Both directions are pinned and both mutations
   are red: `conflictOn` hardcoded to `false` reddens
   `TestAStaleVersionCannotSilentlyResurrectADocument`, and `Details()`
   publishing the key unconditionally reddens
   `TestAnOrdinaryConflictDoesNotClaimTheDocumentWasDeleted`. **Task
   10's `docs.write` description and its conflict-shape test must
   publish this field**, and Task 10's
   `TestEveryMarkdownDomainErrorHasAWireCode` is where a missing arm
   would show up.
4. **The plan's Step 8 predicted `TestADeletionIsAnnounced` would stay
   green against a publish moved inside `withTx`, and it is right — but
   the fix it proposes does not close it either.** Making the tombstone
   insert fail (Step 8's suggestion, landed as
   `TestNoDeletionIsAnnouncedWhenTheTombstoneCannotBeWritten`, which
   seeds a colliding `document_versions` row) leaves the callback with
   an error *before* the publish statement is reached, so that mutation
   survives it too — verified. Only a failure at `COMMIT` distinguishes
   the two placements, exactly as Task 3's correction 4 found for
   `Write`. `TestNoDeletionIsAnnouncedWhenTheDeleteCannotCommit` uses
   the same deferred-constraint technique with two changes forced by
   this call: the key hangs off `document_versions`, not `documents`,
   because `Delete`'s only INSERT is the tombstone and an UPDATE that
   does not touch `documents.id` never fires a key on that column; and
   it is added `NOT VALID`, because version 1 already stands and would
   fail the check on the spot. Both tests are kept — the first pins the
   rollback (the document is still readable at version 1 afterwards),
   the second pins the placement.
5. **The `internal_error` Task 3 left for this task to settle is
   unreachable, and now says so where it lives.**
   `conflictAfterFailedUpsert`'s `err != nil` arm would need the row to
   vanish between the failed upsert and the re-read one statement later:
   that takes a hard delete, and there is none — `Delete` is soft, and
   no query in `internal/db/queries` deletes a `documents` row at all.
   The only remover is a project's own `ON DELETE CASCADE`, which takes
   the same row lock the transaction is already holding. The arm is left
   as `internal_error` deliberately: if it fires, the cause is a hard
   delete nobody wrote, not a caller's mistake. Recorded in the code,
   not only here. The same reasoning is added to
   `GetDocumentByPathForUpdate`'s comment, whose third
   `FOR UPDATE` benefit Task 3's correction 14 flagged as becoming
   load-bearing here: it does not, because `SoftDeleteDocument` is an
   UPDATE and takes the same lock.
6. **The plan's tests use `requireFieldError` for `not_found`s it does
   not fit.** `MissingError` is not a `*metamodel.ValidationError`, so
   the helper cannot assert its message — and asserting only the
   sentinel cannot tell "already deleted" from "never here", which are
   the same code and the same path with different recoveries.
   `requireMissing` is added to `testhelpers_test.go` and both messages
   are asserted in full.
7. **`InsertDocumentVersion`'s `deleted` is a required argument, as the
   plan said, and the argument for that is now in the query's own
   comment**: `Delete`'s tombstone is the only caller that passes true,
   and a defaulted column would let a future snapshot-writing path store
   a live version where a tombstone belonged. Task 6's `Revert` must
   pass `Deleted: false` explicitly; a compile error is what tells it so.
   `TestOnlyTheTombstoneVersionIsMarkedDeleted` reads all four versions
   of one document (write, write, delete, resurrect) back and pins which
   single one carries the flag — the plan had no test that a *live*
   version is not marked deleted, and `writeWith` passing `true` would
   otherwise have gone unnoticed.
8. **Six comments in `documents.sql` and `documents.go` claimed a
   behaviour was "unexercised until Task 4".** All of them are now
   exercised, and each names the test instead: `UpsertDocument`'s
   `deleted_at = NULL` clause, `GetDocumentByPath`'s `include_deleted`
   arm, `Read`'s doc comment, and `GetDocumentByPathForUpdate`'s
   hard-delete paragraph. A comment that dates itself to a task that has
   landed is the same defect as one that overclaims, one step later.
9. **Smaller decisions, argued in the code rather than assumed.**
   `deleteRefusal` does not re-check the path for a respelling the way
   `conflictAfterFailedUpsert` does: `SoftDeleteDocument` matches on
   `lower(path)`, so a delete under another casing reaches the same row
   and succeeds, and `TestAPathIsMatchedWithoutRegardToCaseOnDelete`
   pins that the *stored* spelling comes back on the row and therefore
   in the event payload. A refused delete carries `Include: false` and
   `TestDeletingWithAStaleVersionIsAConflict` asserts `Details()` holds
   no `current_body`. `expected_version: 0` has no special meaning on a
   delete — no stored `current_version` can equal it — so it is refused
   as `not_found` or as a conflict, and `DeleteInput`'s comment says so
   rather than leaving a reader to infer the write's rule applies.
   `TestEveryProblemWithOneDeleteIsReportedInOnePass` pins that a bad
   path, a bad message and a missing version arrive as one refusal with
   three fields.

**Corrections from Task 4's review** (a second pass, after `b5680a5`
landed the task and `73a591b` recorded corrections 1-9 above):

10. **MEDIUM — a real hole, not a comment problem: `conflictAfterFailedUpsert`'s
    `IncludeDeleted: true` was pinned by a test that cannot reach it, and
    its own comment said the opposite of what the flag does.** The
    comment claimed `TestAStaleVersionCannotSilentlyResurrectADocument`
    covers it; that test's stale write is refused earlier, by
    `writeWith`'s own `conflictOn` under the locked read, and never
    reaches this function — proved by flipping the flag to `false` and
    watching all 85 tests stay green. The race that does reach it is a
    *creation* whose path is created **and** soft-deleted by another
    transaction between the creating writer's locked read (which finds
    nothing, so locks nothing) and its guarded upsert: the loser's guard
    fails on the version mismatch exactly as an ordinary racing creation
    does, and `conflictAfterFailedUpsert` must re-read the tombstone —
    not a live row — to report it. `TestACreationRacingACreateAndDeleteIsToldTheTombstone`
    stages exactly that, using the same "hold an uncommitted transaction
    open so the second writer blocks on the guarded upsert" technique as
    `TestTheGuardedUpsertIsWhatRefusesACreationThatRacedAnother`, except
    the held-open transaction both creates the row and soft-deletes it
    before committing. Baseline confirmed green with `-v` (86 tests now,
    the new one included); flipping `IncludeDeleted` to `false` reddens
    it with `re-read document after a failed upsert: no rows in result
    set` — an `internal_error` over the caller's own conflict, exactly
    what the comment claimed the shipped configuration prevented while
    describing the shipped configuration as the broken one. The comment
    on `conflictAfterFailedUpsert` is corrected in both directions: it
    no longer says the filter being *on* is what breaks the read, and it
    names the test that actually exercises the arm. The mirrored
    overclaim in `GetDocumentByPath`'s own comment (`documents.sql`, and
    the generated `dbq/documents.sql.go` after `sqlc generate`) is
    corrected the same way — it now attributes `deleteRefusal`'s
    `include_deleted` arm to `TestDeletingTwiceIsNotFoundRatherThanASecondTombstone`
    and `conflictAfterFailedUpsert`'s to the new test, rather than
    naming both functions against one test that only covers one of them.
11. **LOW — a comment named a test that does not exist, inherited from
    Task 3 and missed by a review round that was hunting exactly this

    named `TestTwoConcurrentCreationsOfOnePathLeaveOneWinner`, which
    appears nowhere; the arm is actually covered by
    `TestTheGuardedUpsertIsWhatRefusesACreationThatRacedAnother`, and the

    test set — reconstructing identifiers that prose-wrapping had split
    across two comment lines, since a Go identifier itself never
    contains a line break. This was the only dangler the sweep found
    beyond the one already known; `errors.go`'s
    `TestEveryMarkdownDomainErrorHasAWireCode` is a second forward

12. **LOW — a claim that was checkable and wrong.** `oversizeForIndex`'s
    comment said "Task 6's revert and Task 4's resurrection write the
    same columns from stored values that no Go bound re-checks," offered
    as part of why the mapping is kept though no test in this package
    reaches it. Resurrection does not support that claim: it routes
    through `writeWith` like any other write, and the content it writes

    writes stored values with no Go bound re-checking them is `Delete`'s
    own tombstone insert — and it cannot trip this mapping regardless,
    because `document_versions` carries no generated `tsvector` column
    for `to_tsvector` to overflow on. The comment is corrected to say
    that every caller reaching `writeWith`'s guarded upsert is equally
    exposed to the mapping, resurrection included, and that the
    tombstone insert is the one write in this task that is not.
13. **Recorded for the tasks that follow, not fixed here.**

      plus the `*markdown.ConflictError` arm and the `deleted` field on

### Task 5: Extract the keyset cursor into `internal/paging`

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/paging/cursor.go`
- Create: `internal/paging/cursor_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/metamodel/list.go`
- Modify: `internal/metamodel/relations.go` (its `relationPageSize` call sites)
- Modify: `internal/metamodel/search.go` (its `pageSize` call site)

**Why this task exists, and why it is here rather than inside Task 8.**
`cursor`, `encodeCursor`, `decodeCursor`, `fingerprintOf`, `pageSize` and
`invalidFilterPart` are all unexported in `internal/metamodel/list.go`.
The documents listing (Task 8) and the version history (Task 6) both
need exactly them. Copying is the "same hole one step along" defect in
its purest form: the bug that shipped in this very code — a fingerprint
that omitted the project id, so one game's cursor paged another game's
rows — would be re-introduced in the copy, fixed in one of the two, and
the surviving one would be the one nobody was looking at.

**The acceptance test for this extraction is that no metamodel test
changes.** Not one line. Then the seam is re-mutated to confirm the old
tests still reach through it.

**The import cycle, and how it is avoided.** `malformedCursor` returns a
`*metamodel.ValidationError`, so a `paging` package that built the error
itself would import `metamodel`, which imports `paging`. So `paging`
owns the cursor logic *and the message text*, and takes a `refuse
func(message string) error` from its caller, which is what turns a
message into that domain's own error type at its own path. One mechanism,
two error types, and the two packages cannot drift on what a bad cursor
says.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the package**

`internal/paging/cursor.go`:

```go
// Package paging is the keyset cursor every listing in Maestro pages
// with: its position, its fingerprint, and the clamping rule for a
// requested limit.
//
// It exists because two domains need the same cursor and a copy of a
// cursor is a copy of its bugs. The one this code shipped with omitted
// the project id from its fingerprint, so an unfiltered listing in one
// game produced a cursor that paged perfectly into another game's rows
// and answered with them. That was a wrong answer, not a leak — no row
// crossed the boundary, the *position* did — and it is exactly the kind
// of defect a second copy would inherit and then keep after the first
// was fixed.
//
// It deliberately builds no errors of its own. Every refusal goes
// through a refuse function the caller supplies, so the error is the
// caller's domain type at the caller's own argument path, and the
// *message* still lives here — which is what keeps two domains from
// telling a caller two different things about one bad cursor. Building
// the error here instead would mean importing internal/metamodel, which
// imports this package.
package paging

import (
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/base64"
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"strings"

	"github.com/google/uuid"
)

// Cursor is the keyset position of the last row of a page, together
// with a fingerprint of the listing it was issued for.
//
// **The contract a caller has to know**, which every listing that
// issues one of these must state on its own exported page type:
//
//   - A cursor is set when a page came back full, and empty otherwise.
//     A caller looping until the cursor is empty is correct and must
//     expect a final empty page rather than treating one as an error.
//   - A cursor is a *position*, not a snapshot: it is "the rows after
//     this position", never "skip this many rows". That is what makes
//     it stable under concurrent editing — a row inserted or deleted
//     before the position does not slide the window, and the row the
//     cursor names need not still exist. What it does not buy is a
//     consistent view of the whole listing: with a mutable sort key, a
//     row edited to sort after the position can be seen twice, and one
//     edited to sort before it is never seen again by that listing.
//     A caller that needs a consistent whole re-reads from no cursor.
//   - A cursor belongs to the listing that issued it, and to no other:
//     the game, the filter, and a traversal's anchor and direction.
//     Every filter of a listing shares one sort order, so a cursor
//     carried across would page perfectly and answer a different
//     question. Fingerprint is what refuses that.
//   - **It is not a capability and it is not signed.** It is base64 of
//     JSON; a caller can decode it, rewrite the position and recompute
//     the fingerprint from values it already holds. That buys nothing,
//     because the position only ever becomes a `>` comparison inside a
//     statement already filtered by the caller's own project id. The
//     fingerprint is a consistency check against a caller's own
//     mistake and must not be relied on as a security boundary.
//
// Sort is the leading half of the sort key, spelled as text, because a
// cursor is an opaque token whose only job is to come back unchanged,
// and the listing that issued it — pinned by the fingerprint — is the
// only code that has to know how to read it.
type Cursor struct {
	Sort        string    `json:"n"`
	ID          uuid.UUID `json:"i"`
	Fingerprint string    `json:"f"`
}

// Encode renders a cursor as the opaque token a caller passes back.
func Encode(c Cursor) string {
	raw, err := json.Marshal(c)
	if err != nil {
		// Unreachable: every field is a string or a uuid.UUID, whose
		// MarshalJSON cannot fail. Returning no cursor rather than
		// panicking keeps a listing answerable if that ever stops being
		// true — a page without a cursor ends the listing, which is a
		// smaller lie than a page with an unreadable one.
		return ""
	}
	return base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(raw)
}

// Decode reads a page position back and checks it belongs to the listing
// it was handed to.
//
// refuse turns a message into the caller's own domain error, at the
// caller's own argument path — `cursor` in both of Maestro's domains.
// Left untyped, a bad cursor reaches an agent as internal_error and
// reads as "the server is broken" over a value the agent itself
// supplied.
//
// **The order of the two refusals matters.** A cursor that is not
// readable at all is malformed, and only a readable one can be judged
// against the fingerprint. Reversed, a truncated cursor would be
// reported as belonging to another listing, which sends a caller to
// inspect its filter instead of the value it passed.
func Decode(s, fingerprint string, refuse func(message string) error) (Cursor, error) {
	if s == "" {
		return Cursor{}, nil
	}
	malformed := func(why string) error {
		return refuse(fmt.Sprintf(
			"is malformed (%s): page from the cursor a previous call returned, or omit it to start",
			why))
	}
	raw, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(s)
	if err != nil {
		return Cursor{}, malformed("it is not the encoding this listing issues")
	}
	var c Cursor
	if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &c); err != nil {
		return Cursor{}, malformed("it does not decode to a page position")
	}
	if c.ID == uuid.Nil {
		return Cursor{}, malformed("it carries no row position")
	}
	if c.Fingerprint != fingerprint {
		return Cursor{}, refuse("was issued for a different listing: page with the filter " +
			"the cursor came from, or omit the cursor to start this listing over")
	}
	return c, nil
}

// Fingerprint digests the resolved filter a cursor was issued under.
//
// **The project id must be the first part, always.** It is not enforced
// here — there is no way to enforce it on a variadic list of strings —
// so every caller passes it first and every listing has a test that a
// cursor from another game is refused. That is the discipline the
// original defect was fixed with, and the reason this comment is the
// first thing a new caller reads.
//
// The parts are joined length-prefixed, because without it a filter
// whose parts run together spells the same string as a different filter
// whose parts divide elsewhere, and two listings would share one
// fingerprint. It hashes rather than storing the parts so the cursor
// does not grow with the filter, and truncates to 96 bits because this
// is a consistency check and not a signature.
func Fingerprint(parts ...string) string {
	var b strings.Builder
	for _, p := range parts {
		fmt.Fprintf(&b, "%d:%s", len(p), p)
	}
	sum := sha256.Sum256([]byte(b.String()))
	return base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(sum[:12])
}

// Size turns a caller's requested limit into the one a listing will use:
// asking for nothing is no opinion and gets the default, asking for too
// much is an opinion and gets the cap.
//
// **Clamped, never folded onto the default.** Folding both arms makes
// asking for 501 return strictly fewer rows than asking for 500, with
// nothing in the answer to say so.
func Size(limit, def, max int32) int32 {
	switch {
	case limit <= 0:
		return def
	case limit > max:
		return max
	default:
		return limit
	}
}

// TriState spells an optional boolean filter for a fingerprint, keeping
// "no opinion" distinct from both of the opinions. Without it, a cursor
// issued for "all rows" pages a listing filtered to "invalid rows only",
// which is a different question with the same answer shape.
func TriState(value *bool) string {
	if value == nil {
		return "any"
	}
	return fmt.Sprintf("%t", *value)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the package's own tests**

`internal/paging/cursor_test.go`:

```go
package paging_test

import (
	"errors"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/google/uuid"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/paging"
)

var errRefused = errors.New("refused")

func refuse(message string) error { return fmt.Errorf("%w: %s", errRefused, message) }

func TestARoundTrippedCursorComesBackUnchanged(t *testing.T) {
	want := paging.Cursor{Sort: "Duskwood", ID: uuid.New(), Fingerprint: "abc"}
	got, err := paging.Decode(paging.Encode(want), "abc", refuse)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Decode: %v", err)
	}
	if got != want {
		t.Fatalf("Decode(Encode(c)) = %+v, want %+v", got, want)
	}
}

func TestAnEmptyCursorIsTheStartOfTheListing(t *testing.T) {
	got, err := paging.Decode("", "abc", refuse)
	if err != nil || got.ID != uuid.Nil {
		t.Fatalf("Decode(\"\") = (%+v, %v), want the zero position and no error", got, err)
	}
}

func TestAnUnreadableCursorIsMalformedBeforeItIsJudgedAgainstAFingerprint(t *testing.T) {
	// The order matters: reported as belonging to another listing, a
	// truncated cursor sends a caller to inspect its filter, which is
	// not where the problem is.
	_, err := paging.Decode("!!!not base64!!!", "abc", refuse)
	if !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "is malformed") {
		t.Fatalf("err = %v, want it to say the cursor is malformed", err)
	}
}

func TestACursorFromAnotherListingIsRefused(t *testing.T) {
	c := paging.Cursor{Sort: "a", ID: uuid.New(), Fingerprint: paging.Fingerprint("game-a", "documents")}
	_, err := paging.Decode(paging.Encode(c), paging.Fingerprint("game-b", "documents"), refuse)
	if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "different listing") {
		t.Fatalf("err = %v, want a refusal naming the listing", err)
	}
}

func TestPartsThatDivideDifferentlyDoNotShareAFingerprint(t *testing.T) {
	// Without the length prefix, ("ab", "c") and ("a", "bc") spell one
	// string and two different listings share one fingerprint.
	if paging.Fingerprint("ab", "c") == paging.Fingerprint("a", "bc") {
		t.Fatal("two filters that divide differently must not share a fingerprint")
	}
}

func TestSizeClampsRatherThanFoldingOntoTheDefault(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct{ limit, want int32 }{
		{-7, 50}, {0, 50}, {1, 1}, {37, 37}, {499, 499}, {500, 500}, {501, 500}, {1 << 20, 500},
	} {
		if got := paging.Size(tc.limit, 50, 500); got != tc.want {
			t.Fatalf("Size(%d, 50, 500) = %d, want %d", tc.limit, got, tc.want)
		}
	}
}

func TestTriStateKeepsNoOpinionDistinctFromBothOpinions(t *testing.T) {
	yes, no := true, false
	got := []string{paging.TriState(nil), paging.TriState(&yes), paging.TriState(&no)}
	if got[0] == got[1] || got[0] == got[2] || got[1] == got[2] {
		t.Fatalf("TriState produced %v, want three distinct spellings", got)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Make `internal/metamodel` use the extracted code**

In `internal/metamodel/list.go`, delete the `cursor` type,
`encodeCursor`, `decodeCursor`, `fingerprintOf`, `pageSize` and
`invalidFilterPart`, and replace their uses. The three edits:

```go
// refuseCursor turns paging's message into this package's own error, at
// the argument's own path. The messages live in internal/paging so that
// this domain and the markdown domain cannot tell a caller two different
// things about one bad cursor; the *type* is this domain's, because that
// is what internal/web's existing invalid_input arm matches on.
func refuseCursor(message string) error {
	return &ValidationError{Code: codeInvalidInput, Fields: []FieldError{{
		Path: "cursor", Message: message,
	}}}
}
```

Then, at each of the three listings, `fingerprintOf(...)` becomes
`paging.Fingerprint(...)` **with the project id still first**,
`decodeCursor(f.Cursor, fingerprint)` becomes
`paging.Decode(f.Cursor, fingerprint, refuseCursor)`,
`encodeCursor(cursor{...})` becomes
`paging.Encode(paging.Cursor{...})`, `pageSize(...)` becomes
`paging.Size(...)` (in `list.go`, `relations.go` and `search.go`), and
`invalidFilterPart(f.Invalid)` becomes `paging.TriState(f.Invalid)`.

The `after.ID` / `after.Sort` reads at each call site are unchanged:
`paging.Cursor` has the same two field names for exactly that reason.

**Corrections from implementing Task 5** (what the plan's code blocks got
wrong, and what the landed code does instead):

1. **Step 5's "delete the six identifiers" contradicts this task's own
   acceptance test, and the acceptance test wins.**
   `internal/metamodel/list_internal_test.go` is `package metamodel` —
   an in-package test — and it calls `pageSize`, `fingerprintOf`,
   `invalidFilterPart`, `encodeCursor`, `cursor` and `decodeCursor` by
   name across four tests
   (`TestAnEntityPageAsksForTooMuchAndGetsTheCap`,
   `TestAFingerprintTellsFiltersApartWhereverTheirPartsDivide`,
   `TestTheInvalidFilterKeepsItsThreeStatesApart`,
   `TestACursorCarriesNothingButAPositionAndAFingerprint`,
   `TestAMalformedCursorIsRefusedBeforeItsFingerprintIsJudged`). Deleting
   the names would not have changed behaviour, it would have failed to
   *compile* the metamodel test binary, and the only way through would
   have been editing five tests in a task whose stated acceptance test is
   that no metamodel test changes. So the six names stay as one-line
   delegations to `internal/paging`, the three listings keep calling
   them, and the implementation — the only thing that could be copied
   and drift — lives in exactly one place. The alternative considered and
   rejected was converting the call sites to `paging.*` and keeping the
   six names as shims nobody but the tests calls: that is two spellings
   of one API in one package plus six test-only functions, for the sake
   of a rename. The extraction is at the package boundary either way, and
   Step 7's mutations prove the tests still reach through it.
2. **`malformedCursor` has a second caller the plan did not see, so the
   message needed an exported home.** `ListRelations` parses a decoded
   cursor's sort half back into a timestamp
   (`internal/metamodel/relations.go`) and calls
   `malformedCursor("it carries no creation time")` *after*
   `paging.Decode` has already returned — a step `Decode` cannot reach.
   With the sentence living only inside `Decode`, that call site would
   have had to spell "is malformed (…): page from the cursor a previous
   call returned, or omit it to start" a second time, which is the drift
   this whole task exists to prevent. `paging.Malformed(why, refuse)` is
   exported for it, `Decode` builds its own three refusals through it,
   and `TestMalformedSaysTheSameThingDecodeDoes` pins that the two
   spellings are one. Recorded but not fixed: **no test in the repository
   reaches the "no creation time" arm** — it needs a forged cursor whose
   fingerprint agrees but whose sort half is not RFC 3339 — and that was
   already true before this task; the arm's own comment says only that a
   hand-edited cursor reaches it, and claims no test.
3. **The plan's `cursor_test.go` does not compile as written**, in two
   ways: `refuse` uses `fmt.Errorf` with no `fmt` import (the block says
   so in a line underneath), and
   `paging.Fingerprint("a", "b") != paging.Fingerprint("a", "b")` is
   staticcheck SA4000 (identical expressions either side of `!=`), which
   `make check` refuses. The stability check is written through two
   named variables, the way `list_internal_test.go` already writes it.
4. **Three tests were added to the plan's seven**, because three of
   `Decode`'s branches and the package's central claim had none:
   `TestEveryUnreadableCursorIsRefusedTheSameWay` covers all three
   malformed shapes (bad encoding, not a page position, no row
   position) rather than only the first and asserts none of them is
   judged against the fingerprint; `TestMalformedSaysTheSameThingDecodeDoes`
   is correction 2's; and both refusal tests now assert
   `errors.Is(err, errRefused)` — that the error came back out of the
   caller's own refuse function — which is the one thing "paging builds
   no errors of its own" actually means and which the plan's tests
   only checked the *text* of.
5. **The fingerprint-composition prose was kept in `internal/metamodel`,
   not moved.** `paging.Fingerprint`'s comment states the rule (project
   id first, always, unenforceable on a variadic list) and names the
   test; the metamodel keeps the sentence saying what *its* parts are and
   in what order, because that is a fact about its three listings and not
   about the shared helper. Every test name either comment cites was
   checked to exist.

Verification for this pass: baseline `go test ./internal/metamodel/ -v
-count=1` with `TEST_DATABASE_URL` exported — **389 PASS, 0 SKIP, 0
FAIL** — and the same 389 verdicts, name for name, after the extraction
(`diff` over the sorted `--- ` lines is empty). **No test file in
`internal/metamodel` was touched**; `git status` shows two modified
non-test files (`list.go`, and one sentence of a comment in
`relations.go`) and one new package. Ten mutations run one at a time,
all ten red: the project
id dropped from `ListEntities`' fingerprint (reddens
`TestACursorFromAnotherGameIsRefused`); `paging.Decode`'s fingerprint
check moved above the base64 decode (reddens the paging order test *and*
metamodel's `TestAMalformedCursorIsInvalidInput`, four subtests — the
plan predicted only the first); `paging.Size`'s over-cap arm folded onto
the default (reddens `TestAnEntityPageAsksForTooMuchAndGetsTheCap`,
`TestARelationPageAsksForTooMuchAndGetsTheCap` and
`TestSearchAnswersAnOverLargeLimitWithMoreThanTheDefault` — all three
listings, from the far side of the seam); and, against the new package's
own tests, the length prefix removed from `Fingerprint`, `TriState`
spelling nil as `false`, `Encode` dropping the sort half, `Decode`
inlining its own malformed sentence, the empty-cursor short circuit
removed, the nil-id check removed, and the fingerprint check removed.
`make check` clean across the repository (`gofmt`, `go vet`,
`golangci-lint`, `sqlc diff`, `go test -race ./...`).

**Corrections from Task 5's review** (a second pass, over commits
`20bfd53` and `70ee18b`):

1. **MEDIUM — `refuse` had no stated contract, and both ways of getting
   it wrong were unpinned.** Proved live: a `refuse` that returns `nil`
   made `Decode` return `(Cursor{}, nil)` — the zero position, which is
   the start of the listing — so a caller whose own refuse function had
   a bug would have a page-2 request silently answered with page 1,
   forever, with no error anywhere. A `nil` refuse would separately
   panic on a nil-pointer dereference the moment the caller tried to use
   the returned error. Neither was mentioned in any doc comment, and
   Task 8 is the first caller besides `internal/metamodel` that could
   get this wrong. **Decision: enforce it, deliberately, with a panic.**
   `internal/paging/cursor.go` now routes every refusal — `Malformed`'s
   and `Decode`'s fingerprint-mismatch arm, which does not go through
   `Malformed` — through one `checkedRefusal` helper that panics with a
   message naming the value if `refuse` comes back `nil`. A panic and
   not a fallback error, because there is no domain type this package
   can safely manufacture on a caller's behalf — the whole design is
   that the error is the caller's own type at the caller's own path —
   and a package whose entire job is refusing to let a bad cursor look
   like a good one must not let a broken caller-supplied function look
   like a working one either. `Malformed` and `Decode`'s doc comments
   now state the contract. `TestMalformedPanicsOnANilRefuse`,
   `TestANilRefusePanicsRatherThanReturningTheZeroPosition` and
   `TestAFingerprintMismatchWithANilRefusePanics` pin all three call
   sites.
2. **LOW — present-tense claims about a domain that does not import this
   package yet.** `internal/metamodel/list.go:149-150` said the cursor
   is "shared with the markdown domain"; `internal/metamodel/relations.go:637-640`
   said `paging.Size` is shared with it "so that none of them can drift
   apart"; `internal/paging/cursor.go`'s `Malformed` doc said `cursor` is
   the argument path "in both of Maestro's domains". All three were
   true of the design and false of the code — `internal/markdown` has no
   listing, no cursor and no import of `internal/paging` today, and
   Task 8 is what will make each sentence true. All three now say what
   is true now (only `internal/metamodel` imports `internal/paging`)
   and what Task 8 will make true, rather than asserting the future in
   the present tense.
3. **LOW — a fact lost in the move, put back.** The pre-extraction
   `cursor` comment recorded what `Sort` holds per listing: an entity
   listing's is the row's name, `ListRelations`' is its `created_at` in
   RFC 3339. `paging.Cursor`'s doc correctly generalises this away — the
   shared package has no business knowing either fact — but the
   metamodel-specific half had nowhere to live and survived only in the
   two call sites that build a `cursor{...}` literal. It is now on each
   page type: `EntityPage`'s doc comment
   (`internal/metamodel/list.go`) states `Sort` is the row's name, and
   `RelationPage`'s (`internal/metamodel/relations.go`) states it is
   `created_at` formatted with `time.RFC3339Nano` and why the nanosecond
   precision matters (two edges created in the same second still divide
   on the id tiebreak) — each pointing at the other, where a reader of
   either listing will find it.
4. **LOW — the reachable-but-untested arm, reached.**
   `internal/metamodel/relations.go:580`'s `malformedCursor("it carries
   no creation time")` had no test: reaching it needs a cursor whose
   fingerprint agrees (so it survives `paging.Decode`) but whose sort
   half is not RFC 3339 (so `ListRelations`' own `time.Parse` fails one
   step past `Decode`), which only a forged cursor can produce.
   `TestARelationsCursorWithAForgedNonTimestampSortIsMalformed`
   (`internal/metamodel/relations_test.go`) seeds one edge, takes a real
   `NextCursor` from a full page (so its fingerprint is the listing's
   actual one), decodes it, replaces the sort half with a non-timestamp
   string, re-encodes, and asserts `invalid_input` at path `cursor` with
   the exact message `is malformed (it carries no creation time): page
   from the cursor a previous call returned, or omit it to start`. The
   empty-`Sort` variant the review predicted would land the same way is
   a second subtest of the same test and does. Proved by mutation:
   short-circuiting the `time.Parse` error check (`if err != nil` →
   `if false`) reddens both subtests; the fix was then restored and the
   full suite re-run green. While there: `relations.go:576` named
   `encodeRelationCursor`, which was never a real identifier — the
   listing calls `encodeCursor`, the six-name delegation Task 5's
   Step-5 correction kept. Fixed alongside the arm the comment sits in.
5. **LOW — two coverage gaps the mutations exposed, closed.**
   `internal/paging`'s `TestTheRecoveryAdviceIsPinnedTooNotJustTheMalformedPrefix`
   now asserts the full recovery clause — "page from the cursor a
   previous call returned, or omit it to start" — and not only the "is
   malformed" prefix and the `Malformed`-equals-`Decode` equivalence
   `TestMalformedSaysTheSameThingDecodeDoes` already pinned; that
   sentence is the whole recovery advice an agent acts on and had no
   test of its own text.
   `TestTheWireNamesArePinnedHereToo` pins `Cursor`'s three JSON tags
   (`n`, `i`, `f`) from inside `internal/paging` itself, so a rename is
   red without needing `internal/metamodel`'s
   `TestACursorCarriesNothingButAPositionAndAFingerprint` — the package
   whose wire format was pinned exclusively from another package is no
   longer one deletion away from being unpinned. `Cursor`'s doc comment
   now names both tests.
6. **Recorded, not fixed — for Task 8.** `internal/paging` mandates
   "project id first" only in prose (`Fingerprint`'s doc comment) and
   mandates no domain discriminator at all. A cross-domain collision
   would need the markdown domain to reuse one of the strings
   `entities`, `related` or `relations` as a fingerprint part — unlikely,
   and unguarded either way. Task 8 must include a discriminator of its
   own in every `paging.Fingerprint(...)` call and add its own
   `TestACursorFromAnotherGameIsRefused`-equivalent, exactly as
   `Fingerprint`'s comment already instructs. Also recorded: two stale
   cross-references the implementer noticed and left rather than
   touching out of scope — Task 1's correction 2 and Task 3's
   correction 11 (both in this same plan file) still say "Task 5" for
   what landed as Task 3's `oversizeForIndex` and Task 6's `Revert`
   respectively. Neither reference is corrected here; this entry is the
   record that they are known and where they point.

Verification for this pass: baseline `go test ./internal/metamodel/ -v

### Task 6: History, read_version, revert and diff

**Files:**
- Modify: `internal/db/queries/documents.sql`
- Create: `internal/markdown/versions.go`
- Create: `internal/markdown/diff.go`
- Create: `internal/markdown/versions_test.go`
- Create: `internal/markdown/diff_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/markdown/events.go`
- Modify: `internal/markdown/delete_test.go` (replace Task 4's placeholder)

This is the task that makes "nothing is ever lost" true rather than
claimed: until a caller can read a past version back, a history table is
a write-only log.

- [ ] **Step 1: Add the queries**

Append to `internal/db/queries/documents.sql`:

```sql
-- name: ListDocumentVersions :many
-- Version metadata only, newest first. **No bodies**: prose is the
-- largest payload in the system and agents are its main consumer, so a
-- history that carried them would blow a context window on the first
-- call against a document anyone has actually worked on (spec §7).
--
-- Keyset by version alone, which is unique within a document
-- (document_versions_key), so there is no tiebreak to add and the page
-- is an index scan. The project filter is load-bearing here and is not
-- defence in depth: this is one of the two calls the spec names as
-- being one forgotten join away from serving another game's prose, and
-- it deliberately does not reach the parent document at all.
SELECT id, project_id, document_id, version, title, summary, message, deleted,
       author_user_id, author_token_id, created_at
FROM document_versions
WHERE project_id = sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid
  AND document_id = sqlc.arg('document_id')::uuid
  AND (sqlc.narg('after_version')::integer IS NULL
       OR version < sqlc.narg('after_version')::integer)
ORDER BY version DESC
LIMIT sqlc.arg('limit')::int;

-- name: GetDocumentVersion :one
-- The other call the spec names. Same rule: filtered by project_id and
-- document_id, never by version alone and never by a join.
SELECT * FROM document_versions
WHERE project_id = sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid
  AND document_id = sqlc.arg('document_id')::uuid
  AND version = sqlc.arg('version')::integer;
```

Run `make sqlc && make sqlc-check`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Add the event kind**

In `internal/markdown/events.go`:

```go
	// eventDocumentReverted fires from Revert once its transaction has
	// committed, with documentEventMinRole / documentEventHumanOnly —
	// the pair eventDocumentWritten's comment argues.
	//
	// It is a separate kind from document.written even though a revert
	// *is* a write, and the reason is what a subscriber does with it: a
	// browser showing "an agent rewrote this" and a browser showing "Ana
	// reverted this to version 7" are two different sentences, and a
	// client that had to infer the second from the first would have to
	// fetch the history to find out. The payload carries the version
	// that was restored as well as the new one, which is the whole
	// difference.
	eventDocumentReverted = "document.reverted"
```

and, beside `DocumentEvent`:

```go
// RevertEvent is document.reverted's payload: identity, the new version,
// and the version whose content was restored.
type RevertEvent struct {
	ID          uuid.UUID `json:"id"`
	Path        string    `json:"path"`
	Version     int32     `json:"version"`
	FromVersion int32     `json:"from_version"`
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the failing tests**

`internal/markdown/versions_test.go`:

```go
package markdown_test

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/markdown"
)

// writeVersions writes n successive versions of one document, whose
// bodies are "v1\n", "v2\n" and so on, and returns the game.
func writeVersions(t *testing.T, svc *markdown.Service, game uuid.UUID, path string, n int32) {
	t.Helper()
	ctx := context.Background()
	for v := int32(0); v < n; v++ {
		if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
			Path:            path,
			Content:         fmt.Sprintf("v%d\n", v+1),
			Message:         fmt.Sprintf("edit %d", v+1),
			ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(v),
		}); err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("write version %d: %v", v+1, err)
		}
	}
}

func TestHistoryIsNewestFirstAndCarriesNoBodies(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "bible", 3)

	page, err := svc.History(ctx, game, markdown.HistoryFilter{Path: "bible"})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("History: %v", err)
	}
	if len(page.Versions) != 3 {
		t.Fatalf("%d versions, want 3", len(page.Versions))
	}
	if page.Versions[0].Version != 3 || page.Versions[2].Version != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("versions came back %d…%d, want newest first",
			page.Versions[0].Version, page.Versions[2].Version)
	}
	if page.Versions[0].Message != "edit 3" {
		t.Fatalf("Message = %q, want %q", page.Versions[0].Message, "edit 3")
	}
	if page.NextCursor != "" {
		t.Fatalf("NextCursor = %q, want empty: the page was not full", page.NextCursor)
	}
}

func TestHistoryPagesWithACursorBelongingToItsOwnDocument(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "bible", 5)
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "tone", 5)

	first, err := svc.History(ctx, game, markdown.HistoryFilter{Path: "bible", Limit: 2})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("History: %v", err)
	}
	if len(first.Versions) != 2 || first.NextCursor == "" {
		t.Fatalf("first page = %d versions with cursor %q, want 2 and a cursor",
			len(first.Versions), first.NextCursor)
	}
	second, err := svc.History(ctx, game, markdown.HistoryFilter{
		Path: "bible", Limit: 2, Cursor: first.NextCursor,
	})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("second page: %v", err)
	}
	if second.Versions[0].Version != 3 {
		t.Fatalf("second page starts at version %d, want 3", second.Versions[0].Version)
	}

	// A cursor belongs to the listing that issued it and to no other.
	_, err = svc.History(ctx, game, markdown.HistoryFilter{
		Path: "tone", Limit: 2, Cursor: first.NextCursor,
	})
	requireFieldError(t, err, "cursor", "different listing")
}

func TestACursorFromAnotherGamesHistoryIsRefused(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	azeroth := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	outland := newGame(t, pool, "outland")
	writeVersions(t, svc, azeroth, "bible", 5)
	writeVersions(t, svc, outland, "bible", 5)

	first, err := svc.History(ctx, azeroth, markdown.HistoryFilter{Path: "bible", Limit: 2})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("History: %v", err)
	}
	_, err = svc.History(ctx, outland, markdown.HistoryFilter{
		Path: "bible", Limit: 2, Cursor: first.NextCursor,
	})
	requireFieldError(t, err, "cursor", "different listing")
}

func TestHistoryOfADeletedDocumentIsStillReadable(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "bible", 1)
	if err := svc.Delete(ctx, game, markdown.DeleteInput{
		Path: "bible", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(1), Message: "cut",
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Delete: %v", err)
	}

	page, err := svc.History(ctx, game, markdown.HistoryFilter{Path: "bible"})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("History of a deleted document: %v", err)
	}
	if len(page.Versions) != 2 || !page.Versions[0].Deleted {
		t.Fatalf("history = %+v, want two versions with the newest marked deleted", page.Versions)
	}
}

func TestReadVersionReturnsTheBodyAsItStood(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "bible", 3)

	v2, err := svc.ReadVersion(ctx, game, "bible", 2)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("ReadVersion: %v", err)
	}
	if v2.BodyMd != "v2\n" {
		t.Fatalf("BodyMd = %q, want %q", v2.BodyMd, "v2\n")
	}
	// The current document is untouched by a read.
	current, err := svc.Read(ctx, game, "bible")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Read: %v", err)
	}
	if current.BodyMd != "v3\n" {
		t.Fatalf("BodyMd = %q, want %q", current.BodyMd, "v3\n")
	}
}

func TestReadingAVersionThatDoesNotExistNamesTheArgument(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "bible", 2)

	_, err := svc.ReadVersion(ctx, game, "bible", 9)
	if !errors.Is(err, markdown.ErrNotFound) {
		t.Fatalf("want not_found, got %v", err)
	}
	var missing *markdown.MissingError
	if !errors.As(err, &missing) || missing.Path != "version" {
		t.Fatalf("want a MissingError at path version, got %#v", err)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(missing.Message, "9") {
		t.Fatalf("message %q must name the version asked for", missing.Message)
	}
}

func TestReadingAnotherGamesVersionIsNotFound(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	azeroth := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	outland := newGame(t, pool, "outland")
	writeVersions(t, svc, azeroth, "bible", 2)

	if _, err := svc.ReadVersion(ctx, outland, "bible", 1); !errors.Is(err, markdown.ErrNotFound) {
		t.Fatalf("a token for outland must not read azeroth's version, got %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := svc.History(ctx, outland, markdown.HistoryFilter{Path: "bible"}); !errors.Is(err, markdown.ErrNotFound) {
		t.Fatalf("nor its history, got %v", err)
	}
}

func TestRevertWritesForwardRatherThanRewritingHistory(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "bible", 3)

	doc, err := svc.Revert(ctx, game, markdown.RevertInput{
		Path: "bible", ToVersion: 1, ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(3),
	})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Revert: %v", err)
	}
	if doc.CurrentVersion != 4 {
		t.Fatalf("CurrentVersion = %d, want 4: a revert appends", doc.CurrentVersion)
	}
	if doc.BodyMd != "v1\n" {
		t.Fatalf("BodyMd = %q, want %q", doc.BodyMd, "v1\n")
	}

	// There is no state in which version 3 exists and version 2 does not.
	page, err := svc.History(ctx, game, markdown.HistoryFilter{Path: "bible"})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("History: %v", err)
	}
	if len(page.Versions) != 4 {
		t.Fatalf("%d versions, want 4", len(page.Versions))
	}
	if page.Versions[0].Message != "reverted to version 1" {
		t.Fatalf("Message = %q, want the automatic message", page.Versions[0].Message)
	}
}

func TestRevertKeepsTheStoredTitleRatherThanDerivingItAgain(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "---\ntitle: The Old Name\n---\nbody\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: "# A New Name\n\nbody\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(1),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("second write: %v", err)
	}
	doc, err := svc.Revert(ctx, game, markdown.RevertInput{
		Path: "bible", ToVersion: 1, ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(2),
	})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Revert: %v", err)
	}
	// Restoring means restoring what was stored, not re-running today's
	// derivation rules over yesterday's body.
	if doc.Title != "The Old Name" {
		t.Fatalf("Title = %q, want the stored title of version 1", doc.Title)
	}
}

func TestRevertingToAMissingVersionNamesTheArgument(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "bible", 2)

	_, err := svc.Revert(ctx, game, markdown.RevertInput{
		Path: "bible", ToVersion: 9, ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(2),
	})
	var missing *markdown.MissingError
	if !errors.As(err, &missing) || missing.Path != "to_version" {
		t.Fatalf("want a MissingError at path to_version, got %#v", err)
	}
	// Nothing was written.
	page, _ := svc.History(ctx, game, markdown.HistoryFilter{Path: "bible"})
	if len(page.Versions) != 2 {
		t.Fatalf("%d versions after a refused revert, want 2", len(page.Versions))
	}
}

func TestRevertingWithAStaleVersionIsAConflict(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "bible", 3)

	_, err := svc.Revert(ctx, game, markdown.RevertInput{
		Path: "bible", ToVersion: 1, ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(2), IncludeCurrent: true,
	})
	var conflict *markdown.ConflictError
	if !errors.As(err, &conflict) || conflict.Current != 3 || conflict.BodyMD != "v3\n" {
		t.Fatalf("want a conflict at version 3 carrying v3, got %#v", err)
	}
}

func TestRevertIsAnnouncedWithTheVersionItRestored(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, hub, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "bible", 2)

	sub := hub.Subscribe(game, "viewer", false)
	defer sub.Close()

	if _, err := svc.Revert(ctx, game, markdown.RevertInput{
		Path: "bible", ToVersion: 1, ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(2),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Revert: %v", err)
	}
	ev := <-sub.Events()
	if ev.Kind != "document.reverted" {
		t.Fatalf("Kind = %q, want %q", ev.Kind, "document.reverted")
	}
	payload, ok := ev.Payload.(markdown.RevertEvent)
	if !ok || payload.Version != 3 || payload.FromVersion != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("payload = %#v, want version 3 restored from 1", ev.Payload)
	}
}
```

`internal/markdown/versions.go`:

```go
package markdown

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"strconv"

	"github.com/google/uuid"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/db/dbq"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/metamodel"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/paging"
)

// The bounds on one page of history.
//
// Exported for the rule that a bound a caller cannot read is a bound a
// caller trips over; the docs.history tool description is built with
// these values interpolated rather than typed out.
const (
	defaultHistoryPage int32 = 50
	maxHistoryPage     int32 = 200

	// DefaultHistoryPage and MaxHistoryPage bound one page of History.
	DefaultHistoryPage = defaultHistoryPage
	MaxHistoryPage     = maxHistoryPage
)

// HistoryFilter selects one document's history.
//
// Cursor is the NextCursor of a previous call. It belongs to the game
// and the document it was issued for and to no other; HistoryPage
// carries the contract.
type HistoryFilter struct {
	Path   string
	Cursor string
	Limit  int32
}

// HistoryPage is one page of version metadata plus the cursor for the
// next.
//
// **NextCursor is set when the page came back full**, and empty
// otherwise, so a caller looping until it is empty is correct and must
// expect a final empty page rather than treating one as an error. The
// rest of the cursor contract — that it is a position and not a
// snapshot, that it cannot be carried to another listing, and that it is
// not signed — is paging.Cursor's, and it is worth reading before paging
// a document that is being edited.
//
// **This is the one listing in Maestro whose sort key is immutable.**
// A version's number never changes and a version row is never updated,
// so the "a row edited to sort before the position is never seen again"
// hazard paging.Cursor documents cannot arise here. Versions are only
// ever appended, at the *newest* end, which the page walks away from.
type HistoryPage struct {
	Versions   []dbq.ListDocumentVersionsRow
	NextCursor string
}

// History returns one page of a document's version metadata, newest
// first.
//
// **No bodies.** A version's body is returned by ReadVersion and by Diff
// and by nothing else (spec §7): prose is the largest payload in the
// system, and a history that carried every body would blow an agent's
// context window on the first call against a document anyone has
// actually worked on.
//
// The history of a deleted document is readable. Deletion is soft
// precisely so that the record survives, and "what did this say before
// someone cut it" is the first question anyone asks.
func (s *Service) History(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, f HistoryFilter) (HistoryPage, error) {
	doc, err := s.documentForVersions(ctx, projectID, f.Path)
	if err != nil {
		return HistoryPage{}, err
	}
	limit := paging.Size(f.Limit, defaultHistoryPage, maxHistoryPage)

	// The project id is the first part, always: without it two games'
	// histories of the same path share a fingerprint and one game's
	// cursor pages the other's versions. The document id is resolved,
	// not the path as spelled, so two spellings of one path give one
	// fingerprint.
	fingerprint := paging.Fingerprint(projectID.String(), "document_versions", doc.ID.String())
	after, err := paging.Decode(f.Cursor, fingerprint, refuseCursor)
	if err != nil {
		return HistoryPage{}, err
	}

	params := dbq.ListDocumentVersionsParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, DocumentID: doc.ID, Limit: limit,
	}
	if after.ID != uuid.Nil {
		version, err := strconv.ParseInt(after.Sort, 10, 32)
		if err != nil {
			return HistoryPage{}, refuseCursor(
				"is malformed (its position is not a version number): page from the cursor " +
					"a previous call returned, or omit it to start")
		}
		v := int32(version)
		params.AfterVersion = &v
	}

	rows, err := s.q.ListDocumentVersions(ctx, params)
	if err != nil {
		return HistoryPage{}, fmt.Errorf("list document versions: %w", err)
	}
	page := HistoryPage{Versions: rows}
	if len(rows) == int(limit) {
		last := rows[len(rows)-1]
		page.NextCursor = paging.Encode(paging.Cursor{
			Sort:        strconv.FormatInt(int64(last.Version), 10),
			ID:          last.ID,
			Fingerprint: fingerprint,
		})
	}
	return page, nil
}

// ReadVersion returns one past version in full, body included.
func (s *Service) ReadVersion(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, path string, version int32) (dbq.DocumentVersion, error) {
	doc, err := s.documentForVersions(ctx, projectID, path)
	if err != nil {
		return dbq.DocumentVersion{}, err
	}
	row, err := s.q.GetDocumentVersion(ctx, dbq.GetDocumentVersionParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, DocumentID: doc.ID, Version: version,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return dbq.DocumentVersion{}, notFound(err, missingVersion("version", path, version),
			"read document version")
	}
	return row, nil
}

// documentForVersions resolves a path to the document its versions hang
// from, deleted documents included.
//
// It is the one place the version calls touch `documents`, and it does
// not establish their scope: ListDocumentVersions and GetDocumentVersion
// filter on their own project_id, denormalised for exactly this reason
// (spec §5). What this resolves is the *identity* — which document — and
// the project filter here is what keeps a path from naming another
// game's document in the first place.
func (s *Service) documentForVersions(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, path string) (dbq.Document, error) {
	if err := CheckPath(path); err != nil {
		return dbq.Document{}, err
	}
	row, err := s.q.GetDocumentByPath(ctx, dbq.GetDocumentByPathParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, Path: path, IncludeDeleted: true,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return dbq.Document{}, notFound(err, missingDocument(path), "resolve document")
	}
	return row, nil
}

// refuseCursor turns internal/paging's message into this package's own
// error, at the argument's own path.
//
// The messages live in internal/paging so that this domain and the
// metamodel cannot tell a caller two different things about one bad
// cursor; the *type* is this domain's, because that is what
// internal/web's existing invalid_input arm matches on. It is declared
// here, beside the first listing that pages, and **Task 8's document
// listing reuses it** rather than declaring a second one.
func refuseCursor(message string) error { return invalidInput("cursor", message) }

// missingVersion names both the document and the version asked for, at
// the argument's own path — `version` for a read, `to_version` for a
// revert — so a caller that mistyped one of two numbers is told which.
func missingVersion(path, docPath string, version int32) error {
	return &MissingError{
		Path:    path,
		Message: fmt.Sprintf("the document at %q has no version %d", docPath, version),
	}
}

// RevertInput restores a past version's content as a new version.
//
// There is no Kind: `kind` is a property of the document and is not
// versioned, so a revert leaves it alone. Restoring a grouping label
// alongside the prose would be a second, unannounced change. Task 3's
// review closed the bug that made this comment aspirational rather than
// true — `writeWith`'s call below no longer needs a `Kind: existing.Kind`
// workaround, because `WriteInput.Kind` is now a `*string` that leaves
// the stored kind alone when nil, and this call simply never sets it.
type RevertInput struct {
	Path            string
	ToVersion       int32
	ExpectedVersion *int32
	Message         string
	IncludeCurrent  bool
	Actor           Actor
}

// Revert writes forward: it creates a *new* version whose content equals
// ToVersion's, with an automatic message when the caller gives none.
//
// **History is append-only.** There is no state in which version 9
// exists and version 8 does not, which is what lets a designer trust
// that the record of the writing is the record of the writing.
//
// **The restored content is the content that was stored**, not the
// content re-derived from the old body: title, summary and frontmatter
// come off the version row verbatim. Re-running today's derivation rules
// over yesterday's body would mean "revert" restored something that was
// never there the day the derivation changed.
//
// Reverting to a tombstone version restores that version's body and
// leaves the document alive; it does not re-delete it. A revert is a
// content operation, and a caller that wants the document gone says so
// with delete — where the expected version, and therefore the refusal if
// someone else has since written, is its own.
func (s *Service) Revert(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, in RevertInput) (dbq.Document, error) {
	problems := pathProblems(in.Path)
	problems = append(problems, checkShortText("message", in.Message, MaxMessageLen)...)
	if in.ExpectedVersion == nil {
		problems = append(problems, metamodel.FieldError{
			Path:    "expected_version",
			Message: "is required: pass the version you read, so a revert cannot race an edit",
		})
	}
	if in.ToVersion < 1 {
		problems = append(problems, metamodel.FieldError{
			Path:    "to_version",
			Message: "must be 1 or greater: versions are numbered from 1",
		})
	}
	if len(problems) > 0 {
		return dbq.Document{}, invalidInputProblems(problems)
	}

	message := in.Message
	if message == "" {
		message = fmt.Sprintf("reverted to version %d", in.ToVersion)
	}

	var (
		written dbq.Document
		from    int32
	)
	err := s.withTx(ctx, func(q *dbq.Queries) error {
		existing, err := q.GetDocumentByPathForUpdate(ctx, dbq.GetDocumentByPathForUpdateParams{
			ProjectID: projectID, Path: in.Path,
		})
		if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) {
			return missingDocument(in.Path)
		}
		if err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("lock document: %w", err)
		}
		if existing.Path != in.Path {
			return pathRespellingError(in.Path, existing.Path)
		}
		if *in.ExpectedVersion != existing.CurrentVersion {
			return conflictOn(existing, in.IncludeCurrent)
		}

		target, err := q.GetDocumentVersion(ctx, dbq.GetDocumentVersionParams{
			ProjectID: projectID, DocumentID: existing.ID, Version: in.ToVersion,
		})
		if err != nil {
			return notFound(err, missingVersion("to_version", in.Path, in.ToVersion),
				"read the version to revert to")
		}
		from = target.Version

		written, err = s.writeWith(ctx, q, projectID, WriteInput{
			Path:            in.Path,
			Message:         message,
			ExpectedVersion: in.ExpectedVersion,
			IncludeCurrent:  in.IncludeCurrent,
			Actor:           in.Actor,
		}, Content{
			Title:           target.Title,
			Summary:         target.Summary,
			Body:            target.BodyMd,
			FrontmatterJSON: target.Frontmatter,
		})
		return err
	})
	if err != nil {
		return dbq.Document{}, err
	}
	s.publish(projectID, eventDocumentReverted, documentEventMinRole, documentEventHumanOnly,
		RevertEvent{ID: written.ID, Path: written.Path, Version: written.CurrentVersion, FromVersion: from})
	return written, nil
}
```

`writeWith` (Task 3) takes its `Content` from the caller, which is what
makes this reuse possible without re-deriving anything — **that is why
`SplitContent` runs in `Write` and not in `writeWith`.** If a later
change moves the split inside, revert silently starts re-deriving and
`TestRevertKeepsTheStoredTitleRatherThanDerivingItAgain` is what goes
red.

- [ ] **Step 7: Write the diff**

`internal/markdown/diff.go`:

```go
package markdown

import (
	"fmt"
	"strings"
)

// Diffs are computed on read and never stored (spec §3). Snapshots are
// full, so a diff is always available and never on the critical path of
// a write; storing them would make every past version depend on every
// link of a chain being right.

// lcsLimit bounds the dynamic-programming table one diff will build,
// per side, after common prefixes and suffixes have been trimmed.
//
// The table is O(n*m), so two 4,000-line halves cost 16 million cells —
// tens of megabytes and a fraction of a second, which is acceptable for
// a call a human makes by clicking "compare". A 1 MiB body is around
// 20,000 lines, and 20,000 squared is not acceptable for anything. Past
// the limit the answer is one coarse hunk saying the whole body was
// replaced, and DiffResult.Coarse says so out loud rather than
// pretending the line-by-line answer was computed.
const lcsLimit = 4000

// DiffResult is one comparison between two versions of a document.
//
// **Coarse is not decoration.** A caller that renders Unified without
// reading it would show "the whole document was replaced" for two
// versions that differ by a word, and blame the author. It is on the
// wire for the same reason SearchHit.NameMatch is: a client cannot
// reconstruct from the diff itself why the diff looks like that.
type DiffResult struct {
	Path        string
	FromVersion int32
	ToVersion   int32
	Unified     string
	Coarse      bool
}

// Diff compares two versions of one document and returns a unified diff.
//
// The versions may be given in either order; the diff reads from the
// first to the second, so `from: 3, to: 1` is the reverse of
// `from: 1, to: 3` and both are legitimate questions.
func (s *Service) Diff(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, path string, from, to int32) (DiffResult, error) {
	fromRow, err := s.ReadVersion(ctx, projectID, path, from)
	if err != nil {
		return DiffResult{}, versionArgument(err, "from_version")
	}
	toRow, err := s.ReadVersion(ctx, projectID, path, to)
	if err != nil {
		return DiffResult{}, versionArgument(err, "to_version")
	}
	unified, coarse := unifiedDiff(
		fmt.Sprintf("%s@%d", path, from), fmt.Sprintf("%s@%d", path, to),
		fromRow.BodyMd, toRow.BodyMd)
	return DiffResult{
		Path: path, FromVersion: from, ToVersion: to, Unified: unified, Coarse: coarse,
	}, nil
}

// versionArgument re-points a MissingError at the argument the caller
// actually sent. ReadVersion reports at `version`, which is right for
// its own tool and wrong here, where there are two of them and a caller
// that mistyped one needs to know which.
func versionArgument(err error, path string) error {
	var missing *MissingError
	if errors.As(err, &missing) && missing.Path == "version" {
		return &MissingError{Path: path, Message: missing.Message}
	}
	return err
}

// unifiedDiff renders a line diff in unified format with three lines of
// context, and reports whether it had to fall back to a coarse answer.
func unifiedDiff(fromName, toName, fromBody, toBody string) (string, bool) {
	from := splitLines(fromBody)
	to := splitLines(toBody)

	// Trim the common head and tail first: two versions of a long
	// document usually differ in one place, so this is what keeps the
	// table small enough to build at all.
	head := 0
	for head < len(from) && head < len(to) && from[head] == to[head] {
		head++
	}
	tail := 0
	for tail < len(from)-head && tail < len(to)-head &&
		from[len(from)-1-tail] == to[len(to)-1-tail] {
		tail++
	}
	midFrom := from[head : len(from)-tail]
	midTo := to[head : len(to)-tail]

	var b strings.Builder
	fmt.Fprintf(&b, "--- %s\n+++ %s\n", fromName, toName)
	if len(midFrom) == 0 && len(midTo) == 0 {
		return b.String(), false
	}
	if len(midFrom) > lcsLimit || len(midTo) > lcsLimit {
		fmt.Fprintf(&b, "@@ -%d,%d +%d,%d @@\n", head+1, len(midFrom), head+1, len(midTo))
		for _, line := range midFrom {
			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "-%s\n", line)
		}
		for _, line := range midTo {
			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "+%s\n", line)
		}
		return b.String(), true
	}

	ops := diffOps(midFrom, midTo)
	writeHunks(&b, ops, head)
	return b.String(), false
}

// op is one line of the diff: ' ' kept, '-' removed, '+' added.
type op struct {
	kind byte
	line string
}

// diffOps is the classic LCS table, walked back into a line script.
func diffOps(from, to []string) []op {
	n, m := len(from), len(to)
	table := make([][]int32, n+1)
	for i := range table {
		table[i] = make([]int32, m+1)
	}
	for i := n - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
		for j := m - 1; j >= 0; j-- {
			if from[i] == to[j] {
				table[i][j] = table[i+1][j+1] + 1
			} else if table[i+1][j] >= table[i][j+1] {
				table[i][j] = table[i+1][j]
			} else {
				table[i][j] = table[i][j+1]
			}
		}
	}
	var ops []op
	i, j := 0, 0
	for i < n && j < m {
		switch {
		case from[i] == to[j]:
			ops = append(ops, op{' ', from[i]})
			i, j = i+1, j+1
		case table[i+1][j] >= table[i][j+1]:
			ops = append(ops, op{'-', from[i]})
			i++
		default:
			ops = append(ops, op{'+', to[j]})
			j++
		}
	}
	for ; i < n; i++ {
		ops = append(ops, op{'-', from[i]})
	}
	for ; j < m; j++ {
		ops = append(ops, op{'+', to[j]})
	}
	return ops
}

// writeHunks groups a line script into unified hunks with three lines of
// context, offset by the common head the caller trimmed.
func writeHunks(b *strings.Builder, ops []op, offset int) {
	const context = 3
	keep := make([]bool, len(ops))
	for i, o := range ops {
		if o.kind == ' ' {
			continue
		}
		for j := max(0, i-context); j <= min(len(ops)-1, i+context); j++ {
			keep[j] = true
		}
	}
	fromLine, toLine := offset+1, offset+1
	for i := 0; i < len(ops); {
		if !keep[i] {
			if ops[i].kind != '+' {
				fromLine++
			}
			if ops[i].kind != '-' {
				toLine++
			}
			i++
			continue
		}
		start := i
		startFrom, startTo := fromLine, toLine
		var fromCount, toCount int
		for i < len(ops) && keep[i] {
			if ops[i].kind != '+' {
				fromLine++
				fromCount++
			}
			if ops[i].kind != '-' {
				toLine++
				toCount++
			}
			i++
		}
		fmt.Fprintf(b, "@@ -%d,%d +%d,%d @@\n", startFrom, fromCount, startTo, toCount)
		for _, o := range ops[start:i] {
			fmt.Fprintf(b, "%c%s\n", o.kind, o.line)
		}
	}
}

// splitLines splits a body into lines, dropping the empty element a
// trailing newline produces so that a body ending in "\n" and one that
// does not are not reported as differing by a phantom line.
func splitLines(body string) []string {
	if body == "" {
		return nil
	}
	lines := strings.Split(strings.TrimSuffix(body, "\n"), "\n")
	return lines
}
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Write the diff tests**

`internal/markdown/diff_test.go`:

```go
package markdown_test

import (
	"context"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/markdown"
)

func TestADiffShowsOnlyWhatChanged(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	before := "one\ntwo\nthree\nfour\nfive\nsix\nseven\neight\nnine\nten\n"
	after := "one\ntwo\nthree\nfour\nFIVE\nsix\nseven\neight\nnine\nten\n"
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: before, ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: after, ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(1),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("second write: %v", err)
	}

	got, err := svc.Diff(ctx, game, "bible", 1, 2)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Diff: %v", err)
	}
	if got.Coarse {
		t.Fatal("a one-line change must not come back coarse")
	}
	for _, want := range []string{"-five", "+FIVE", "@@ "} {
		if !strings.Contains(got.Unified, want) {
			t.Fatalf("diff %q is missing %q", got.Unified, want)
		}
	}
	// Three lines of context each side, and nothing beyond it.
	if strings.Contains(got.Unified, "one") {
		t.Fatalf("diff %q reaches past three lines of context", got.Unified)
	}
}

func TestDiffingAVersionAgainstItselfIsEmpty(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "bible", 2)

	got, err := svc.Diff(ctx, game, "bible", 2, 2)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Diff: %v", err)
	}
	if strings.Contains(got.Unified, "@@") {
		t.Fatalf("diff = %q, want no hunks", got.Unified)
	}
}

func TestDiffReadsInTheDirectionItWasAsked(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "bible", 2)

	forward, err := svc.Diff(ctx, game, "bible", 1, 2)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Diff: %v", err)
	}
	backward, err := svc.Diff(ctx, game, "bible", 2, 1)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Diff: %v", err)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(forward.Unified, "+v2") || !strings.Contains(backward.Unified, "+v1") {
		t.Fatalf("forward = %q, backward = %q: the direction must follow the arguments",
			forward.Unified, backward.Unified)
	}
}

func TestAHugeChangeComesBackCoarseAndSaysSo(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")

	before := strings.Repeat("a\n", 5000)
	after := strings.Repeat("b\n", 5000)
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: before, ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "bible", Content: after, ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(1),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("second write: %v", err)
	}

	got, err := svc.Diff(ctx, game, "bible", 1, 2)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Diff: %v", err)
	}
	if !got.Coarse {
		t.Fatal("a 5,000-line rewrite must report itself coarse rather than pretending")
	}
}

func TestDiffNamesWhichVersionArgumentWasWrong(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	writeVersions(t, svc, game, "bible", 2)

	_, err := svc.Diff(ctx, game, "bible", 1, 9)
	var missing *markdown.MissingError
	if !errorsAs(err, &missing) || missing.Path != "to_version" {
		t.Fatalf("want a MissingError at to_version, got %#v", err)
	}
	_, err = svc.Diff(ctx, game, "bible", 9, 1)
	if !errorsAs(err, &missing) || missing.Path != "from_version" {
		t.Fatalf("want a MissingError at from_version, got %#v", err)
	}
}
```
