**Corrections from implementing Task 6** (what the plan's code blocks got
wrong, and what the landed code does instead):

1. **The plan's Step 10 mutation table was wrong about the project
   filter on both version queries, and right about nothing that
   replaced it.** It predicted that dropping the `project_id` filter
   from `GetDocumentVersion` or from `ListDocumentVersions` would redden
   `TestReadingAnotherGamesVersionIsNotFound`. It does not, and the
   reason is structural: `documentForVersions` resolves the path inside
   the caller's own game first, so a token for outland never obtains
   azeroth's `document_id` at all, and the queries are then filtered by
   that id. Verified by mutation, twice (both filters neutralised behind
   a no-op `OR TRUE`, the whole package green each time). **No call this
   package offers can make either filter matter**, because
   `0007_documents.sql`'s composite key additionally makes a version row
   whose `project_id` disagrees with its document's unrepresentable.
   The filters stay -- Task 11's REST mirror may address a document by
   id, where the resolution is not in the path -- and both
   `documentForVersions`' doc comment and `documents.sql`'s header now
   say exactly this, rather than naming a test that would not go red.
   What *is* pinned instead: `TestAVersionIsAddressedByItsOwnDocument`
   (two documents inside one game -- the project filter separates
   nothing there, and dropping either query's `document_id` filter
   reddens it) and
   `TestAVersionRowCannotClaimAGameItsDocumentDoesNotBelongTo`, which
   forces the composite key's refusal from this package rather than only
   from `internal/db`.

2. **The plan's `unifiedDiff` produced diffs with no context at all**,
   and its own test could not see it. Trimming the common prefix and
   suffix down to the first and last difference removes exactly the
   lines the unified format exists to show: for the plan's
   `TestADiffShowsOnlyWhatChanged` fixture the whole answer was
   `@@ -5,1 +5,1 @@`, `-five`, `+FIVE`, and the test's "does not contain
   `one`" assertion passed *because there was no context*, not because
   the context was bounded at three lines. The trim now backs off by
   `diffContext` on each side, so the head and tail keep three common
   lines, and the test asserts the hunk header (`@@ -2,7 +2,7 @@`) as
   well as the absence -- an assertion that is red both when the context
   is missing and when it is too wide. Both mutations are red: the
   backoff removed, and `diffContext` widened to 30.

3. **`lcsLimit`'s cost is measured, and the number is worse than the
   plan's guess in the dimension that matters.** `BenchmarkUnifiedDiff`
   (added, `diff_internal_test.go`, Apple M1): 4,000 lines rewritten on
   both sides is **49 ms and 66.7 MB of allocation** for one call -- the
   plan said "tens of megabytes and a fraction of a second", which is
   right about the time and understates the memory. One line past the
   bound the coarse fallback is 0.5 ms and 0.37 MB, and a one-line edit
   inside a 40,000-line document is 1.1 ms and 1.3 MB, almost all of it
   splitting lines rather than diffing. The bound stays at 4,000 --
   20,000 per side is 25x the cells, 1.6 GB and over a second -- and
   `lcsLimit`'s comment now carries the real numbers plus the thing the
   plan did not say out loud: **nothing bounds how many diffs run at
   once**, so an instance's honest worst case is 66.7 MB times the
   number of simultaneous comparison requests, and the fix if it ever
   bites is a semaphore around the call, not a smaller table.

4. **`TestACursorFromAnotherGamesHistoryIsRefused` passes for the wrong
   reason, and the composition it was supposed to pin now has its own
   test.** Task 5's review instructed this task to write the
   markdown-domain equivalent of
   `TestACursorFromAnotherGameIsRefused`; it is written and it is green,
   but dropping `projectID.String()` from the fingerprint leaves it
   green too. The document id already discriminates between games --
   two games' documents at one path are two different rows -- so in
   *this* listing the project id is redundant, which is precisely the
   shape a behaviour test cannot pin. The fingerprint is extracted as
   `historyFingerprint(projectID, documentID)` and
   `TestTheHistoryFingerprintLeadsWithTheProjectId`
   (`versions_internal_test.go`) asserts the composition itself: the
   exact `paging.Fingerprint` call, that each of the three parts moves
   the answer, and that the domain discriminator is really there.
   Dropping the project id and dropping `"document_versions"` are both
   red against it. The rule stops being redundant the moment this
   listing grows a filter not derived from a per-game row, and the
   comment says so instead of claiming the behaviour test covers it.

5. **The publish-placement gap this package has now found three times.**
   `TestRevertIsAnnouncedWithTheVersionItRestored` stays green with the
   publish moved inside `withTx` (verified), exactly as Task 3's
   correction 4 found for `Write` and Task 4's for `Delete`, and the
   plan's Step 10 predicted it and proposed a rollback assertion --
   which does not close it either, for the same reason Task 4 recorded:
   a refused revert returns before the publish wherever the call sits.
   Both tests are kept and a third added:
   `TestNoRevertIsAnnouncedWhenTheRevertIsRefused` (the rollback half,
   the plan's suggestion) and
   `TestNoRevertIsAnnouncedWhenTheRevertCannotCommit`, which reuses
   `TestNoDeletionIsAnnouncedWhenTheDeleteCannotCommit`'s deferred
   `NOT VALID` foreign key unchanged and is the only one of the three
   that goes red against the moved publish.

6. **Two of the plan's Step 10 mutations were invalid as written, and
   the valid forms are recorded so the next reader does not re-run
   them.** "Pass `SplitContent(target.BodyMd)`'s result instead of the
   stored fields" needs a helper to compile (`SplitContent` returns two
   values); with one, it reddens
   `TestRevertKeepsTheStoredTitleRatherThanDerivingItAgain` as
   predicted. "Move the `GetDocumentVersion` read after `writeWith`"
   leaves `from` unused and does not build unless the read's result is
   still consumed; restructured so it does, it reddens
   `TestRevertingToAMissingVersionNamesTheArgument`'s
   "nothing was written" half. Also recorded: `Kind: &existing.Kind` is
   **not** a mutation of "revert leaves the kind alone" -- it restores
   the value that is already there and stays green. `Kind: new(string)`
   is the one that reddens `TestRevertingLeavesTheDocumentsKindAlone`.

7. **`Revert`'s own expected-version check is behaviourally redundant,
   like the two before it, and now says so.** Neutralising it leaves the
   suite green: `writeWith` compares again under the same lock and
   `UpsertDocument`'s SQL guard refuses regardless. It is kept for the
   reason `writeWith`'s identical check and `GetDocumentByPathForUpdate`'s
   `FOR UPDATE` are kept -- a caller already known to be wrong is turned
   away before the version it asked to restore is even read -- and the
   comment states it, so this file applies one standard to all three
   guards rather than arguing for two of them and leaving the third
   bare. That is Task 3's correction 13, one function along.

8. **The deferred assertions are paid, and all of them moved.** Task 3's
   `TestTheFirstWriteAlsoWritesVersionOneWithItsAuthorAndMessage` and
   Task 4's `TestATombstonesBodyIsStillReadableAsAVersion` and
   `TestOnlyTheTombstoneVersionIsMarkedDeleted` no longer touch the
   database directly: the version's `message`, `title`, `summary`,
   author columns, `deleted` flag and `created_at` come back through
   `History`, and its `body_md` and `frontmatter` through
   `ReadVersion`. Two further placeholders the plan did not list moved
   with them, both inside `delete_test.go`:
   `TestDeletingADocumentHidesItFromReadsAndKeepsItsHistory`'s read of
   the tombstone's `deleted`, `body_md` and `message`, and
   `TestTheActorOfADeletionIsRecorded`'s read of its `author_user_id`,
   which said in as many words that Task 6 would render it through
   `History`. **After this task no test in the package reads a version's
   stored *values* through SQL.** What remains against
   `document_versions` is three row *counts* (which have no reader and
   are about `current_version` never disagreeing with the number of
   snapshots) and two rows deliberately seeded or forged --
   `TestNoDeletionIsAnnouncedWhenTheTombstoneCannotBeWritten`'s squatter
   and `TestAVersionRowCannotClaimAGameItsDocumentDoesNotBelongTo`'s
   refused insert. `document_versions.project_id`, which Task 3's
   correction 15 recorded as written and asserted by nothing, is now
   read back on both readers. Six mutations prove the converted
   assertions are real: the tombstone storing an empty body, the
   tombstone storing an empty message, the tombstone recording the
   document's creator instead of its remover, every version written with
   `Deleted: true`, the version row losing its author, and the version
   row losing its message.

9. **Smaller things.** `InsertDocumentVersion.Deleted` needed no new
   `false` from `Revert` after all -- `Revert` writes through
   `writeWith`, which already passes it explicitly, so the compile-time
   guard Task 4's correction 13 promised is satisfied one frame down and
   `Revert` never touches the argument. `sub.Events()`/`sub.Close()` in

   Task 3's correction 10 already recorded), and the plan's `errorsAs`

   `HistoryPage.Versions` is `[]dbq.ListDocumentVersionsRow`, whose
   absence of a body field is pinned by reflection over every field
   (`TestAHistoryRowCarriesNoBodyAtAll`) rather than by a claim, because
   what has to hold is that no such field exists. `splitLines`' rule has
   a consequence the plan did not state and the comment now does: a
   change that is *only* a trailing newline cannot be shown by this
   diff, the way git's "\ No newline at end of file" shows it -- the

**Corrections from Task 6's review** (a second pass, over commits
`92e6ac0`, `d74ce47` and `8120349`):

1. **MEDIUM -- a zero-count hunk range named the wrong line.** Unified
   format numbers a zero-length range by the line *before* it (GNU
   `diff -U3` on an empty-to-full pair prints `@@ -0,0 +1,3 @@`, never
   `@@ -1,0`), and `writeHunks` and the coarse fallback both printed
   `startFrom`/`startTo` verbatim, one too high on an empty side. A
   frontmatter-only document stores an empty body, so this was reachable
   through `Diff` on a design-bible stub, and anything positioning by
   the number -- `patch`, a renderer jumping to a hunk -- would have
   landed one line high. Fixed with one `hunkRange(start, count int)
   string` helper, used at both call sites (`diff.go`), that prints
   `start-1` when `count` is 0. `TestAnAddedDocumentsDiffNumbersTheEmpty
   SideByGNURules` and `TestADeletedDocumentsDiffNumbersTheEmptySideByGN
   URules` (`diff_test.go`) pin both directions against the exact GNU
   output; both write an empty body on one side of the diff, which no
   test in the package did before. Proved red against the pre-fix
   formatting (`fmt.Sprintf("%d,%d", start, count)` verbatim): both new
   tests fail, reproducing exactly the `@@ -1,0` / `@@ -1,3 +1,0`
   mis-numbering the finding described.
2. **MEDIUM -- the corrections block fixed two of four places, and
   three claims were disprovable.** `internal/db/queries/documents.sql`'s
   `ListDocumentVersions` and `GetDocumentVersion` comments (and the
   generated `dbq` copy) still said `TestReadingAnotherGamesVersionIsNot
   Found` covered the project filter through `History`/`ReadVersion`
   directly -- false, and contradicting the file header and
   `documentForVersions`, both already corrected by the implementer.
   Both query comments now say what the header and the Go doc comment
   say: no call this package offers can make either query's own project
   filter matter, because the document id only ever arrives already
   resolved inside the caller's own game, and the composite key makes a
   disagreeing row unrepresentable; they point at
   `TestAVersionIsAddressedByItsOwnDocument`,
   `TestTwoGamesSharingOnePathKeepSeparateHistories` and
   `TestAVersionRowCannotClaimAGameItsDocumentDoesNotBelongTo` for what
   is actually pinned. `sqlc generate` re-ran to carry the fix into
   `internal/db/dbq/documents.sql.go`; `sqlc diff` clean afterward.
   Separately, `internal/markdown/versions.go`'s `DefaultHistoryPage`/
   `MaxHistoryPage` comment cited
   `TestAHistoryPageAsksForTooMuchAndGetsTheCap` as pinning the clamp;
   it does not, and cannot with its two-version fixture -- clamping to
   the cap, folding onto the default and applying no bound at all all
   return the same two rows. Rather than growing the fixture (the clamp
   policy is `paging.Size`'s, not this listing's, and duplicating a
   large-fixture version of it here is exactly what the finding warned
   Task 8 would copy verbatim), the comment now points at
   `internal/paging/cursor_test.go`'s
   `TestSizeClampsRatherThanFoldingOntoTheDefault`, which pins the
   policy directly against `paging.Size`, and states what the local test
   actually pins instead (that asking for more than the cap does not
   error or silently truncate below a full page). The test's own comment
   says the same thing, so a reader does not have to cross-reference the
   type's doc comment to learn it.
3. **LOW -- a cited test did not exist, and now there is a standing
   check for the class.** `TestAVersionRowCarriesItsOwnProjectId`,
   cited at `documents.sql:255` and duplicated into `dbq`, named no test
   anywhere in the module; the assertion it described lives in
   `TestTwoGamesSharingOnePathKeepSeparateHistories`, which correction 2
   above now cites instead. `internal/markdown/dangling_test_names_test.go`
   (new) is the standing check: it collects every top-level `func
   TestXxx(...)` the module defines, scans every comment in
   `internal/markdown`'s own files and in
   `internal/db/queries/*.sql` for something shaped like a test name,
   and fails on any that names no test in that set. Two things it had to
   get right to be trustworthy rather than noisy: long identifiers get
   hard-wrapped mid-word across comment lines with no space (this very
   package's comments do it -- `TestDeletingTwiceIsNotFoundRatherThan
   ASecondTombstone` spans two `--` lines in `documents.sql`), so a
   citation ending exactly at its line's end has its continuation pulled
   from the next comment line's leading capitalised run before being
   checked; and one real forward reference exists today
   (`errors.go`'s `TestEveryMarkdownDomainErrorHasAWireCode`, which
   Task 10 will add), carried in a small documented
   `forwardReferencedTestNames` map rather than silently ignored, so the
   day Task 10 lands and forgets to remove the entry, the check starts
   failing for the right reason. **Run against the code as it stood
   before this review's fixes** (`documents.sql` with only correction 2's
   query comments reverted), it found exactly the one dangling name:
   `documents.sql:255: cites TestAVersionRowCarriesItsOwnProjectId,
   which no test in the module defines` -- nothing else, confirming it
   neither over- nor under-reports on the one known case. It passes
   clean against the corrected tree.
4. **LOW -- revert silently resurrects a deleted document, now
   documented and pinned.** Proved: delete at version 4 (tombstone lands
   at 5), revert to version 1 with `ExpectedVersion: 5`, and the
   document comes back live at version 6 with `deleted_at` cleared. That
   follows from `Revert` sharing `writeWith` with `Write` -- neither
   checks `existing.DeletedAt`, and `writeWith`'s own
   `GetDocumentByPathForUpdate` read carries no such filter, resurrection
   on write having been Task 4's deliberate choice -- rather than being a
   decision made in this task, and `Revert`'s doc comment now says so in
   those terms, distinct from the tombstone-*version* paragraph already
   there (which is about the content of the version restored, not
   whether the document itself was deleted). `TestRevertingADeletedDocum
   entResurrectsIt` (`versions_test.go`) pins it. Proved red: an explicit
   `if existing.DeletedAt.Valid { return missingDocument(in.Path) }`
   inserted into `Revert` right after the path-respelling check turns
   the test's "must resurrect" assertion into a `not_found` error,
   confirming the test actually exercises the resurrect path rather than
   passing vacuously. The published `document.reverted` event carries no
   field marking the resurrection, which the comment now states matches
   the existing precedent -- `document.written` carries none for the
   same case on `Write` -- rather than leaving it an unstated gap.
5. **LOW -- the inventory in correction 8 was off, now recorded as
   found rather than silently amended.** Its headline holds: no test in
   the package reads a version's stored values through SQL. The counts
   it gave were wrong -- five count queries (`delete_test.go:49,270`,
   `documents_test.go:244,477,557`), not three, and four seeded or
   forged inserts (`documents_test.go:638,647` are the concurrent-writer
   seed, missing from the original list), not two. Correction 8's text
   is left as the implementer wrote it, per this plan's own rule of not
   rewriting a landed correction; this entry is the fix, the way
   Task 5's review corrected earlier entries by addition rather than by
   edit.
6. **NIT -- the coarse path re-emitted backed-off context as changed
   lines.** With a common head and tail, the coarse fallback printed the
   up-to-three backed-off identical lines as both removed and added:
   arithmetically consistent with a header that counts what it prints,
   cosmetically wrong for a line nobody changed. The coarse branch now
   runs, and is bounds-checked, against the *raw* trim (head and tail
   before the `diffContext` back-off), so it never sees the common lines
   the fine path's `writeHunks` needs the back-off for; the back-off
   itself now runs only on the path that uses it.
   `TestACoarseDiffDoesNotReprintItsCommonHeadAndTailAsChanged`
   (`diff_test.go`) pins it: a 5,000-line rewrite with three identical
   lines on each side, asserting none of `ctx1`..`ctx6` appears under a

   applied before the `lcsLimit` check, as the plan originally had it):

### Task 7: Links between documents and entities

**Files:**
- Modify: `internal/db/queries/documents.sql`
- Create: `internal/markdown/links.go`
- Create: `internal/markdown/links_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/markdown/documents.go` (`WriteInput` gains `Links`)
- Modify: `internal/markdown/events.go`

Three decisions this task carries, each argued where the code makes it:

- **A link's unique key is `(document_id, entity_id)`, and `role` is not
  part of it.** One document has exactly one role on one entity;
  re-adding a link updates the role rather than making a second edge. The
  spec leaves the role vocabulary open (§10.3) and free text is the v1
  answer, but "free text" must not also mean "the same pair can be
  attached twice under two spellings".
- **A `links` array on a write *replaces* the set; omitting it leaves it
  alone.** "Omitted means empty" would silently detach everything on
  every ordinary edit, which is why the distinction is a pointer in Go
  and is stated in the tool description.
- **Attachment is never inferred from content.** Not from frontmatter,
  not from a heading matching an entity name. A wrong attachment is
  invisible: the document simply shows up on the wrong quest and nobody
  notices.

- [ ] **Step 1: Add the queries**

Append to `internal/db/queries/documents.sql`:

```sql
-- name: GetEntityTypeIDByKey :one
-- Link endpoints are resolved here rather than through
-- internal/metamodel's own EntityByKey, in two steps rather than one
-- join, for a reason that is about the *answer* and not about coupling:
-- a caller that typed "quesst" and a caller that typed "wanted-hoggerr"
-- have made two different mistakes at two different arguments, and one
-- flat "not found" makes an agent guess which. Two lookups let the
-- refusal name entity_type or entity_key, which is the discrimination
-- the spec wanted a whole extra wire code for.
SELECT id FROM entity_types
WHERE project_id = sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid
  AND lower(key) = lower(sqlc.arg('key')::text);

-- name: GetEntityIDByKey :one
SELECT id FROM entities
WHERE project_id = sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid
  AND entity_type_id = sqlc.arg('entity_type_id')::uuid
  AND lower(key) = lower(sqlc.arg('key')::text);

-- name: UpsertDocumentLink :one
-- Idempotent by (document, entity): re-adding a link updates its role
-- rather than making a second edge, which is what keeps a re-run seeding
-- script from doubling every attachment.
--
-- project_id is written, not filtered, and 0007_documents.sql's two
-- composite keys are what make a cross-game link impossible by
-- construction: this row's project_id must agree with the document's
-- *and* with the entity's, so a document in one game cannot be attached
-- to an entity in another whatever Go believes.
INSERT INTO document_links (project_id, document_id, entity_id, role)
VALUES (sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid, sqlc.arg('document_id')::uuid,
        sqlc.arg('entity_id')::uuid, sqlc.arg('role')::text)
ON CONFLICT (document_id, entity_id) DO UPDATE
SET role = excluded.role
RETURNING *;

-- name: DeleteDocumentLink :execrows
DELETE FROM document_links
WHERE project_id = sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid
  AND document_id = sqlc.arg('document_id')::uuid
  AND entity_id = sqlc.arg('entity_id')::uuid;

-- name: DeleteDocumentLinksExcept :exec
-- The other half of a replacing write. An empty keep list detaches
-- everything, which is exactly what `links: []` means.
DELETE FROM document_links
WHERE project_id = sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid
  AND document_id = sqlc.arg('document_id')::uuid
  AND NOT (entity_id = ANY(sqlc.arg('keep')::uuid[]));

-- name: ListDocumentLinksByDocument :many
-- Ordered by the entity's type key then its key then its id: none of the
-- first two is unique on its own, and an order that can tie reshuffles
-- its ties between two identical calls.
SELECT l.role, l.created_at, t.key AS entity_type_key, e.key AS entity_key, e.name AS entity_name, e.id AS entity_id
FROM document_links l
JOIN entities e ON e.id = l.entity_id AND e.project_id = l.project_id
JOIN entity_types t ON t.id = e.entity_type_id AND t.project_id = e.project_id
WHERE l.project_id = sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid
  AND l.document_id = sqlc.arg('document_id')::uuid
ORDER BY t.key, e.key, e.id;

-- name: ListDocumentLinksByEntity :many
-- The reverse direction, which is how the UI builds an entity page and
-- how an agent asked to rewrite the Hogger dialogue finds the document
-- from the quest instead of guessing a path.
--
-- Soft-deleted documents are excluded: an entity page listing a document
-- nobody can read would be a dead link on every quest it was attached
-- to.
SELECT l.role, l.created_at, d.id AS document_id, d.path, d.title, d.kind
FROM document_links l
JOIN documents d ON d.id = l.document_id AND d.project_id = l.project_id
WHERE l.project_id = sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid
  AND l.entity_id = sqlc.arg('entity_id')::uuid
  AND d.deleted_at IS NULL
ORDER BY d.path, d.id;
```

Run `make sqlc && make sqlc-check`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Add the event kind**

In `internal/markdown/events.go`:

```go
	// eventDocumentLinked fires from LinkAdd, LinkRemove and any Write
	// that carried a links array, once the transaction has committed,
	// with documentEventMinRole / documentEventHumanOnly.
	//
	// One kind covers attach and detach, deliberately: the payload
	// carries the document's identity and nothing about the link set, so
	// there is no sentence a client could write from "linked" that it
	// could not write from "unlinked", and both have the same recovery —
	// re-read the document's links, or the entity's. Two kinds would be
	// two things to subscribe to for one refresh.
	//
	// **A write that carries a links array publishes both
	// document.written and document.linked**, in that order. A client
	// watching only one of them is watching for one of the two things
	// that changed, and the alternative — folding the link change into
	// the write event — would mean a client had to re-read links on
	// every write in case one had.
	eventDocumentLinked = "document.linked"
```

package markdown_test

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"testing"

	"github.com/google/uuid"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/markdown"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/metamodel"
)

// newQuest declares a `quest` entity type in the game and seeds one
// entity, returning its key. It goes through the metamodel service
// rather than a raw INSERT because the entity must be a real row with a
// real search vector — a fixture written by hand bypasses the
// application-computed column and a later search test would silently
// find nothing.
func newQuest(t *testing.T, entities *metamodel.Service, game uuid.UUID, key, name string) {
	t.Helper()
	ctx := context.Background()
	if _, err := entities.UpsertEntityType(ctx, game, metamodel.EntityTypeInput{
		Key: "quest", Label: "Quest", LabelPlural: "Quests",
	}); err != nil && !errors.Is(err, metamodel.ErrVersionConflict) {
		t.Fatalf("declare quest type: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := entities.UpsertEntity(ctx, game, metamodel.EntityInput{
		TypeKey: "quest", Key: key, Name: name,
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("seed quest %s: %v", key, err)
	}
}

func TestALinkAttachesADocumentToAnEntityAndReadsBackFromBothSides(t *testing.T) {
	svc, entities, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	newQuest(t, entities, game, "wanted-hogger", "Wanted: Hogger")

	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "scripts/wanted-hogger", Content: "HOGGER: *snarls*\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}
	if err := svc.LinkAdd(ctx, game, markdown.LinkInput{
		Path: "scripts/wanted-hogger", EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "wanted-hogger",
		Role: "script",
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("LinkAdd: %v", err)
	}

	// From the document.
	fromDoc, err := svc.LinksByDocument(ctx, game, "scripts/wanted-hogger")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("LinksByDocument: %v", err)
	}
	if len(fromDoc) != 1 || fromDoc[0].EntityKey != "wanted-hogger" || fromDoc[0].Role != "script" {
		t.Fatalf("links from the document = %+v, want one script link to wanted-hogger", fromDoc)
	}
	if fromDoc[0].EntityName != "Wanted: Hogger" {
		t.Fatalf("EntityName = %q, want the entity's name so a UI need not fetch it",
			fromDoc[0].EntityName)
	}

	// From the entity. This is the read-back that matters most: it is
	// how the UI builds a quest page and how an agent finds the script
	// from the quest instead of guessing a path.
	fromEntity, err := svc.LinksByEntity(ctx, game, "quest", "wanted-hogger")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("LinksByEntity: %v", err)
	}
	if len(fromEntity) != 1 || fromEntity[0].Path != "scripts/wanted-hogger" {
		t.Fatalf("links from the entity = %+v, want the script", fromEntity)
	}
	if fromEntity[0].Title != "scripts/wanted-hogger" || fromEntity[0].Role != "script" {
		t.Fatalf("entity-side link = %+v, want the document's title and the role", fromEntity[0])
	}
}

func TestReAddingALinkUpdatesItsRoleRatherThanDuplicatingIt(t *testing.T) {
	svc, entities, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	newQuest(t, entities, game, "wanted-hogger", "Wanted: Hogger")
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "s", Content: "x\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}

	for _, role := range []string{"script", "dialogue"} {
		if err := svc.LinkAdd(ctx, game, markdown.LinkInput{
			Path: "s", EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "wanted-hogger", Role: role,
		}); err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("LinkAdd %s: %v", role, err)
		}
	}
	links, err := svc.LinksByDocument(ctx, game, "s")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("LinksByDocument: %v", err)
	}
	if len(links) != 1 || links[0].Role != "dialogue" {
		t.Fatalf("links = %+v, want exactly one, with the latest role", links)
	}
}

func TestALinkToAnEntityInAnotherGameIsRefused(t *testing.T) {
	svc, entities, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	azeroth := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	outland := newGame(t, pool, "outland")
	newQuest(t, entities, outland, "wanted-hogger", "Wanted: Hogger")

	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, azeroth, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "s", Content: "x\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}
	err := svc.LinkAdd(ctx, azeroth, markdown.LinkInput{
		Path: "s", EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "wanted-hogger",
	})
	var missing *markdown.MissingError
	if !errors.As(err, &missing) || missing.Path != "entity_type" {
		t.Fatalf("want a MissingError at entity_type — azeroth has no quest type at all — got %#v", err)
	}
}

func TestAMistypedEntityTypeAndAMistypedEntityKeyAreToldApart(t *testing.T) {
	svc, entities, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	newQuest(t, entities, game, "wanted-hogger", "Wanted: Hogger")
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "s", Content: "x\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}

	var missing *markdown.MissingError

	err := svc.LinkAdd(ctx, game, markdown.LinkInput{
		Path: "s", EntityType: "quesst", EntityKey: "wanted-hogger",
	})
	if !errors.As(err, &missing) || missing.Path != "entity_type" {
		t.Fatalf("want a MissingError at entity_type, got %#v", err)
	}

	err = svc.LinkAdd(ctx, game, markdown.LinkInput{
		Path: "s", EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "wanted-hoggerr",
	})
	if !errors.As(err, &missing) || missing.Path != "entity_key" {
		t.Fatalf("want a MissingError at entity_key, got %#v", err)
	}

	// Both are not_found on the wire — the discrimination is the path,
	// which is data, not prose an agent has to parse. This is why the
	// spec's proposed `entity_not_found` code does not ship.
	if !errors.Is(err, markdown.ErrNotFound) {
		t.Fatalf("want not_found, got %v", err)
	}
}

func TestRemovingALinkLeavesTheDocumentAndTheEntity(t *testing.T) {
	svc, entities, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	newQuest(t, entities, game, "wanted-hogger", "Wanted: Hogger")
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "s", Content: "x\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}
	if err := svc.LinkAdd(ctx, game, markdown.LinkInput{
		Path: "s", EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "wanted-hogger",
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("LinkAdd: %v", err)
	}
	if err := svc.LinkRemove(ctx, game, markdown.LinkInput{
		Path: "s", EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "wanted-hogger",
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("LinkRemove: %v", err)
	}
	links, err := svc.LinksByDocument(ctx, game, "s")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("LinksByDocument: %v", err)
	}
	if len(links) != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("links = %+v, want none", links)
	}
	if _, err := svc.Read(ctx, game, "s"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("the document must survive: %v", err)
	}

	// Removing a link that is not there is not_found, not silence: an
	// agent that removed the wrong one needs to be told.
	err = svc.LinkRemove(ctx, game, markdown.LinkInput{
		Path: "s", EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "wanted-hogger",
	})
	if !errors.Is(err, markdown.ErrNotFound) {
		t.Fatalf("want not_found removing a link that is not there, got %v", err)
	}
}

func TestDeletingAnEntityDropsItsLinksAndLeavesTheDocument(t *testing.T) {
	svc, entities, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	newQuest(t, entities, game, "wanted-hogger", "Wanted: Hogger")
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "s", Content: "x\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}
	if err := svc.LinkAdd(ctx, game, markdown.LinkInput{
		Path: "s", EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "wanted-hogger",
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("LinkAdd: %v", err)
	}

	entity, err := entities.EntityByKey(ctx, game, "quest", "wanted-hogger")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("EntityByKey: %v", err)
	}
	if err := entities.RemoveEntity(ctx, game, entity.ID); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("RemoveEntity: %v", err)
	}

	// A quest is cut; its lore survives for the next quest.
	if _, err := svc.Read(ctx, game, "s"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("the document must survive the entity: %v", err)
	}
	links, err := svc.LinksByDocument(ctx, game, "s")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("LinksByDocument: %v", err)
	}
	if len(links) != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("links = %+v, want the cascade to have dropped them", links)
	}
}

func TestALinksArrayOnAWriteReplacesTheSetAndOmittingItPreservesIt(t *testing.T) {
	svc, entities, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	newQuest(t, entities, game, "wanted-hogger", "Wanted: Hogger")
	newQuest(t, entities, game, "the-defias", "The Defias Brotherhood")

	// Created with one link, in one call.
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "s", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
		Links: &[]markdown.LinkTarget{{EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "wanted-hogger", Role: "script"}},
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write with links: %v", err)
	}
	if links, _ := svc.LinksByDocument(ctx, game, "s"); len(links) != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("links = %+v, want one", links)
	}

	// An ordinary edit, no links array: the set is untouched. This is
	// the case that would silently detach everything if "omitted" meant
	// "empty".
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "s", Content: "two\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(1),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("edit: %v", err)
	}
	if links, _ := svc.LinksByDocument(ctx, game, "s"); len(links) != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("links = %+v after an edit with no links array, want the set preserved", links)
	}

	// A links array replaces.
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "s", Content: "three\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(2),
		Links: &[]markdown.LinkTarget{{EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "the-defias", Role: "lore"}},
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write replacing links: %v", err)
	}
	links, _ := svc.LinksByDocument(ctx, game, "s")
	if len(links) != 1 || links[0].EntityKey != "the-defias" {
		t.Fatalf("links = %+v, want only the-defias", links)
	}

	// An empty array detaches everything, which is the one way to say so.
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "s", Content: "four\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(3),
		Links: &[]markdown.LinkTarget{},
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write detaching links: %v", err)
	}
	if links, _ := svc.LinksByDocument(ctx, game, "s"); len(links) != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("links = %+v, want none", links)
	}
}

func TestABadLinkInAWriteRollsTheWholeWriteBack(t *testing.T) {
	svc, entities, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	newQuest(t, entities, game, "wanted-hogger", "Wanted: Hogger")
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "s", Content: "one\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}

	_, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "s", Content: "two\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(1),
		Links: &[]markdown.LinkTarget{
			{EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "wanted-hogger"},
			{EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "does-not-exist"},
		},
	})
	var missing *markdown.MissingError
	if !errors.As(err, &missing) || missing.Path != "links[1].entity_key" {
		t.Fatalf("want a MissingError naming the failing element, got %#v", err)
	}
	// The body did not move either: a write and its links are one change.
	doc, err := svc.Read(ctx, game, "s")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Read: %v", err)
	}
	if doc.BodyMd != "one\n" || doc.CurrentVersion != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("document = (%q, v%d), want the write rolled back", doc.BodyMd, doc.CurrentVersion)
	}
}

func TestALinkRoleIsBoundedAsTheCallersOwnArgument(t *testing.T) {
	svc, entities, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	newQuest(t, entities, game, "wanted-hogger", "Wanted: Hogger")
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{

	}
	err := svc.LinkAdd(ctx, game, markdown.LinkInput{
		Path: "s", EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "wanted-hogger",
		Role: strings.Repeat("r", markdown.MaxRoleLen+1),
	})
	requireFieldError(t, err, "role", "must be at most")
}

func TestLinkingIsAnnounced(t *testing.T) {
	svc, entities, hub, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	newQuest(t, entities, game, "wanted-hogger", "Wanted: Hogger")
	if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
		Path: "s", Content: "x\n", ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}

	sub := hub.Subscribe(game, "viewer", false)
	defer sub.Close()

	if err := svc.LinkAdd(ctx, game, markdown.LinkInput{
		Path: "s", EntityType: "quest", EntityKey: "wanted-hogger",
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("LinkAdd: %v", err)
	}
	ev := <-sub.Events()
	if ev.Kind != "document.linked" {
		t.Fatalf("Kind = %q, want %q", ev.Kind, "document.linked")
	}
}

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the links**

`internal/markdown/links.go`:

```go
package markdown

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"fmt"

	"github.com/google/uuid"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/db/dbq"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/metamodel"
)

// MaxRoleLen bounds a link's free-text role ("script", "lore",
// "backstory"). Maestro attaches no meaning to it and ships no
// vocabulary: the same rule that forbids a built-in Quest type forbids a
// built-in Script role. Whether a project should be able to *declare*
// its roles the way it declares relation types is open (spec §10.3) and
// is not settled here; what is settled is that the link's key is
// (document, entity), so free text cannot produce two attachments of one
// pair under two spellings.
const MaxRoleLen = 64

// MaxLinksPerWrite bounds the links array a single write may carry.
//
// A document about a hundred entities is not a document, it is a
// taxonomy that should be entities and relations. The bound is here so
// that a caller that has confused the two is told, at its own argument,
// rather than discovering it as a slow write.
const MaxLinksPerWrite = 100

// LinkTarget is one end of an attachment: the entity, and the role the
// document plays for it.
type LinkTarget struct {
	EntityType string
	EntityKey  string
	Role       string
}

// LinkInput is one attachment operation, from the document's side.
type LinkInput struct {
	Path       string
	EntityType string
	EntityKey  string
	Role       string
}

// EntityLink is one attachment as seen from the document: which entity,
// and in what role.
//
// EntityName is carried so a UI listing a document's attachments does
// not have to fetch each entity to render a label. It is a *display*
// value on a read, not an event payload, so the "carry identity only"
// rule does not apply: a read is answered from the same transaction's
// snapshot the caller asked for, and there is no ordering hazard.
type EntityLink struct {
	EntityID      uuid.UUID
	EntityTypeKey string
	EntityKey     string
	EntityName    string
	Role          string
}

// DocumentLink is one attachment as seen from the entity: which
// document, and in what role. It carries no body — a listing never does.
type DocumentLink struct {
	DocumentID uuid.UUID
	Path       string
	Title      string
	Kind       string
	Role       string
}

// LinkAdd attaches a document to an entity, or updates the role of an
// attachment that is already there.
//
// It does not take an expected_version and does not move the document's
// own version, deliberately. A link is not the document's content: two
// people attaching one script to two different quests are not editing
// the same thing, and making them serialise on the document's version
// would turn an independent operation into a conflict. What that costs
// is that a link change is not in the version history, which is the
// right trade — the history is the history of the *writing*.
func (s *Service) LinkAdd(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, in LinkInput) error {
	problems := pathProblems(in.Path)
	problems = append(problems, checkShortText("role", in.Role, MaxRoleLen)...)
	if len(problems) > 0 {
		return invalidInputProblems(problems)
	}

	var doc dbq.Document
	err := s.withTx(ctx, func(q *dbq.Queries) error {
		var err error
		doc, err = s.documentForLinks(ctx, q, projectID, in.Path)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		entityID, err := s.resolveEntity(ctx, q, projectID, "", LinkTarget{
			EntityType: in.EntityType, EntityKey: in.EntityKey,
		})
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		_, err = q.UpsertDocumentLink(ctx, dbq.UpsertDocumentLinkParams{
			ProjectID: projectID, DocumentID: doc.ID, EntityID: entityID, Role: in.Role,
		})
		if err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("attach document: %w", err)
		}
		return nil
	})
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	s.publish(projectID, eventDocumentLinked, documentEventMinRole, documentEventHumanOnly,
		DocumentEvent{ID: doc.ID, Path: doc.Path, Version: doc.CurrentVersion})
	return nil
}

// LinkRemove detaches a document from an entity.
//
// Removing an attachment that is not there is not_found rather than
// silence: an agent that detached the wrong pair, or that detached the
// right pair twice, has made a mistake it can act on, and "it worked"
// tells it nothing.
func (s *Service) LinkRemove(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, in LinkInput) error {
	if err := CheckPath(in.Path); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	var doc dbq.Document
	err := s.withTx(ctx, func(q *dbq.Queries) error {
		var err error
		doc, err = s.documentForLinks(ctx, q, projectID, in.Path)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		entityID, err := s.resolveEntity(ctx, q, projectID, "", LinkTarget{
			EntityType: in.EntityType, EntityKey: in.EntityKey,
		})
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		rows, err := q.DeleteDocumentLink(ctx, dbq.DeleteDocumentLinkParams{
			ProjectID: projectID, DocumentID: doc.ID, EntityID: entityID,
		})
		if err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("detach document: %w", err)
		}
		if rows == 0 {
			return &MissingError{
				Path: "entity_key",
				Message: fmt.Sprintf("the document at %q is not attached to the %s %q",
					in.Path, in.EntityType, in.EntityKey),
			}
		}
		return nil
	})
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	s.publish(projectID, eventDocumentLinked, documentEventMinRole, documentEventHumanOnly,
		DocumentEvent{ID: doc.ID, Path: doc.Path, Version: doc.CurrentVersion})
	return nil
}

// LinksByDocument lists everything one document is attached to.
func (s *Service) LinksByDocument(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, path string) ([]EntityLink, error) {
	doc, err := s.documentForLinks(ctx, s.q, projectID, path)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	rows, err := s.q.ListDocumentLinksByDocument(ctx, dbq.ListDocumentLinksByDocumentParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, DocumentID: doc.ID,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("list links by document: %w", err)
	}
	// Never nil: an empty slice marshals as [], and "this document is
	// attached to nothing" must not reach a client as null.
	links := make([]EntityLink, 0, len(rows))
	for _, row := range rows {
		links = append(links, EntityLink{
			EntityID: row.EntityID, EntityTypeKey: row.EntityTypeKey,
			EntityKey: row.EntityKey, EntityName: row.EntityName, Role: row.Role,
		})
	}
	return links, nil
}

// LinksByEntity lists every document attached to one entity. This is how
// the UI builds an entity page, and how an agent asked to rewrite the
// Hogger dialogue finds the document from the quest.
func (s *Service) LinksByEntity(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, entityType, entityKey string) ([]DocumentLink, error) {
	entityID, err := s.resolveEntity(ctx, s.q, projectID, "", LinkTarget{
		EntityType: entityType, EntityKey: entityKey,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	rows, err := s.q.ListDocumentLinksByEntity(ctx, dbq.ListDocumentLinksByEntityParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, EntityID: entityID,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("list links by entity: %w", err)
	}
	links := make([]DocumentLink, 0, len(rows))
	for _, row := range rows {
		links = append(links, DocumentLink{
			DocumentID: row.DocumentID, Path: row.Path,
			Title: row.Title, Kind: row.Kind, Role: row.Role,
		})
	}
	return links, nil
}

// documentForLinks resolves a path to the document links hang from. A
// soft-deleted document is not found: attaching prose nobody can read
// would put a dead entry on an entity page.
func (s *Service) documentForLinks(ctx context.Context, q *dbq.Queries, projectID uuid.UUID, path string) (dbq.Document, error) {
	row, err := q.GetDocumentByPath(ctx, dbq.GetDocumentByPathParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, Path: path, IncludeDeleted: false,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return dbq.Document{}, notFound(err, missingDocument(path), "resolve document")
	}
	return row, nil
}

// resolveEntity turns (entity type key, entity key) into an entity id,
// naming *which* of the two was not found.
//
// prefix is "" for the single-link tools and "links[i]." for an element
// of a write's array, so a caller sending twenty links is told which one
// is wrong — the rule metamodel.parseIDs applies to a list of ids.
//
// **This is what the spec's proposed `entity_not_found` code was for**,
// and it is why that code does not ship: the discrimination an agent
// needs is *which argument*, and a path is that, as data. See
// MissingError's own doc comment.
func (s *Service) resolveEntity(ctx context.Context, q *dbq.Queries, projectID uuid.UUID,
	prefix string, target LinkTarget,
) (uuid.UUID, error) {
	typeID, err := q.GetEntityTypeIDByKey(ctx, dbq.GetEntityTypeIDByKeyParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, Key: target.EntityType,
	})
	if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) {
		return uuid.Nil, &MissingError{
			Path:    prefix + "entity_type",
			Message: fmt.Sprintf("this game has no entity type %q", target.EntityType),
		}
	}
	if err != nil {
		return uuid.Nil, fmt.Errorf("resolve entity type: %w", err)
	}
	entityID, err := q.GetEntityIDByKey(ctx, dbq.GetEntityIDByKeyParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, EntityTypeID: typeID, Key: target.EntityKey,
	})
	if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) {
		return uuid.Nil, &MissingError{
			Path: prefix + "entity_key",
			Message: fmt.Sprintf("this game has no %s named %q",
				target.EntityType, target.EntityKey),
		}
	}
	if err != nil {
		return uuid.Nil, fmt.Errorf("resolve entity: %w", err)
	}
	return entityID, nil
}

// replaceLinks is the links array a write carries. It runs inside the
// write's own transaction, so a bad link rolls the body back with it: a
// document and what it is about are one change, and half of it landing
// would attach a script to a quest it no longer describes.
func (s *Service) replaceLinks(ctx context.Context, q *dbq.Queries, projectID uuid.UUID,
	documentID uuid.UUID, targets []LinkTarget,
) error {
	keep := make([]uuid.UUID, 0, len(targets))
	for i, target := range targets {
		prefix := fmt.Sprintf("links[%d].", i)
		if problems := checkShortText(prefix+"role", target.Role, MaxRoleLen); len(problems) > 0 {
			return invalidInputProblems(problems)
		}
		entityID, err := s.resolveEntity(ctx, q, projectID, prefix, target)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if _, err := q.UpsertDocumentLink(ctx, dbq.UpsertDocumentLinkParams{
			ProjectID: projectID, DocumentID: documentID, EntityID: entityID, Role: target.Role,
		}); err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("attach document: %w", err)
		}
		keep = append(keep, entityID)
	}
	// pgx encodes a nil slice as SQL NULL, and `NOT (x = ANY(NULL))` is
	// NULL, which deletes nothing — so an empty links array would
	// silently preserve the set it was meant to clear. The empty slice
	// above is allocated with make for exactly that reason; this
	// assertion is what keeps a later "optimisation" from reintroducing
	// it.
	if keep == nil {
		keep = []uuid.UUID{}
	}
	return q.DeleteDocumentLinksExcept(ctx, dbq.DeleteDocumentLinksExceptParams{
		ProjectID: projectID, DocumentID: documentID, Keep: keep,
	})
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Wire the links array into `Write`**

In `internal/markdown/documents.go`, add the field to `WriteInput`:

```go
	// Links replaces the document's whole attachment set when it is
	// non-nil, and leaves it untouched when it is nil.
	//
	// **The pointer is the distinction and it is load-bearing.** A plain
	// slice cannot tell "attach nothing" from "do not touch the links",
	// and reading an omitted array as empty would silently detach every
	// attachment on every ordinary edit — the single most destructive
	// thing this tool could do quietly. `Links: &[]LinkTarget{}` is the
	// one way to say "detach everything", and it has to be said.
	Links *[]LinkTarget
```

**Corrections from implementing Task 7** (what the plan's code blocks got
wrong, and what the landed code does instead):

1. **A comment claiming a test forced a filter that no test can force --
   caught in the same commit it was written, which is the class the
   plan's header names first.** `GetEntityIDByKey`'s comment as first
   written said
   `TestALinkToAnEntityFromAnotherGamesTypeOfTheSameNameIsRefusedAtTheKey`
   "forces this filter to matter, with both games declaring `quest`".
   It does not: neutralising the filter behind a no-op `OR TRUE` leaves
   the package green, because `entity_type_id` arrives already resolved
   inside the caller's own game and `0004_metamodel.sql` gives
   `entities` a composite key to `entity_types` carrying `project_id`,
   so an entity whose project disagrees with its type's is
   unrepresentable. The comment now states that plainly -- the shape
   `ListDocumentVersions` already uses since Task 6's correction 1 --
   says the filter stays for a caller that does not resolve first (Task
   11's REST mirror may be one), and names what the test *does* pin: the
   refusal lands at `entity_key` rather than at `entity_type` or on
   somebody else's row. `GetEntityTypeIDByKey`'s own project filter is
   the one that is load-bearing, and dropping it reddens
   `TestALinkToAnEntityInAnotherGameIsRefused`.
2. **The plan's two predicted findings, both confirmed, one closed.**
   Dropping `ListDocumentLinksByDocument`'s project filter reddens
   nothing, for the same structural reason as item 1 -- the document id
   only ever arrives resolved through `documentForLinks` -- and no
   two-game listing assertion can change that, so the query's comment
   records it rather than a test being written that would pass either
   way. What is pinned instead is the `document_id` filter, by
   `TestALinkIsAddressedByItsOwnDocument`, whose fixture is two
   documents *inside one game*, where the project filter separates
   nothing; dropping either that filter or
   `ListDocumentLinksByEntity`'s `entity_id` reddens it. The second
   prediction was real and is closed:
   `TestAnEntityStopsListingADocumentThatWasDeleted` reddens when
   `d.deleted_at IS NULL` goes, and its second act pins the other half
   of the same rule -- nothing cascades on a soft delete, so the link
   row survives and comes back with its role when the document is
   written to again.
3. **Caller input reached Postgres unbounded, and its refusal would have
   been an `internal_error`.** Neither the plan's `LinkAdd` nor its
   `resolveEntity` checked `entity_type` or `entity_key` at all: an
   entity key holding an invalid UTF-8 byte reaches the server as a byte
   sequence it refuses outright (SQLSTATE 22021), which lands on the
   default arm as `internal_error` over a value the caller supplied --
   against this sub-project's standing rule that nothing a caller can
   fix reports one. Closed by `entityAddressProblems`, which bounds both
   keys through **`metamodel.RowKeyProblems`** -- `rowKeyProblems` under
   an exported name, added in `internal/metamodel/keys.go` for this --
   rather than through a second copy of the same three rules here: the
   rule that decides which spellings can exist must be the one that
   decides which spellings can be asked for. `TestAnEntityAddressIsBoundedBeforePostgresSeesIt`
   pins it at the single-link path and inside a write's array, and both
   mutations are red (the bounds removed; the element prefix dropped in
   `replaceLinks`). Consequences: `LinkRemove` reports every argument in
   one pass like `Write` and `Delete` rather than checking only the path
   as the plan had it, and `LinksByDocument` and `LinksByEntity` -- which
   the plan's blocks left with no validation whatsoever -- check theirs
   too.
4. **The `keep == nil` guard cannot fire as the function stands, and the
   comment now says so.** The hazard it describes is real and was
   measured, not assumed: with `keep` declared as a nil slice, pgx sends
   SQL NULL, `NOT (x = ANY(NULL))` is NULL, and the empty-array case of
   `TestALinksArrayOnAWriteReplacesTheSetAndOmittingItPreservesIt` fails
   with the previous attachment still there. But `make([]uuid.UUID, 0,
   n)` never returns nil, so the branch is unreachable today. It is kept
   for the one edit it catches -- a later `var keep []uuid.UUID`, which
   reads as an equivalent "optimisation" and is not one -- and stated as
   unreachable rather than left to look load-bearing, which is Task 3's
   correction 3 applied before the fact instead of after. **Recorded
   because the first attempt at this mutation was invalid**: replacing
   the `make` alone left the guard in place, the test stayed green, and
   a mutation that hits one of two redundant defences proves nothing
   either way (Task 4's discarded twentieth attempt, one task along).
5. **The publish-placement gap, found for the fourth time.**
   `TestNoLinkIsAnnouncedWhenTheAttachmentIsRefused` stays green with
   the publish moved inside `withTx`, exactly as Task 3's correction 4
   found for `Write`, Task 4's for `Delete` and Task 6's for `Revert`:
   every failure `LinkAdd` can produce before its commit is a refusal,
   and a refusal returns before the publish wherever the call sits.
   `TestNoLinkIsAnnouncedWhenTheAttachmentCannotCommit` closes it,
   reusing `TestNoDeletionIsAnnouncedWhenTheDeleteCannotCommit`'s
   deferred `NOT VALID` foreign key unchanged but for the table
   (`document_links`, `LinkAdd`'s only INSERT), and it is the only one
   of the two that goes red against the moved publish.
6. **The omitted-versus-empty distinction is pinned on the wire here,
   not left to Task 10.** Task 3's review had to chase `kind`'s identical
   rule from the service onto the wire after the fact, because
   `omitempty` on a plain value cannot tell "absent" from "zero"; the
   same is true of a plain slice, which `omitempty` omits when it is
   empty, collapsing "detach everything" back into "say nothing".
   `TestOmittingLinksAndSendingAnEmptyArrayAreDifferentOnTheWire`
   decodes both JSON shapes into a struct carrying the exact tag Task 10
   must use -- `Links *[]DocsLinkInput` tagged
   `json:"links,omitempty"` -- and
   encodes both back out, so the distinction is asserted in both
   directions in the domain that defines the meaning. Proved red by
   making the field a plain slice. `WriteInput.Links`' doc comment names
   the test and names the Task 10 field that must keep the shape.
7. **Two behaviours the plan stated in prose and pinned with nothing.**
   The listing order (`t.key, e.key, e.id`) had no fixture that could
   see it -- one link cannot show an order -- so
   `TestADocumentIsAttachedToSeveralEntitiesAndListedInAStableOrder`
   attaches one document to three entities across two types, which is
   also the case the whole many-to-many table was chosen for; it reddens
   against `ORDER BY l.created_at`. And "an empty slice, never nil" is
   asserted through `encoding/json` rather than by checking non-nilness,
   in `TestADocumentWithNoAttachmentsIsAnOrdinaryDocument` -- the other
   end of the same decision, a game bible attached to nothing.
8. **Smaller things.** The plan's event test uses `sub.Events()` and
   `sub.Close()`, which still do not exist (`sub.C` and
   `hub.Unsubscribe`, as Task 3's correction 10 recorded and Task 6
   repeated); a local `nextEvent` helper takes a 5-second timeout so a
   mutation that starves the channel reports instead of hanging the
   suite. The plan's negative tests assert `errors.As` inline where
   `requireMissing` already exists, and the landed tests assert the full
   message of every refusal through it. `Revert`'s doc comment now
   carries the plan's "revert does not carry links and must not grow
   one" sentence, where a reader of `Revert` will find it rather than in
   a plan file. And the standing dangling-test-name check earned its
   keep on its first outing after Task 6 added it: a test renamed while
   the SQL comment citing it was not caught immediately, by name and
   line.

**Review response on Task 7** (closing the seven findings raised against
the pass above; nothing here reopens items 1--6 of the corrections list,
which stand):

1. **The wire-shape test protects a decoy.** `wireWrite` in
   `TestOmittingLinksAndSendingAnEmptyArrayAreDifferentOnTheWire` cannot
   pin a type Task 10 has not written yet, so the test's own comment now
   says plainly that it pins a stand-in, and that Task 10's review must
   re-pin the claim against the real `DocsWriteInput.Links`. That
   obligation is stated three times on purpose, so it cannot be missed
   by reading only one of the three: in the test's comment, on
   `WriteInput.Links`' own doc comment (already named the exact field
   shape, `*[]DocsLinkInput` tagged `json:"links,omitempty"` — line 9073
   above already carries it), and here, as a requirement rather than a
   note for Task 10 to pick up. **Task 10 must not merge until its
   review confirms `DocsWriteInput.Links` is declared exactly as this
   comment states, and that a test in `internal/web` pins it the way
   this one pins the stand-in.**
2. **`"links": null` is decided, not just observed.** It preserves the
   set, the same as an omitted field, and not the same as `[]`, which
   detaches everything — the safe side, since Go's own zero value for a
   nil `*[]T` already collapses "omitted" and "null" into one value with
   no choice made anywhere. The decision is now stated on
   `WriteInput.Links`, and
   `TestOmittingLinksAndSendingAnEmptyArrayAreDifferentOnTheWire` decodes
   `"links":null` alongside the omitted and empty cases and asserts it
   lands on the preserving side. **Task 10's tool description for
   `docs.write` must say this in words an agent reads before it
   guesses** — an agent that sends `null` meaning "detach" gets silence,
   not an error, which is exactly the failure mode `omitempty` on a
   plain slice would have produced for the field as a whole.
3. **`LinkRemove` no longer accepts a `role` it ignores.** `LinkInput`
   (kept for `LinkAdd`, which still needs `Role`) is joined by a new
   `UnlinkInput` — `Path`, `EntityType`, `EntityKey`, nothing else —
   which `LinkRemove` now takes. A struct that cannot carry the field
   cannot be misread as selecting among links by it. **Task 10 must
   address the same decision on the wire**: `docs.links.remove`'s input
   type must not carry a `role` field, unlike `docs.links.add`'s
   `DocsLinkInput`, which keeps it — the two tools' inputs are not one
   shared shape, whatever Step 1's draft above assumed.
4. **Duplicate targets inside one links array are refused, not
   collapsed.** `duplicateLinkTargets` checks a write's whole array
   before anything is written, folding case the way the entity key's own
   unique index does, and reports the second occurrence at
   `links[i].entity_key` naming the first. The call was the metamodel's
   own: its bulk writes refuse a batch that names one row twice
   (`repeatedIdentities`, `internal/metamodel/bulk.go`), on the argument
   that a batch naming a row twice cannot be satisfied as submitted —
   the identical argument applies here, over targets in one array rather
   than rows in one batch, and the two link-key columns are exactly the
   metamodel's identity for this join. `TestALinksArrayNamingOneEntityTwiceIsRefused`
   pins it at two spellings of one key
   (`wanted-hogger` / `WANTED-HOGGER`), and asserts the write lands
   nothing, not even the unambiguous first element. Proved red: removing
   the `duplicateLinkTargets` call from `replaceLinks` fails the new
   test with "want a refusal ... got nil".
5. **The standing dangling-test-name check now scans
   `internal/metamodel` as well.** `internal/metamodel/keys.go:145`'s
   citation of `TestAnEntityAddressIsBoundedBeforePostgresSeesIt` was
   real but uncovered, because
   `TestNoCommentNamesATestThatDoesNotExist` only swept
   `internal/markdown` and `internal/db/queries` — Task 7 is what made
   this domain cite into `internal/metamodel` in the first place, so the
   check's coverage had drifted from where its own citations live.
   Widening it immediately found two genuine dangling citations that
   predate this task and were invisible until now:
   `internal/metamodel/relations_test.go:452` cited
   `TestARelationTypeCreatedDuringATypeRemovalCannot…`, truncated
   mid-identifier, and the same file's line 539 cited
   `TestIsRetryableNamesTheFour…`, truncated the same way — both fixed
   to name the real tests in full
   (`TestARelationTypeCreatedDuringATypeRemovalCannotKeepTheRemovedID`,
   `TestIsRetryableNamesTheFourContentionStatesAndNothingElse`). A third
   hit was a false positive the regex cannot avoid on its own:
   `internal/metamodel/search_test.go` narrates that the current test
   "used to be `TestSearchRanksTheStrongerMatchFirst`" — history, not a
   claim that the old name is present coverage — so the prose was reworded
   to drop the literal old name rather than taught to a checker that
   cannot parse tense. The widened check was confirmed working before
   any of the three were fixed — it reported all three by name and
   line — and is green now that they are.
6. **Smaller things, closed.** Both listing queries dropped
   `l.created_at` from their `SELECT` list; it was mapped into neither
   `EntityLink` nor `DocumentLink` and nothing orders by it (the order is
   `t.key, e.key, e.id` and `d.path, d.id`), so it was dead weight on
   every row, re-generated through `sqlc`. And `links_test.go`'s stray
   `"` where a backtick was meant — the same defect class commit
   `fb9e5df` fixed in this plan file and left in the source, one step
   along — is gone; the sentence was reworded rather than re-escaped, so
   there is no adjacent double-backtick left for a future pass to
   mis-render either.
7. **Recorded for Task 11, not acted on now**: `document.linked`'s
   payload names only the document (`DocumentEvent`: id, path, version).
   An entity-page subscriber watching a quest cannot tell from the event
   alone whether *its own* entity was the one that moved, and must
   re-read on every `document.linked` in the game to find out. Task 11's
   SSE work should weigh whether the payload needs the entity's identity
   too, or whether the existing "re-read to find out" recovery (already
   the story for every other event this domain publishes) is accepted
   here as well.

### Task 8: Listing documents

**Files:**
- Modify: `internal/db/queries/documents.sql`
- Create: `internal/markdown/list.go`
- Create: `internal/markdown/list_test.go`

**Summaries only, never bodies.** A body is returned by `Read`,
`ReadVersion` and `Diff` and by nothing else (spec §7). Prose is the
largest payload in the system and agents are its main consumer; a
listing that carried bodies would blow a context window on the first
call against a real game.

- [ ] **Step 1: Add the query**

Append to `internal/db/queries/documents.sql`:

```sql
-- name: ListDocumentsPage :many
-- One page of a game's documents, keyset-ordered by (path, id).
--
-- **No body_md and no frontmatter in the select list.** They are the two
-- largest columns in the schema and a listing never needs either; a
-- SELECT * here would quietly put a megabyte of prose per row on a page
-- of fifty.
--
-- **starts_with, not LIKE, and this is a correctness choice rather than
-- a style one.** A path may legally contain `_`, which is LIKE's
-- single-character wildcard, so `path_prefix: "lore_x"` under LIKE would
-- also match `loreax` — a filter silently answering a question the
-- caller did not ask, which is the class of defect this whole read
-- surface is most exposed to. Escaping it correctly is possible and is
-- one forgotten backslash away from the same bug. The cost is that
-- starts_with cannot use documents_listing_idx for the prefix, so a
-- prefixed listing scans the game's documents; a game has hundreds of
-- documents, not millions, and the ordering half of the index still
-- applies.
--
-- The prefix is folded, like every other path comparison in this file,
-- so `Lore/` and `lore/` name the same subtree.
--
-- The entity filter is an EXISTS rather than a join, so a document
-- attached to one entity appears once and the page's row count is the
-- number of documents rather than the number of links.
SELECT d.id, d.project_id, d.path, d.kind, d.title, d.summary, d.current_version,
       d.deleted_at, d.created_at, d.updated_at,
       d.created_by_user_id, d.created_by_token_id,
       d.updated_by_user_id, d.updated_by_token_id
FROM documents d
WHERE d.project_id = sqlc.arg('project_id')::uuid
  AND (sqlc.arg('include_deleted')::bool OR d.deleted_at IS NULL)
  AND (sqlc.narg('path_prefix')::text IS NULL
       OR starts_with(lower(d.path), lower(sqlc.narg('path_prefix')::text)))
  AND (sqlc.narg('kind')::text IS NULL OR lower(d.kind) = lower(sqlc.narg('kind')::text))
  AND (sqlc.narg('entity_id')::uuid IS NULL
       OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM document_links l
                   WHERE l.project_id = d.project_id
                     AND l.document_id = d.id
                     AND l.entity_id = sqlc.narg('entity_id')::uuid))
  AND (sqlc.narg('after_id')::uuid IS NULL
       OR (d.path, d.id) > (sqlc.narg('after_path')::text, sqlc.narg('after_id')::uuid))
ORDER BY d.path, d.id
LIMIT sqlc.arg('limit')::int;
```

Run `make sqlc && make sqlc-check`.

package markdown_test

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"
	"testing"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/markdown"
)

// seedDocuments writes n documents under a prefix, so a listing has
// enough rows to page. Sixty, when the default page is fifty and the cap
// is two hundred: fewer than the default makes clamping and folding
// indistinguishable.
func seedDocuments(t *testing.T, svc *markdown.Service, game uuid.UUID, prefix string, n int) {
	t.Helper()
	ctx := context.Background()
	for i := 0; i < n; i++ {
		if _, err := svc.Write(ctx, game, markdown.WriteInput{
			Path:            fmt.Sprintf("%s/doc-%03d", prefix, i),
			Content:         fmt.Sprintf("body %d\n", i),
			Kind:            "lore",
			ExpectedVersion: ptrInt32(0),
		}); err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("seed %s/doc-%03d: %v", prefix, i, err)
		}
	}
}

func TestAListingIsSummariesInPathOrderWithNoBodies(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	seedDocuments(t, svc, game, "lore", 3)

	page, err := svc.List(ctx, game, markdown.ListFilter{})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("List: %v", err)
	}
	if len(page.Documents) != 3 {
		t.Fatalf("%d documents, want 3", len(page.Documents))
	}
	if page.Documents[0].Path != "lore/doc-000" {
		t.Fatalf("first path = %q, want path order", page.Documents[0].Path)
	}
	if page.Documents[0].Summary != "body 0" {
		t.Fatalf("Summary = %q, want the derived summary", page.Documents[0].Summary)
	}
	// The type the listing returns has no body field at all, which is
	// how "never a body" is a compile-time fact rather than a promise.
	// This assertion is the reminder; if it stops compiling because
	// someone added one, that is the finding.
	var _ = markdown.DocumentSummary{
		ID: page.Documents[0].ID, Path: "", Kind: "", Title: "", Summary: "",
		CurrentVersion: 0, Deleted: false,
	}
}

func TestAListingPagesAndItsCursorBelongsToItsFilter(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	seedDocuments(t, svc, game, "lore", 60)
	seedDocuments(t, svc, game, "scripts", 5)

	first, err := svc.List(ctx, game, markdown.ListFilter{Limit: 25})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("List: %v", err)
	}
	if len(first.Documents) != 25 || first.NextCursor == "" {
		t.Fatalf("first page = %d docs, cursor %q", len(first.Documents), first.NextCursor)
	}
	second, err := svc.List(ctx, game, markdown.ListFilter{Limit: 25, Cursor: first.NextCursor})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("second page: %v", err)
	}
	if second.Documents[0].Path != "lore/doc-025" {
		t.Fatalf("second page starts at %q, want lore/doc-025", second.Documents[0].Path)
	}

	// The same cursor against a different filter is refused.
	_, err = svc.List(ctx, game, markdown.ListFilter{
		Limit: 25, Cursor: first.NextCursor, PathPrefix: "lore/",
	})
	requireFieldError(t, err, "cursor", "different listing")
}

	seedDocuments(t, svc, azeroth, "lore", 3)

	page, err := svc.List(ctx, outland, markdown.ListFilter{})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("List: %v", err)
	}
	if len(page.Documents) != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("outland sees %d of azeroth's documents, want none", len(page.Documents))

func TestTheLimitIsClampedRatherThanFoldedOntoTheDefault(t *testing.T) {
	svc, _, _, pool := newService(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	game := newGame(t, pool, "azeroth")
	seedDocuments(t, svc, game, "lore", 60)

	atCap, err := svc.List(ctx, game, markdown.ListFilter{Limit: markdown.MaxDocumentPage})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("List: %v", err)
	}
	overCap, err := svc.List(ctx, game, markdown.ListFilter{Limit: markdown.MaxDocumentPage + 1})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("List: %v", err)
	}
	if len(atCap.Documents) != len(overCap.Documents) {
		t.Fatalf("asking for one more than the cap returned %d rather than %d: "+
			"a limit over the cap is clamped, never folded onto the default",
			len(overCap.Documents), len(atCap.Documents))
	}
	none, err := svc.List(ctx, game, markdown.ListFilter{Limit: 0})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("List: %v", err)
	}
	if len(none.Documents) != int(markdown.DefaultDocumentPage) {
		t.Fatalf("%d documents with no limit, want the default %d",
			len(none.Documents), markdown.DefaultDocumentPage)
	}
}

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the listing**

`internal/markdown/list.go`:

```go
package markdown

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"

	"github.com/google/uuid"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/db/dbq"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/metamodel"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/paging"
)

// The bounds on one page of documents.
const (
	defaultDocumentPage int32 = 50
	maxDocumentPage     int32 = 200

	// DefaultDocumentPage and MaxDocumentPage bound one page of List.
	// They are exported so the tool description is built from them
	// rather than typing the numbers into prose, where they would go on
	// promising a cap that moved.
	DefaultDocumentPage = defaultDocumentPage
	MaxDocumentPage     = maxDocumentPage
)

// ListFilter narrows a document listing.
//
// EntityType and EntityKey are given together or not at all: half of an
// address is not a filter, and reading one half as "no filter" would
// answer a different question with a full page.
type ListFilter struct {
	PathPrefix     string
	Kind           string
	EntityType     string
	EntityKey      string
	IncludeDeleted bool
	Cursor         string
	Limit          int32
}

// DocumentSummary is one row of a listing.
//
// **It has no body and no frontmatter field**, which is what makes
// "a listing never returns a body" a fact about the type rather than a
// promise about the query. A body is returned by Read, ReadVersion and
// Diff and by nothing else.
type DocumentSummary struct {
	ID             uuid.UUID
	Path           string
	Kind           string
	Title          string
	Summary        string
	CurrentVersion int32
	Deleted        bool
}

// DocumentPage is one page of summaries plus the cursor for the next.
//
// **NextCursor is set when the page came back full**, and empty
// otherwise. That is one call more than strictly necessary on a listing
// whose length is an exact multiple of the limit — the last full page
// carries a cursor to an empty one — and the alternative, reading
// limit+1 rows and dropping the extra, costs a row on every page of
// every listing to save that one call. A caller looping until
// NextCursor is empty is correct and must expect an empty final page
// rather than treating one as an error.
//
// The rest of the cursor contract is paging.Cursor's: a position and not
// a snapshot, refused against any other listing, unsigned and not a
// capability. **A document path is mutable in principle and immutable in
// practice** — UpsertDocument never writes the path column, so the only
// way a row's sort key moves is a caller creating a document at a path
// that sorts earlier, which a paged walk will simply not see. That is
// the ordinary "a row inserted before the position is never seen"
// behaviour, not a special case.
type DocumentPage struct {
	Documents  []DocumentSummary
	NextCursor string
}

// List returns one page of a game's documents.
//
// **An unknown key in a filter is a not_found naming the key**, not an
// empty page. A caller that mistyped `quesst` hears about `quesst`
// instead of being told this game has no documents and going off to
// seed a second copy of them.
func (s *Service) List(ctx context.Context, projectID uuid.UUID, f ListFilter) (DocumentPage, error) {
	problems := checkPathPrefix(f.PathPrefix)
	problems = append(problems, checkShortText("kind", f.Kind, MaxKindLen)...)
	switch {
	case f.EntityType != "" && f.EntityKey == "":
		problems = append(problems, metamodel.FieldError{
			Path:    "entity_key",
			Message: "and entity_type must be given together: half an address is not a filter",
		})
	case f.EntityKey != "" && f.EntityType == "":
		problems = append(problems, metamodel.FieldError{
			Path:    "entity_type",
			Message: "and entity_key must be given together: half an address is not a filter",
		})
	}
	if len(problems) > 0 {
		return DocumentPage{}, invalidInputProblems(problems)
	}

	params := dbq.ListDocumentsPageParams{
		ProjectID:      projectID,
		IncludeDeleted: f.IncludeDeleted,
		Limit:          paging.Size(f.Limit, defaultDocumentPage, maxDocumentPage),
	}
	entityPart := ""
	if f.EntityType != "" {
		entityID, err := s.resolveEntity(ctx, s.q, projectID, "", LinkTarget{
			EntityType: f.EntityType, EntityKey: f.EntityKey,
		})
		if err != nil {
			return DocumentPage{}, err
		}
		params.EntityID = &entityID
		entityPart = entityID.String()
	}
	if f.PathPrefix != "" {
		prefix := f.PathPrefix
		params.PathPrefix = &prefix
	}
	if f.Kind != "" {
		kind := f.Kind
		params.Kind = &kind
	}

	// The project id is the first part, always. The entity filter is
	// spelled as the *resolved* id rather than as the caller's keys, so
	// two spellings of one key give one fingerprint; the prefix and the
	// kind are folded for the same reason, since the query folds them
	// too.
	fingerprint := paging.Fingerprint(projectID.String(), "documents",
		lowerOf(f.PathPrefix), lowerOf(f.Kind), entityPart,
		fmt.Sprintf("%t", f.IncludeDeleted))
	after, err := paging.Decode(f.Cursor, fingerprint, refuseCursor)
	if err != nil {
		return DocumentPage{}, err
	}
	if after.ID != uuid.Nil {
		params.AfterID = &after.ID
		params.AfterPath = &after.Sort
	}

	rows, err := s.q.ListDocumentsPage(ctx, params)
	if err != nil {
		return DocumentPage{}, fmt.Errorf("list documents: %w", err)
	}

	page := DocumentPage{Documents: make([]DocumentSummary, 0, len(rows))}
	for _, row := range rows {
		page.Documents = append(page.Documents, DocumentSummary{
			ID: row.ID, Path: row.Path, Kind: row.Kind, Title: row.Title,
			Summary: row.Summary, CurrentVersion: row.CurrentVersion,
			Deleted: row.DeletedAt != nil,
		})
	}
	// paging.Size never returns a limit below one, so a full page is
	// never an empty one and there is no separate emptiness check.
	if len(rows) == int(params.Limit) {
		last := rows[len(rows)-1]
		page.NextCursor = paging.Encode(paging.Cursor{
			Sort: last.Path, ID: last.ID, Fingerprint: fingerprint,
		})
	}
	return page, nil
}

// checkPathPrefix bounds a prefix filter.
//
// It is deliberately looser than CheckPath: "lore/" is a legitimate
// prefix and CheckPath refuses a trailing slash, so running the full
// grammar here would refuse the most obvious thing a caller would type.
// What it keeps is the part that protects the database — the length
// bound, valid UTF-8, no control characters — and the part that protects
// the caller from a silent wrong answer is in the query, where
// starts_with rather than LIKE means `_` is not a wildcard.
func checkPathPrefix(prefix string) []metamodel.FieldError {
	if prefix == "" {
		return nil
	}
	return checkShortText("path_prefix", prefix, MaxPathLen)
}

// lowerOf folds a filter part for the fingerprint, so two spellings of
// one filter give one fingerprint — which is what the query's own
// lower() comparisons already mean.
func lowerOf(s string) string { return strings.ToLower(s) }
```
